Policy`n288`nIS-CIRQ-P-025-G: Employee Termination Process`n292`n `nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy (Master Policy)`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-001-G`nStandards Name: Information Security Policy (Master Policy)`nCategory: Base Policy &amp; ISMS Manual`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 7/1/2025 `nReview Date:`n 7/1/2026 `nApproved By:`n `n[CEO/President, or specific Management Committee] (As the IT department maintains, but usually top management `napproves`n the master policy)`n1. Introduction`nCirque Corporation is an `ninnovative manufacturing`n and engineering`n company`n committed to protecting its information assets to ensure business continuity, minimize business damage, and maximize return on investments and business opportunities. Information, in all its forms, is a critical asset for Cirque Corporation's manufacturing operations, intellectual property, customer relations, and regulatory compliance.`nThis Information Security Policy establishes the overarching principles and commitment of `nCirque Corporation`n's management to information security, forming the foundation for the Information Security Management System (ISMS) in accordance with the requirements of `nISO/IEC 27001:2022. By upholding this policy, Cirque Corporation aims to enable innovative manufacturing, ensure product quality, and maintain customer trust.`n2. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to:`nState `nCirque Corporation`n's commitment to information security.`nDefine the objectives of information security within `nCirque Corporation`n.`nProvide a framework for establishing and achieving information security objectives.`nEnsure compliance with relevant legal, regulatory, and contractual obligations.`nProtect information assets from all threats, whether internal or external, deliberate or accidental.`n3. Scope`nThis policy applies to all information, information processing facilities, information systems, networks, applications, services, and personnel within `nCirque Corporation`n, including employees, contractors, and third parties who access `nCirque Corporation`n's information assets.`nThe scope of the ISMS is further defined in the IS-CIRQ-D-001-G: ISMS Scope Document.`n4. Information Security Objectives`nCirque Corporation`n is committed to:`nMaintaining the confidentiality, integrity, and availability of all information assets.`nProtecting intellectual property, including manufacturing designs, processes, and customer data.`nEnsuring the security of operational technology (OT) systems used in manufacturing processes.`nComplying with all applicable legal, regulatory, and contractual requirements related to information security, including data protection laws in regions where `nCirque Corporation`n operates (e.g., US, Japan, China).`nImplementing a robust risk management framework to identify, assess, and treat information security risks.`nPromoting information security awareness and competence among all personnel.`nEnsuring business continuity and rapid recovery from information security incidents, aiming for `nzero critical data breaches.`nEnhancing `nsupply chain security for critical components`n related to our manufacturing processes.`nContinuously improving the effectiveness of the ISMS.`n5. Management Commitment`nTop management at `nCirque Corporation`n is fully committed to the ISMS and will:`nApprove and support this Information Security Policy.`nProvide adequate resources for the establishment, implementation, maintenance, and continual improvement of the ISMS.`nCommunicate the importance of information security and compliance with the ISMS requirements throughout the organization.`nEnsure that information security objectives are established and reviewed regularly.`nConduct regular management reviews of the ISMS to ensure its continuing suitability, adequacy, and effectiveness.`n6. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if significant changes occur to Cirque Corporation's business, legal, or technical environment. This policy is `ncontrolled and maintained by the IT Department`n, with ultimate approval by `n[CEO/President, or specific Management Committee]`n.`nNote on "Approved By":`n While the IT Department controls and maintains the policy, a `nMaster Information Security Policy`n for ISO 27001 is typically approved by top management (e.g., CEO, President, or a governing board/committee) to demonstrate commitment and provide authority. I've updated the placeholder to reflect this common practice. You can adjust this to the specific top management individual or committee responsible for formal approval.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-001-G: ISMS Scope Definition Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-001-G`nStandards Name: ISMS Scope Definition Procedure`nCategory: Base Policy &amp; ISMS Manual`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.1 `nEffective Date:`n `n7/1/2025`n `nReview Date:`n `n7/1/2026`n `nApproved By:`n `n[IT Department Head / Information Security Manager]`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the process for establishing and maintaining the scope of `nCirque Corporation`n's Information Security Management System (ISMS) in accordance with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause 4.3. A clearly defined scope ensures that all relevant information assets, processes, and locations are included, and that the ISMS effectively supports `nCirque Corporation`n's business objectives.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all activities involved in defining, documenting, and reviewing the ISMS scope, including relevant internal and external issues, interested parties' requirements, and interfaces with `nCirque Corporation`n's manufacturing and business functions.`n3. Responsibilities`nExecutive Committee:`n Provides ultimate approval for the ISMS scope.`nIT Department / Information Security Manager (ISM):`n Responsible for leading the scope definition process, documenting the scope, and ensuring its review and communication. Also responsible for maintaining the ISMS Scope Document.`nDepartment Heads/Process Owners:`n Provide input regarding their respective areas, information assets, and processes that fall within or interface with the ISMS scope.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Identify Internal and External Issues (ISO 27001 Clause 4.1)`n a. The IT Department / ISM will identify and analyze internal and external issues relevant to `nCirque Corporation`n's purpose and its ability to achieve the intended outcomes of the ISMS. This includes: * `nInternal Issues:`n Organizational culture, capabilities (e.g., resources, knowledge), governance structure, information technology infrastructure, manufacturing processes, operational technology (OT) systems (even if not currently in scope, their potential future inclusion is considered), and proprietary information like designs and customer data. * `nExternal Issues:`n Legal and regulatory requirements (e.g., data privacy laws like CCPA, APPI, PIPL, state data breach notification laws), technological advancements, competitive landscape, market conditions, and societal expectations. b. The analysis of these issues will inform the boundaries and applicability of the ISMS.`n4.2. Identify Interested Parties and Their Requirements (ISO 27001 Clause 4.2)`n a. The IT Department / ISM will identify internal and external interested parties relevant to the ISMS. This includes, but is not limited to: * `nInternal:`n Employees, management, the Executive Committee, internal audit. * `nExternal:`n Customers (including those in Japan, Taiwan, and China), suppliers, regulators, investors, partners, local communities, law enforcement, and auditors. b. The IT Department / ISM will determine the information security requirements of these interested parties that are relevant to the ISMS. This may involve reviewing contracts, regulations, and industry best practices. c. Document identified interested parties and their requirements in `nIS-CIRQ-D-002-G: Interested Parties and Their Requirements Register`n.`n4.3. Determine ISMS Boundaries and Applicability (ISO 27001 Clause 4.3)`n a. Based on the analysis from sections 4.1 and 4.2, the IT Department / ISM, in consultation with relevant department heads, will define the physical and logical boundaries of the ISMS. This includes: * `nOrganizational Units:`n Which departments, teams, or business functions are included (e.g., specific manufacturing functions, R&amp;D, Sales, IT, Finance, HR). * `nPhysical Locations:`n Which buildings, sites, or geographic regions are covered. Initially, this includes: * Main Headquarters and Production Facility, Sandy, Utah, USA. * Sales and Operations Office, Taipei, Taiwan. * Business operations and relationships with entities in Japan and China. * `nInformation Assets:`n Which types of information (e.g., customer data, intellectual property, financial data, employee data, manufacturing designs, raw material specifications) and associated IT assets (e.g., servers, workstations, network devices, business applications, cloud services) are within scope. * `nTechnologies:`n Which IT systems, networks, and applications are included. While no specific OT assets are currently integrated into the ISMS scope, the ISMS is designed to be extensible to include them should they become information-security-critical. * `nProcesses:`n Which business and manufacturing processes generating, processing, or storing information are covered by the ISMS (e.g., customer order processing, product design, financial reporting, employee onboarding, IT support). b. Any exclusions from the scope must be justified and documented, ensuring they do not compromise the confidentiality, integrity, or availability `nof information critical to `nCirque Corporation`n's core business functions. c. The ISMS scope must be clearly documented in the `nIS-CIRQ-D-001-G: ISMS Scope Document`n.`n4.4. ISMS Scope Review and Approval`n a. The proposed ISMS scope documented in `nIS-CIRQ-D-001-G`n will be reviewed by the IT Department / ISM and relevant stakeholders. b. The ISMS scope must be formally approved by the `nExecutive Committee`n. c. Any changes to the ISMS scope must follow this procedure and be formally re-approved by the Executive Committee.`n5. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-D-001-G: ISMS Scope Document`nIS-CIRQ-D-002-G: Interested Parties and Their Requirements Register`nIS-CIRQ-D-003-G: Legal, Regulatory, and Contractual Requirements Register`nIS-CIRQ-D-001-G: ISMS Scope Document`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-D-001-G`nStandards Name: ISMS Scope Document`nCategory: Base Policy &amp; ISMS Manual`nDivision: Document`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.1 `nEffective Date:`n `n7/1/2025`n `nReview Date:`n `n7/1/2026`n `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Introduction`nThis document defines the scope of `nCirque Corporation`n's Information Security Management System (ISMS) in accordance with ISO/IEC 27001:2022. It details the organizational units, physical locations, information assets, technologies, and processes that are included within the ISMS, along with any justified exclusions.`n2. Context of the Organization`nThis ISMS scope has been determined considering:`nInternal Issues:`n `nCirque Corporation`n's organizational structure, strategic direction, existing IT infrastructure, manufacturing processes, critical intellectual property assets (e.g., designs, formulas, production methods, `nHardware Engineering designs, Firmware Engineering code, Software Engineering code, and ASIC development specifications`n), customer data, and current operational technology (OT) environment.`nExternal Issues:`n Applicable laws and regulations (e.g., data privacy regulations like CCPA, APPI, PIPL, Cybersecurity Law), industry standards, contractual obligations, technological landscape, and market demands.`n3. Interested Parties and Their Requirements`nThe requirements of relevant interested parties, as documented in IS-CIRQ-D-002-G: Interested Parties and Their Requirements Register, have been considered in defining this ISMS scope. These include, but are not limited to, customers in the US, Japan, Taiwan, and China, regulatory bodies, and supply chain partners.`n4. ISMS Boundaries and Applicability`nThe ISMS at `nCirque Corporation`n covers the information security aspects of the following:`n4.1. Organizational Units / Functions:`nAll departments and functions directly involved in or supporting the processing, storage, or transmission of `nCirque Corporation`n's information assets. This includes, but is not limited to:`nExecutive Management`nInformation Technology (IT) Department`nSales &amp; Marketing`nHardware Engineering`nFirmware Engineering`nSoftware Engineering`nASIC Development`nManufacturing Operations (for information related to production planning, quality control, inventory, and intellectual property)`nFinance &amp; Accounting`nHuman Resources (HR)`nCustomer Service`nResearch &amp; Development (R&amp;D) - `nThis is now more specifically detailed by the engineering functions above.`n4.2. Physical Locations:`nMain Headquarters and Production Facility:`n [Full Address, e.g., 123 Manufacturing Way, Sandy, Utah, USA]`nIncludes:`n Office spaces, server rooms, manufacturing floors (information systems and data on these floors).`nSales and Operations Office:`n [Full Address, e.g., 456 Business Lane, Taipei, Taiwan]`nIncludes:`n Office spaces, local IT infrastructure.`nRemote Work Locations:`n Authorized home offices and temporary remote work sites for `nCirque Corporation`n employees, where they access and process `nCirque Corporation`n's information assets.`n4.3. Information Assets:`nAll information, in both physical and digital forms, owned by or in the custody of `nCirque Corporation`n. This includes, but is not limited to:`nCustomer Data (e.g., contact information, order history, payment details)`nEmployee Data (e.g., personal details, payroll information, HR records)`nFinancial Data (e.g., accounting records, budgets, invoices)`nIntellectual Property (e.g., product designs, manufacturing processes, R&amp;D data, patents, trade secrets, `nCAD files, schematics, source code for firmware and software, ASIC design specifications, test data, and intellectual property related to innovative manufacturing techniques`n)`nProprietary Business Information (e.g., strategic plans, marketing materials, sales data)`nOperational Data (e.g., production schedules, quality control data, inventory records, supply chain information, `nmachine calibration data, production efficiency metrics`n)`nSystem Configuration and Log Data`n4.4. Technologies and Systems:`nAll information technology infrastructure, including:`nServers (physical and virtual)`nNetwork devices (routers, switches, firewalls, wireless access points)`nWorkstations, laptops, mobile devices`nBusiness applications (e.g., ERP, CRM, HRIS, CAD/CAM systems, `ndesign software, simulation tools, code repositories`n)`nEmail systems and collaboration platforms`nCloud services utilized by `nCirque Corporation`n (e.g., `nCloud-based ERP system, CRM SaaS, cloud storage for engineering files`n)`nData storage systems (on-premise and cloud)`nNote:`n While no specific Operational Technology (OT) assets are currently identified as requiring direct inclusion within the ISO 27001 ISMS scope, information systems that interact with or manage data from OT environments (e.g., production planning systems, quality control databases) are included. The ISMS is structured to allow for the future integration of specific OT controls as deemed necessary by risk assessment.`n4.5. Processes:`nAll business processes that involve the creation, processing, storage, transmission, or disposal of `nCirque Corporation`n's information assets. This includes, but is not limited to:`nProduct Design and Development (including `nHardware, Firmware, Software, and ASIC design lifecycles`n)`nManufacturing Planning and Execution`nSales and Order Fulfillment`nCustomer Relationship Management`nHuman Resources Management`nFinancial Operations`nInformation Technology Management and Support`nSupplier Management (`nincluding sharing design specifications and production schedules`n)`nBusiness Continuity and Disaster Recovery`n5. Exclusions from Scope`nAt this time, no specific organizational units, physical locations, or major information asset categories are formally excluded from the ISMS scope. All entities and assets that support `nCirque Corporation`n's core business and involve sensitive information are intended to be covered.`nSpecific low-risk, non-information-processing equipment (e.g., non-networked office appliances) are implicitly outside the direct ISMS control, though general physical security applies.`n6. Review and Approval`nThis ISMS Scope Document will be reviewed annually or when significant changes occur to `nCirque Corporation`n's operations, business objectives, or legal/regulatory environment, and formally approved by the Executive Committee.`nIS-CIRQ-D-002-G: Interested Parties and Their Requirements Register`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-D-002-G`nStandards Name: Interested Parties and Their Requirements Register`nCategory: Base Policy &amp; ISMS Manual`nDivision: Document`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.1 `nEffective Date:`n `n7/1/2025`n `nReview Date:`n `n7/1/2026`n `nApproved By:`n IT Department / Information Security Manager`n1. Purpose`nThis register identifies the internal and external interested parties relevant to `nCirque Corporation`n's Information Security Management System (ISMS) and documents their information security requirements. Understanding these requirements is crucial for defining the ISMS scope, establishing objectives, and ensuring the ISMS effectively meets stakeholder expectations in accordance with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause 4.2.`n2. Scope`nThis document applies to all identified interested parties whose requirements or expectations may impact, or be impacted by, `nCirque Corporation`n's information security posture.`n3. Review and Update`nThis register will be reviewed at least annually, or when significant changes occur to `nCirque Corporation`n's business, legal environment, or stakeholder landscape. Updates will be approved by the IT Department / Information Security Manager.`nIS-CIRQ-D-003-G: Legal, Regulatory, and Contractual Requirements Register (Global Core)`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-D-003-G`nStandards Name: Legal, Regulatory, and Contractual Requirements Register (Global Core)`nCategory: Information Management Regulations`nDivision: Document`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n `n7/1/2025`n `nReview Date:`n `n7/1/2026`n `nApproved By:`n IT Department / Information Security Manager`n1. Purpose`nThis register identifies and documents universally applicable legal, regulatory, and contractual requirements related to information security for `nCirque Corporation`n's global operations. Compliance with these requirements is a fundamental aspect of `nCirque Corporation`n's Information Security Management System (ISMS) in accordance with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause 4.2. Specific regional requirements are detailed in localized registers (e.g., IS-CIRQ-D-003-US, IS-CIRQ-D-003-`nASIA`n).`n2. Scope`nThis document applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n's global operations, processes, and information assets that are subject to international legal and ethical norms concerning information security.`n3. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to define the roles, responsibilities, and authorities for information security management within `nCirque Corporation`n, in accordance with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause 5.3. Clearly assigning these responsibilities ensures accountability and effective operation of the Information Security Management System (ISMS).`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all personnel within `nCirque Corporation`n, including employees, contractors, and relevant external parties, insofar as their roles relate to information security and the operation of the ISMS.`n3. Information Security Roles and Responsibilities`nInformation security is a shared responsibility across `nCirque Corporation`n. While the IT Department maintains the ISMS, overall security relies on the active participation of all personnel. Specific roles with defined responsibilities are established as follows:`n3.1. Executive Committee / Top Management`nResponsibility:`n Ultimate accountability for the ISMS and information security performance.`nAuthority:`nApprove the Information Security Policy and ISMS Scope.`nEnsure the availability of necessary resources for the ISMS.`nCommunicate the importance of effective information security management.`nConduct management reviews of the ISMS.`nEnsure information security objectives are consistent with the strategic direction of `nCirque Corporation`n.`n3.2. IT Manager (also acts as Information Security Manager)`nResponsibility:`n Overall leadership and management of the ISMS; overseeing its establishment, implementation, maintenance, and continual improvement. `nAdditionally, serves as the primary "Information Asset Manager," responsible for the overall risk posture and security requirements of `nCirque Corporation`n's information assets.`nAuthority:`nDevelop, implement, and maintain ISMS policies and procedures.`nIdentify and assess information security risks and recommend treatment options.`nManage information security incidents.`nCoordinate information security awareness and training programs.`nReport on the performance of the ISMS to the Executive Committee.`nAct as the primary point of contact for ISMS-related matters (e.g., audits).`nEnsure compliance with legal, regulatory, and contractual information security requirements.`nAuthorize access to information assets based on documented procedures.`n3.3. IT Department Personnel`nResponsibility:`n Implementing and maintaining technical security controls, managing IT infrastructure, and providing technical support for information security.`nAuthority:`nImplement and manage network security devices (firewalls, IDS/IPS).`nManage user access rights and authentication systems.`nPerform system hardening and vulnerability management.`nConduct data backups and ensure recovery capabilities.`nRespond to technical security incidents under the direction of the IT Manager.`n3.4. Department Managers (e.g., Engineering, Sales, Operations)`nResponsibility:`n Ensuring information security within their respective departments and processes; identifying and protecting information assets under their custody.`nAuthority:`nDecide on and approve the selection and use of software tools and systems within their respective departments.`nSupport the IT Manager in identifying and assessing information security risks relevant to their operations.`nEnsure their teams comply with ISMS policies and procedures.`nReport information security incidents or weaknesses promptly.`nProvide input on the classification and handling of information assets unique to their department (e.g., CAD drawings in Hardware Engineering, sales data in Sales).`n3.5. All Employees and Contractors`nResponsibility:`n Adhering to `nCirque Corporation`n's Information Security Policy and all related policies and procedures.`nAuthority:`nProtecting information assets they handle from unauthorized access, use, modification, or disclosure.`nReporting any actual, suspected, or potential information security incidents or weaknesses.`nParticipating in information security awareness training.`nMaintaining confidentiality of `nCirque Corporation`n's information and customer data.`n4. Related Document`nIS-CIRQ-D-004-G: Information Security Roles Matrix`n5. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if significant changes occur to `nCirque Corporation`n's organizational structure or ISMS responsibilities.`nIS-CIRQ-D-004-G: Information Security Roles Matrix`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-D-004-G`nStandards Name: Information Security Roles Matrix`nCategory: Base Policy &amp; ISMS Manual`nDivision: Document`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n `n7/1/2025`n `nReview Date:`n `n7/1/2026`n `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. It serves as a quick reference complementing the IS-CIRQ-P-002-G: Roles, Responsibilities, and Authorities Policy.`n2. Scope`nThis matrix summarizes information security responsibilities across `nCirque Corporation`n for all personnel.`n3. Roles and Responsibilities Matrix`nThis matrix uses the following legend for responsibilities:`nA (Accountable):`n The individual or role ultimately answerable for the correct and thorough completion of the deliverable or task. Updates will be approved by the IT Manager.`nIS-CIRQ-P-003-G: Risk Management Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-003-G`nStandards Name: Risk Management Policy`nCategory: Base Policy &amp; ISMS Manual`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.1 `nEffective Date:`n `n7/1/2025`n `nReview Date:`n `n7/1/2026`n `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish `nCirque Corporation`n's commitment and methodology for identifying, assessing, and treating information security risks in alignment with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause 6.1. This systematic approach ensures that information security risks are managed to an acceptable level, supporting `nCirque Corporation`n's business objectives and compliance obligations.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all information security risk management activities undertaken within `nCirque Corporation`n's Information Security Management System (ISMS) scope, as defined in IS-CIRQ-D-001-G: ISMS Scope Document. It covers all information assets, processes, technologies, and personnel relevant to information security across all `nCirque Corporation`n locations and operations.`n3. Principles of Risk Management`nCirque Corporation`n's approach to information security risk management is based on the following principles:`nSystematic and Consistent:`n Risks will be identified, analyzed, evaluated, and treated using a consistent and repeatable methodology, employing `nLow, Medium, and High`n classifications.`nProactive:`n Information security risks will be identified and addressed proactively, rather than reactively, where possible.`nRisk-Based Decision Making:`n Decisions regarding information security controls and investments will be based on a clear understanding of identified risks and their potential impact.`nContinual Improvement:`n The risk management process will be regularly reviewed and improved.`nTransparency:`n Risk management activities and decisions will be documented and communicated to relevant stakeholders.`nTop Management Support:`n Top management is committed to providing adequate resources for risk management.`n4. Risk Management Framework`nCirque Corporation`n adopts a structured approach to risk management, encompassing the following phases:`n4.1. Risk Identification`n a. Identify all information assets within the ISMS scope, considering their value to `nCirque Corporation`n (e.g., intellectual property, customer data, financial data, manufacturing designs, source code). b. Identify potential threats to these assets (e.g., cyber-attacks, human error, natural disasters, hardware failure, unauthorized access). c. Identify existing vulnerabilities that could be exploited by threats. d. Identify existing controls and their effectiveness.`n4.2. Risk Analysis`n a. Assess the likelihood of a threat exploiting a vulnerability to cause an information security incident using `nLow, Medium, or High`n qualitative ratings. b. Assess the potential impact (e.g., financial, reputational, legal, operational) of such an incident on `nCirque Corporation`n's business objectives using `nLow, Medium, or High`n qualitative ratings. c. Combine likelihood and impact to determine the level of inherent risk (e.g., High, Medium, Low).`n4.3. Risk Evaluation`n a. Compare the assessed risk levels against `nCirque Corporation`n's established risk acceptance criteria. b. Prioritize risks based on their severity and alignment with risk acceptance levels.`n4.4. Risk Treatment`n a. Select appropriate risk treatment options based on the risk evaluation: * `nModify:`n Implement controls to reduce the risk (e.g., encryption, access controls, training). * `nRetain:`n Accept the risk as it falls within acceptable levels or treatment is not cost-effective. * `nAvoid:`n Cease the activity that generates the risk. * `nShare/Transfer:`n Transfer the risk to another party (e.g., insurance, outsourcing with robust contracts). b. Develop and implement a Risk Treatment Plan (IS-CIRQ-D-005-G) detailing the selected controls, responsibilities, and timelines. c. Implement controls from ISO 27001 Annex A as appropriate.`n4.5. Risk Monitoring and Review`n a. Identified risks and the effectiveness of implemented controls will be regularly monitored. b. `nA formal review of high risks will be conducted quarterly.`n c. The entire risk assessment and treatment processes will be reviewed periodically to ensure their ongoing suitability and effectiveness. d. Risks will be re-evaluated when significant changes occur (e.g., new systems, new business processes, new threats).`n5. Risk Acceptance Criteria`nThe Executive Committee will define and approve the criteria for accepting information security risks. `nCirque Corporation`n maintains a `nlow risk appetite`n, aiming to mitigate almost all identified risks, even those initially assessed as minor, where feasible and cost-effective. Risks exceeding the defined acceptance criteria must be treated.`n6. Roles and Responsibilities`nExecutive Committee:`n Approves the Risk Management Policy and Risk Acceptance Criteria.`nIT Manager:`n Accountable for the overall risk management process, including facilitating risk assessments, maintaining the Risk Assessment Register, and reporting on risk status.`nDepartment Managers/Process Owners:`n Responsible for identifying and assessing risks within their operational areas and contributing to risk treatment plans.`nAll Employees:`n Responsible for reporting identified vulnerabilities or threats.`n7. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-PR-002-G: Information Security Risk Assessment Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-003-G: Information Security Risk Treatment Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register`nIS-CIRQ-D-005-G: Risk Treatment Plan (RTP)`nIS-CIRQ-D-006-G: Statement of Applicability (SoA)`n8. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if significant changes occur to `nCirque Corporation`n's risk appetite, business environment, or legal/regulatory landscape.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-002-G: Information Security Risk Assessment Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-002-G`nStandards Name: Information Security Risk Assessment Procedure`nCategory: Base Policy &amp; ISMS Manual`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.1 `nEffective Date:`n `n7/1/2025`n `nReview Date:`n `n7/1/2026`n `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to describe the systematic process for identifying, analyzing, and evaluating information security risks within `nCirque Corporation`n's Information Security Management System (ISMS) scope, in accordance with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause 6.1. This procedure implements the principles outlined in the IS-CIRQ-P-003-G: Risk Management Policy.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all information assets, processes, and systems within the defined ISMS scope of `nCirque Corporation`n. It covers both initial and periodic risk assessments, as well as ad-hoc assessments triggered by significant changes.`n3. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall responsible for coordinating and facilitating risk assessment activities, maintaining the Risk Assessment Register (IS-CIRQ-F-001-G), and ensuring adherence to this procedure.`nDepartment Managers/Process Owners:`n Provide input for asset identification, threat and vulnerability identification specific to their areas, and contribute to impact assessment.`nIT Department Personnel:`n Provide technical input on vulnerabilities, existing controls, and technical impacts, leveraging insights from security tools.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Planning the Risk Assessment`n a. The IT Manager will schedule and plan risk assessment sessions. This includes defining the scope of the specific assessment (e.g., an annual full assessment, or a targeted assessment for a new system). b. Identify and gather relevant documentation, including the ISMS Scope Document (IS-CIRQ-D-001-G), previous risk assessments, and the current information asset inventory. c. Assemble a cross-functional risk assessment team, including representatives from IT, relevant department managers, and subject matter experts as needed.`n4.2. Information Asset Identification and Valuation`n a. For the defined assessment scope, identify all relevant information assets by leveraging the `nAsset Inventory maintained via RMM and associated spreadsheet`n. This includes, but is not limited to, data (e.g., customer data, IP, financial), software (e.g., ERP, CAD, GitLab), hardware (e.g., servers, workstations, network devices), services (e.g., cloud services, internet access), and people. b. For each identified asset, determine its business value and importance to `nCirque Corporation`n's operations, especially concerning its Confidentiality, Integrity, and Availability (CIA).`n4.3. Threat Identification`n a. For each asset or group of assets, identify potential threats that could exploit vulnerabilities and cause harm. Threat identification will leverage insights from `nWindows Defender for Business`n (for common cyber threats and malware) as well as broader categories, including: * `nNatural Disasters:`n Fire, flood, earthquake. * `nEnvironmental:`n Power failure, HVAC failure. * `nHuman (Accidental):`n Error, negligence, unintentional disclosure. * `nHuman (Deliberate):`n Unauthorized access, theft, fraud, sabotage, sophisticated cyber-attacks (phishing, ransomware, zero-day exploits). * `nTechnical Failure:`n Hardware failure, software bugs, network outage. b. Record identified threats in the IS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register.`n4.4. Vulnerability Identification`n a. For each asset, identify existing vulnerabilities that could be exploited by identified threats. Vulnerability identification will heavily utilize `nWindows Defender for Business`n for endpoint and server vulnerabilities, alongside other sources such as: * Results from any network scans or penetration tests (if conducted). * Software and system configuration reviews. * Identified weaknesses in processes or human factors (e.g., lack of awareness). * Physical security weaknesses. b. Record identified vulnerabilities in the IS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register.`n4.5. Existing Control Identification`n a. For each identified threat-vulnerability pair, identify any existing information security controls currently in place that mitigate the risk. This includes both technical (e.g., firewalls, encryption, Windows Defender for Business configurations) and non-technical (e.g., policies, training, physical security measures) controls. b. Record existing controls in the IS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register.`n4.6. Risk Analysis (Likelihood and Impact Assessment)`n a. `nLikelihood Assessment:`n For each threat-vulnerability pair, assess the likelihood of the threat exploiting the vulnerability, considering the effectiveness of existing controls. Use the following qualitative scale: * `nLow:`n Unlikely to occur in the foreseeable future (e.g., once every 5+ years). * `nMedium:`n Could occur occasionally (e.g., once every 1-5 years). * `nHigh:`n Likely to occur frequently (e.g., multiple times per year). b. `nImpact Assessment:`n For each threat-vulnerability pair, assess the potential business impact if the incident were to occur, considering the CIA of the affected assets. Use the following qualitative scale: * `nLow:`n Minor disruption, limited financial loss, no significant legal/reputational damage. * `nMedium:`n Moderate disruption, measurable financial loss, some reputational/legal impact, non-critical data breach. * `nHigh:`n Severe disruption, significant financial loss, major reputational/legal damage, critical data breach (e.g., IP loss, severe privacy violation). c. Record both likelihood and impact in the IS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register.`n4.7. Risk Evaluation`n a. Combine the likelihood and impact ratings to determine the inherent risk level (before additional treatment) using the following matrix:`n| **Impact** | **Low Likelihood** | **Medium Likelihood** | **High Likelihood** |`n| :--------- | :----------------- | :-------------------- | :------------------ |`n| **High** | Medium             | High                  | High                |`n| **Medium** | Low                | Medium                | High                |`n| **Low** | Low                | Low                   | Medium              |`nb. Compare the calculated risk level against ``nCirque `nCorporation`n’s`n **low risk appetite** as defined in `IS-CIRQ-P-003-G`. Risks assessed as Medium or High will typically require treatment.`nc. Record the resulting risk level in the `IS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register`.`n4.8. Documentation`n a. All identified risks, their analysis (likelihood, impact, risk level), and existing controls will be documented in the IS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register.`n5. Review and Update`n a. Risk assessments will be conducted periodically, and `nhigh risks will be formally reviewed quarterly`n by the IT Manager and relevant stakeholders. b. The risk assessment process will be reviewed annually as part of the ISMS management review or whenever significant changes in `nCirque Corporation`n's context occur.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-003-G: Risk Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-003-G: Information Security Risk Treatment Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register`nIS-CIRQ-D-001-G: ISMS Scope Document`nIS-CIRQ-PR-003-G: Information Security Risk Treatment Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-003-G`nStandards Name: Information Security Risk Treatment Procedure`nCategory: Base Policy &amp; ISMS Manual`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.1 `nEffective Date:`n `n7/1/2025`n `nReview Date:`n `n7/1/2026`n `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the systematic process for selecting and implementing appropriate risk treatment options for information security risks identified by `nCirque Corporation`n, in accordance with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause 6.1.3. This ensures that risks are reduced to an acceptable level as defined by `nCirque Corporation`n's risk appetite.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all risks identified and evaluated as requiring treatment within `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS scope.`n3. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Accountable for leading the risk treatment process, ensuring Risk Treatment Plans (IS-CIRQ-D-005-G) are developed, implemented, and monitored, and reporting on their status. Also approves the acceptance of residual risks that are not classified as "High."`nDepartment Managers/Process Owners:`n Responsible for implementing risk treatment controls within their respective areas, providing updates on progress, and accepting residual risk.`nIT Department Personnel:`n Responsible for implementing technical controls and providing support for risk treatment activities, leveraging tools such as Windows Defender for Business and Intune.`nExecutive Committee:`n Approves significant risk treatment plans and any accepted residual "High" risks.`nInternal Audit Function:`n Verifies the implementation and effectiveness of risk treatment controls through monthly internal audits.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Identify Risks Requiring Treatment`n a. Review the IS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register. b. Identify all risks evaluated as "Medium" or "High," or any "Low" risks that are deemed unacceptable due to `nCirque Corporation`n's `nlow risk appetite`n. These risks require a formal treatment plan.`n4.2. Select Risk Treatment Options`n a. For each risk requiring treatment, the IT Manager, in consultation with relevant Department Managers and IT Department Personnel, will evaluate and select the most appropriate risk treatment option(s). Options include: * `nModify the risk (Reduce):`n Implement controls to reduce the likelihood or impact of the risk. This is the primary focus for `nCirque Corporation`n given its low risk appetite. Examples include: * Implementing new security software (e.g., enhanced EDR, SIEM). * Applying patches and updates (e.g., via Intune). * Strengthening access controls. * Conducting targeted security awareness training. * Improving backup and recovery procedures. * Implementing encryption for sensitive data. * `nRetain the risk (Accept):`n Accept the risk without further action if it falls within the defined risk acceptance criteria (e.g., a "Low" risk that is impractical or excessively costly to reduce further, and where the IT Manager agrees to accept it). * `nAvoid the risk:`n Stop the activity or remove the asset that is generating the risk. * `nShare or Transfer the risk:`n Transfer the risk to another party, e.g., through insurance, or by contractually obliging a third-party service provider to manage specific risks. b. The selected treatment option(s) must align with the IS-CIRQ-P-003-G: Risk Management Policy and `nCirque Corporation`n's overall risk appetite.`n4.3. Develop Risk Treatment Plan (RTP)`n a. For each risk (or group of related risks) selected for modification, a detailed Risk Treatment Plan (IS-CIRQ-D-005-G) will be developed. b. The RTP will include: * Identification of the risk. * Selected risk treatment option(s). * Specific controls to be implemented (referencing ISO 27001 Annex A controls where applicable). * Responsible parties for implementation. * Target completion dates. * Required resources. * Metrics for measuring effectiveness (if applicable). c. The IT Manager is responsible for developing the RTP in collaboration with relevant stakeholders.`n4.4. Implement Risk Treatment Plan`n a. Responsible parties identified in the RTP will implement the agreed-upon controls and actions within the specified timelines. b. Progress will be regularly monitored by the IT Manager, leveraging monitoring data from `nWindows Defender for Business and Intune`n where applicable for security control status and risk posture. c. Any deviations from the plan or delays must be communicated to the IT Manager for review and adjustment.`n4.5. Assess Residual Risk`n a. Once risk treatment actions have been implemented, the IT Manager will reassess the risk to determine the `nresidual risk`n level (the risk remaining after controls have been applied). b. The reassessment process will follow the methodology outlined in IS-CIRQ-PR-002-G: Information Security Risk Assessment Procedure. c. Record the residual risk in the IS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register.`n4.6. Residual Risk Acceptance`n a. If the residual risk is within `nCirque Corporation`n's defined risk acceptance criteria (i.e., "Low" as per IS-CIRQ-P-003-G), the risk is accepted. The `nIT Manager is authorized to approve the acceptance of these "Low" and acceptable "Medium" residual risks`n. b. If the residual risk remains "High," further treatment options must be identified, and the process (from 4.2 onwards) must be repeated until the risk is reduced to an acceptable level or formally accepted by the Executive Committee with documented justification. c. Any residual risk assessed as "High" must be formally accepted by the Executive Committee.`n4.7. Statement of Applicability (SoA)`n a. Upon completion of risk treatment and the determination of residual risks, the IT Manager will prepare the IS-CIRQ-D-006-G: Statement of Applicability (SoA). b. The SoA will identify the ISO 27001 Annex A controls selected for implementation, provide justification for their inclusion, and justify any exclusions.`n4.8. Internal Audit Verification`n a. The effectiveness of implemented risk treatment controls and the adherence to this procedure will be `nverified through monthly internal audits`n conducted by the designated internal audit function. Findings from these audits will inform continuous improvement.`n5. Review and Update`n a. The effectiveness of implemented risk treatments will be monitored continuously. b. The Risk Treatment Plans and the residual risk levels will be reviewed regularly, especially during the `nquarterly review of high risks`n and as part of the annual ISMS management review. c. This procedure will be reviewed annually or when significant changes occur to the risk management policy or methodology.`n6. Planning to Achieve Objectives`nThe achievement of these objectives will be driven through the ongoing implementation and maintenance of `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS, including:`nRegular information security risk assessments and treatment plans (IS-CIRQ-F-001-G, IS-CIRQ-D-005-G).`nImplementation of controls from the Statement of Applicability (IS-CIRQ-D-006-G).`nDevelopment and delivery of competence, awareness, and training programs (IS-CIRQ-PR-004-G).`nPerformance monitoring and measurement.`nManagement reviews.`n6. The Executive Committee will approve any updates to these objectives.`nIS-CIRQ-P-004-G: Competence, Awareness, and Training Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-004-G`nStandards Name: Competence, Awareness, and Training Policy`nCategory: Base Policy &amp; ISMS Manual`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.1 `nEffective Date:`n `n7/1/2025`n `nReview Date:`n `n7/1/2026`n `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to define `nCirque Corporation`n's commitment and methodology for ensuring that all personnel performing work under its control are competent, aware of the Information Security Management System (ISMS) and its objectives, and properly trained. This policy addresses the requirements of ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clauses 7.2 (Competence) and 7.3 (Awareness).`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel, including permanent employees, temporary staff, contractors, and relevant external parties working within or impacting the ISMS scope, across all locations (US, Japan, China). The same information security rules and training apply globally.`n3. Competence`nCirque Corporation`n shall determine the necessary competence for persons performing work affecting information security performance and ensure that these persons are competent on the basis of appropriate education, training, or experience.`n3.1. Determining Necessary Competence`n a. Job descriptions for all roles will clearly identify the required information security competencies, particularly for roles with significant information security responsibilities (e.g., IT personnel, engineering teams, finance). b. The IT Manager, in conjunction with Department Managers, will assess the information security competence required for each role based on the information assets handled, systems accessed, and security controls managed.`n3.2. Ensuring Competence`n a. `nCirque Corporation`n will ensure that personnel are competent through a combination of: * `nEducation:`n Relevant academic qualifications. * `nTraining:`n Formal or informal training programs specific to information security. * `nExperience:`n Practical work experience in information security or related fields. b. Where necessary competence gaps are identified, `nCirque Corporation`n will take action to acquire the necessary competence (e.g., provide training, assign experienced personnel, or hire new staff) and evaluate the effectiveness of the actions taken. c. Records of education, training, skills, and experience will be maintained by the HR Department.`n4. Awareness`nCirque Corporation`n shall ensure that all persons performing work under its control are aware of:`n4.1. The Information Security Policy and Objectives:`n a. All personnel will be made aware of `nCirque Corporation`n's overall Information Security Policy (IS-CIRQ-P-001-G) and the general objectives of the ISMS upon hiring and periodically thereafter.`n4.2. Their Contribution to the Effectiveness of the ISMS:`n a. Personnel will understand how their individual actions contribute to the effectiveness of the ISMS, including the benefits of improved information security performance. b. Emphasis will be placed on the protection of highly confidential assets such as `nCAD drawings and sales information`n, and the secure handling of intellectual property, firmware, software, and ASIC designs.`n4.3. The Implications of Non-Conformity:`n a. Personnel will be aware of the consequences of non-compliance with the ISMS requirements, including security policy violations, procedures, and legal/contractual obligations. This includes potential disciplinary actions, which may be administered in consultation with HR for severe breaches, and the impact on business operations, reputation, and customer trust.`n5. Training Program`nA structured information security training program will be implemented to support competence and awareness requirements.`n5.1. Training Content`n a. Training content will be tailored to different audiences and roles, covering topics such as: * General information security best practices. * Phishing and social engineering awareness. * Password management. * Clean desk and clear screen policy. * Incident reporting procedures. * Specific policy requirements (e.g., Acceptable Use, Access Control). * Data classification and handling procedures (especially for confidential customer data and intellectual property). * Secure use of internal tools (e.g., GitLab, QuickBooks) and security tools (e.g., Windows Defender for Business, Intune). * Legal and regulatory requirements relevant to `nCirque Corporation`n's global operations. b. `nSpecialized training content for specific departments (e.g., Secure Development Practices for Engineering teams) will be developed and maintained by the respective Department Managers.`n5.2. Training Delivery`n a. Training may be delivered through various methods, including: * Indoctrination/New Hire training. * Online modules (e.g., quizzes, interactive lessons). * Classroom sessions or workshops. * Phishing simulation exercises. * Regular security awareness communications (e.g., emails, posters). * `nDepartment-specific training sessions conducted by managers.`n5.3. Training Frequency`n a. Initial awareness training will be provided to all new personnel upon joining `nCirque Corporation`n. b. Refresher training will be conducted at least annually for all personnel. c. Role-specific and specialized training will be conducted as needed, particularly when new systems, technologies, or significant changes to processes are introduced.`n5.4. Training Records`n a. Records of all information security awareness and training activities, including attendance and completion, will be maintained by the HR Department and/or the IT Manager.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-PR-004-G: Information Security Awareness and Training Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-002-G: Training Records Log`n7. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if significant changes occur to `nCirque Corporation`n's business objectives, ISMS scope, or legal/regulatory environment.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-004-G: Information Security Awareness and Training Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-004-G`nStandards Name: Information Security Awareness and Training Procedure`nCategory: Base Policy &amp; ISMS Manual`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n `n7/1/2025`n `nReview Date:`n `n7/1/2026`n `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to outline the process for planning, developing, delivering, and evaluating information security awareness and training programs at `nCirque Corporation`n, in accordance with IS-CIRQ-P-004-G: Competence, Awareness, and Training Policy. This ensures that all personnel possess the necessary competence and awareness to protect `nCirque Corporation`n's information assets.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel, including permanent employees, temporary staff, and contractors, across all operational locations.`n3. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall coordination and oversight of the information security awareness and training program. Develops and delivers general security awareness content. Ensures training records are maintained.`nDepartment Managers:`n Responsible for identifying specific training needs within their departments, developing specialized training content relevant to their teams' tools and processes, and delivering or arranging for such training.`nHR Department:`n Supports the IT Manager in scheduling and coordinating general training sessions, and maintains records of all employee training and competence.`nAll Employees and Contractors:`n Required to participate in all mandatory information security awareness and training activities.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Annual Training Needs Assessment and Planning`n a. `n(Q4 Annually):`n The IT Manager will collaborate with Department Managers to conduct an annual assessment of information security training needs based on: * Results of risk assessments (IS-CIRQ-F-001-G). * Feedback from incident reports (IS-CIRQ-F-003-G). * Changes in technology, systems (e.g., new tools like Windows Defender/Intune features, GitLab updates), or business processes. * New or updated legal, regulatory, or contractual requirements. * Performance evaluation feedback (if security aspects are included). b. Based on the assessment, the IT Manager will develop an annual Information Security Awareness and Training Plan. This plan will identify: * Target audiences. * Core awareness topics (general). * Specialized training topics (department-specific). * Delivery methods. * Schedule and frequency. * Required resources.`n4.2. Content Development`n a. `nGeneral Awareness Content:`n The IT Manager is responsible for developing and/or procuring general information security awareness content (e.g., phishing awareness, password hygiene, clean desk). b. `nSpecialized Departmental Content:`n Department Managers are responsible for developing or arranging for specialized training content relevant to their teams' specific tools, processes, and highly confidential data (e.g., secure design principles for engineering, secure handling of sales data). This includes training on the secure use of tools like Cadence, SolidWorks, Visual Studio, and internal platforms like GitLab and QuickBooks.`n4.3. Training Delivery`n a. `nNew Hire Orientation:`n All new employees and contractors receive initial information security awareness training as part of their onboarding process, covering IS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy and other foundational topics. b. `nAnnual Refresher Training:`n All personnel will complete mandatory information security awareness training at least annually. c. `nRole-Specific Training:`n Delivered to relevant personnel as identified in the annual plan or as needed due to changes in roles or systems. d. `nAd-hoc Training/Communications:`n The IT Manager may issue ad-hoc communications (e.g., emails, posters, quick tips) or brief training sessions in response to emerging threats (e.g., new phishing campaigns) or specific incidents. e. `nDelivery Methods:`n Training will utilize appropriate methods such as online modules, interactive sessions, webinars, or in-person workshops, as determined by the IT Manager and Department Managers.`n4.4. Training Records Management`n a. The IT Manager is responsible for collecting training completion data. b. The HR Department will maintain comprehensive records of all information security training completed by employees and contractors in the IS-CIRQ-F-002-G: Training Records Log. Records will include: * Employee/Contractor Name. * Date of Training. * Training Topic/Course Name. * Training Provider/Method. * Duration. * Completion Status (e.g., Passed, Completed).`n4.5. Evaluation of Effectiveness`n a. The effectiveness of awareness and training programs will be evaluated through various means, which may include: * Quizzes or assessments following training modules. * Phishing simulation click-through rates. * Analysis of incident reports (e.g., decrease in user-reported incidents, fewer policy violations). * Feedback from participants and managers. b. The results of the evaluation will be used to improve future training programs.`n5. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are changes to relevant policies, technologies, organizational structure, or significant findings from training effectiveness evaluations or audits.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-004-G: Competence, Awareness, and Training Policy`nIS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register`nIS-CIRQ-F-003-G: Information Security Incident Report Form`nIS-CIRQ-F-002-G: Training Records Log`nIS-CIRQ-F-002-G: Records Log`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-F-002-G`nStandards Name: Training Records Log`nCategory: Information Management Forms`nDivision: Form`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n `n7/1/2025`n `nReview Date:`n `n7/1/2026`n `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThis log serves as the central record for documenting all information security awareness and training activities undertaken by `nCirque Corporation`n personnel. It provides evidence of compliance with the IS-CIRQ-P-004-G: Competence, Awareness, and Training Policy and IS-CIRQ-PR-004-G: Information Security Awareness and Training Procedure.`n2. Scope`nThis log covers all personnel (employees and contractors) across all `nCirque Corporation`n locations who undergo information security awareness or competence training.`n3. Instructions for Use`nThis log is maintained by the HR Department, with input from the IT Manager and Department Managers.`nA new entry should be made for each training session attended by personnel.`nEnsure all fields are completed accurately.`n4.
