Dept.)`n3 hrs`nAttended`nGood engagement from team.`nTRL-003`n...`nIS-CIRQ-P-005-G: Communication Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-005-G`nStandards Name: Communication Policy`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.1 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to define `nCirque Corporation`n's framework for effective internal and external communication regarding its Information Security Management System (ISMS). This policy addresses ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause 7.4, ensuring that relevant information security matters are communicated appropriately, consistently, and in a timely manner to interested parties.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all information security-related communications within `nCirque Corporation`n, including communications with employees, contractors, management, external parties (e.g., customers, suppliers, regulators, public), and across all `nCirque Corporation`n locations (US, Japan, China).`n3. Principles of Communication`nCirque Corporation`n's information security communication is guided by the following principles:`nTimeliness:`n Communications will be issued promptly, especially concerning incidents or significant changes.`nClarity and Accuracy:`n Information conveyed will be clear, concise, accurate, and understandable to the intended audience.`nRelevance:`n Only necessary and relevant information will be communicated to specific audiences based on their need-to-know.`nConsistency:`n Information security messages will be consistent across different channels and over time.`nConfidentiality:`n Communications containing sensitive information will be handled with appropriate confidentiality.`nAccountability:`n Responsibilities for communication activities will be clearly defined.`n4. Communication Requirements`nCirque Corporation`n shall establish what to communicate, when to communicate, with whom to communicate, how to communicate, and who communicates regarding information security.`n4.1. What to Communicate:`n a. The scope of the ISMS and the Information Security Policy. b. Information security objectives. c. Roles, responsibilities, and authorities for information security. d. Results of risk assessments and treatment plans. e. Information security incidents (actual or potential) and their handling. f. Changes to the ISMS, policies, and procedures. g. Performance of the ISMS. h. Relevant legal, regulatory, and contractual requirements. i. Security awareness and training information. j. Opportunities for improvement within the ISMS.`n4.2. When to Communicate:`n a. Upon hiring (initial awareness). b. Annually (refresher awareness, policy updates, objectives review). c. Immediately for incident notifications or critical vulnerabilities. d. Periodically as part of ongoing ISMS operation (e.g., risk reviews, audit findings). e. When significant changes affecting information security occur (e.g., new systems, major process changes).`n4.3. With Whom to Communicate (Interested Parties):`nInternal:`nExecutive Committee / Top Management`nIT Manager`nDepartment Managers / Process Owners`nIT Department Personnel`nAll Employees`nContractors`nExternal (as required):`nCustomers (e.g., regarding data breaches, security posture)`nSuppliers/Vendors (e.g., security requirements, supply chain risks)`nRegulatory and legal authorities (e.g., data protection agencies, industry bodies)`nShareholders/Investors`nPublic/Media`n4.4. How to Communicate (Communication Channels):`nInternal:`nCompany Intranet`nTeams channels`nEmails`n (general announcements, targeted updates)`nCompany meetings`n (All-Hands, Departmental, Management Review)`nFormal documentation (policies, procedures, records)`nDirect communication by managers`nTraining sessions`nExternal:`nOfficial correspondence (letters, email)`nWebsite (for public statements)`nSecured portals for customer/supplier information exchange`nDirect contact through designated points of contact (e.g., Legal, IT Manager)`nFor suppliers, communication of security requirements is primarily managed through contractual agreements and verification of security and SOC2 compliance.`n4.5. Who Communicates (Responsibilities):`nExecutive Committee:`n Approves overall communication strategy, communicates high-level commitment, and provides final approval for highly sensitive external communications.`nIT Manager:`n Responsible for managing and coordinating internal ISMS-related communications, particularly technical security alerts, policy/procedure updates, risk assessment results, and initial incident notifications to internal stakeholders. Serves as primary contact for internal ISMS audits/assessments.`nDepartment Managers:`n Communicate information security requirements and updates relevant to their teams and processes.`nHR Department:`n Communicates information security requirements during onboarding, manages disciplinary communications related to security, and supports general awareness campaigns.`nLegal Counsel:`n `nSolely responsible for communications related to external breaches impacting legal or regulatory obligations, and for advising on all legal aspects of information security communications.`nDesignated Public Relations/Communications Lead:`n Manages official public/media statements, particularly during crisis situations, in coordination with Legal Counsel and the Executive Committee.`n5. Related Document`nIS-CIRQ-PR-005-G: ISMS Communication Procedure`n6. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if significant changes occur to `nCirque Corporation`n's organizational structure, communication channels, or regulatory requirements.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-005-G: ISMS Communication Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-005-G`nStandards Name: ISMS Communication Procedure`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to establish the methods and responsibilities for effective internal and external communication related to `nCirque Corporation`n's Information Security Management System (ISMS), in accordance with IS-CIRQ-P-005-G: Communication Policy. This procedure ensures that relevant information is delivered to the right audience, at the right time, and through appropriate channels.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all communications concerning information security within `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS scope, involving all internal personnel and relevant external parties.`n3. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall owner of this procedure. Manages and coordinates internal ISMS communications. Initiates incident-related internal communications.`nDepartment Managers:`n Responsible for cascading ISMS-related communications to their teams and providing feedback upwards.`nHR Department:`n Communicates foundational security policies during onboarding and supports general awareness campaigns.`nLegal Counsel:`n Manages all legally required external communications, especially related to data breaches.`nAll Employees/Contractors:`n Responsible for actively seeking and understanding ISMS communications, and for reporting security incidents or concerns.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Internal ISMS Communication`n4.1.1. General Awareness &amp; Policy Updates (Ongoing)`n a. `nWhat:`n Information Security Policy (IS-CIRQ-P-001-G), ISMS Objectives (IS-CIRQ-D-007-G), general security awareness topics (e.g., phishing prevention, password hygiene, clean desk). b. `nWhen:`n * New hires: During onboarding. * All personnel: Annually (refresher training, policy reviews, objectives communication). * Ad-hoc: As new threats emerge or policies are updated. c. `nHow:`n * New Hire Training (managed by HR and IT Manager). * Annual mandatory security awareness training (online modules, workshops). * `nCompany meetings (All-Hands):`n IT Manager or Executive Committee presents high-level ISMS updates, objectives, and policy highlights. * `nTeams channels/Email:`n Used for general announcements, quick tips, security alerts, and links to updated policies/procedures. * Company Intranet: Repository for all ISMS documentation (policies, procedures, forms). d. `nWho:`n IT Manager, HR Department, Department Managers.`n4.1.2. ISMS Performance and Review Communications (Periodic)`n a. `nWhat:`n Results of risk assessments, audit findings (internal/external), ISMS performance metrics (KPIs from IS-CIRQ-D-007-G), opportunities for improvement. b. `nWhen:`n * `nQuarterly:`n Review of high risks and incident summaries by IT Manager with relevant Department Managers. * `nAnnually:`n Formal Management Review (Executive Committee, IT Manager, key Department Managers). * Ad-hoc: As needed based on significant findings. c. `nHow:`n Formal management review meetings, dedicated reports, team meetings, email summaries. d. `nWho:`n IT Manager, Executive Committee, Department Managers.`n4.1.3. Incident Communication (As Needed - Internal Escalation)`n a. `nWhat:`n Notification of actual or suspected information security incidents. b. `nWhen:`n Immediately upon detection or suspicion of an incident. c. `nHow:`n Defined escalation path: * `nEmployee/Contractor:`n Reports incident to IT Manager (or designated IT contact) via email, Teams message, or ticketing system. * `nIT Manager:`n * Confirms incident. * Notifies Executive Committee. * Notifies relevant Department Managers if their area is affected. * Initiates incident response procedures (IS-CIRQ-PR-006-G). * `nExecutive Committee:`n Notified by IT Manager. * `nLegal Counsel:`n Notified by Executive Committee/IT Manager if a breach or regulatory impact is suspected. * `nAffected Internal Parties:`n Notified by IT `nManager as needed for awareness or action. d. `nWho:`n All personnel (reporting), IT Manager (coordination), Executive Committee, Legal Counsel, Department Managers.`n4.2. External ISMS Communication`n4.2.1. Customer and Partner Communications (As Required)`n a. `nWhat:`n Responses to security questionnaires, information on `nCirque Corporation`n's security posture, security requirements for partners. b. `nWhen:`n Upon request, during contract negotiation, or as required by incidents. c. `nHow:`n Formal documentation, dedicated security portals, direct communication by designated IT or Sales personnel. d. `nWho:`n IT Manager, Sales, Legal Counsel (for contractual aspects).`n4.2.2. Supplier/Vendor Security Communications (Ongoing)`n a. `nWhat:`n Communication of security requirements, verification of compliance (e.g., SOC2 reports). b. `nWhen:`n During vendor selection, contract renewal, and periodic reviews. c. `nHow:`n Included in `ncontracts and agreements`n. Requests for `nsecurity and SOC2 compliance`n documentation. d. `nWho:`n Procurement, IT Manager, Legal Counsel.`n4.2.3. Regulatory and Legal Communications (As Required)`n a. `nWhat:`n Data breach notifications, responses to regulatory inquiries, compliance attestations. b. `nWhen:`n As mandated by law or regulation, or upon direct request from authorities. c. `nHow:`n Formal legal channels, official government portals. d. `nWho:`n `nLegal Counsel (primary)`n, Executive Committee (oversight), IT Manager (technical input).`n4.2.4. Public and Media Communications (Crisis Only)`n a. `nWhat:`n Official statements regarding major security incidents or significant security posture changes. b. `nWhen:`n During crisis situations requiring public disclosure. c. `nHow:`n Public statements, press releases, company website updates. d. `nWho:`n Designated Public Relations/Communications Lead, in strict coordination with Legal Counsel and Executive Committee.`n5. Documentation of Communication`nAll significant communications, particularly those related to incidents, audit findings, policy changes, and external interactions, will be documented and retained as evidence of communication. This may include email archives, meeting minutes, formal reports, and signed acknowledgments.`n6. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are changes to the IS-CIRQ-P-005-G: Communication Policy, communication channels, organizational structure, or significant findings from ISMS audits.`n7. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-005-G: Communication Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-D-007-G: Information Security Objectives`nIS-CIRQ-PR-006-G: Information Security Incident Management Procedure (Next document to draft)`nIS-CIRQ-F-003-G: Information Security Incident Report Form`nIS-CIRQ-P-006-G: Documented Information Control Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-006-G`nStandards Name: Documented Information Control Policy`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish `nCirque Corporation`n's requirements for controlling all documented information related to its Information Security Management System (ISMS). This policy addresses ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause 7.5, ensuring that ISMS documents are properly created, updated, distributed, stored, and retained to maintain the integrity, availability, and confidentiality of information vital to the ISMS.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all documented information required by the ISMS standard (ISO 27001:2022) or deemed necessary for the effectiveness of the ISMS. This includes, but is not limited to, policies, procedures, work instructions, forms, templates, records, and external documents of relevance (e.g., legal and regulatory requirements, contractual agreements). This policy covers documented information in all formats (e.g., electronic, hard copy).`n3. Principles of Document Control`nCirque Corporation`n shall ensure that documented information is:`nAvailable and Suitable:`n Available where and when it is needed, and suitable for use.`nProtected:`n Adequately protected from loss of confidentiality, improper use, or loss of integrity.`nControlled:`n Managed through a systematic process covering creation, update, distribution, retention, and disposition.`n4. Requirements for Documented Information`nCirque Corporation`n's documented information shall be controlled to ensure:`n4.1. Identification and Description:`n a. Each document shall be clearly identified (e.g., unique ID, title, date, author, version number). b. The document's purpose and content shall be clearly described.`n4.2. Format and Media:`n a. Documented information shall be presented in a suitable format and on appropriate media (e.g., paper, electronic).`n4.3. Review and Approval for Suitability and Adequacy:`n a. All documents shall be reviewed and approved by authorized personnel prior to their issuance or update to ensure their suitability and adequacy.`n4.4. Availability:`n a. Documented information shall be available at points of use, to the relevant persons, when and where it is needed.`n4.5. Protection:`n a. Documented information shall be protected against unintended alterations, unauthorized access, loss, damage, or deterioration. b. Access controls will be applied to electronic documents.`n4.6. Distribution, Access, Retrieval, and Use:`n a. There shall be a defined method for the distribution, access, retrieval, and use of documented information.`n4.7. Storage and Preservation:`n a. Documented information shall be stored in a manner that preserves its legibility and integrity, and protects it from damage or deterioration.`n4.8. Control of Changes:`n a. Changes to documented information shall be controlled. This includes ensuring that changes are identified, the current version status is clear, and relevant personnel are aware of the changes.`n4.9. Retention and Disposition:`n a. Documented information shall be retained for a defined period based on legal, regulatory, contractual, and operational requirements. b. A process for the appropriate disposition (archiving or destruction) of documented information shall be defined once its retention period expires.`n4.10. Control of External Documents:`n a. Documents of external origin (e.g., customer contracts, regulatory guidelines, vendor security reports, ISO 27001 standard itself) deemed necessary for the planning and operation of the ISMS shall be identified and controlled. Their distribution and version control will be managed appropriately.`n5. Responsibilities`nIT Manager (ISMS Manager):`n Accountable for the overall implementation and adherence to this policy and the associated procedure. Ensures the Document Control Procedure is maintained and followed.`nDocument Owners:`n Responsible for the content, accuracy, review, and timely update of the documents they own. They initiate the review and approval process.`nAll Employees and Contractors:`n Responsible for adhering to this policy and using the current versions of documented information as required by their roles.`n6. Related Document`nIS-CIRQ-PR-006-G: Document Control Procedure`n7. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if significant changes occur to `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS, organizational structure, or legal/regulatory requirements regarding documented information.`nQuestions for IS-CIRQ-P-006-G: Documented Information Control Policy:`nDocument Storage Location(s):`n Where are ISMS documents (policies, procedures, forms) primarily stored electronically? (e.g., SharePoint, a network drive, specific GRC software). This informs the procedure.`nVersioning System:`n Is there an existing versioning system or naming convention for documents? (e.g., adding v1.0, v1.1 to file names, or a system that manages versions automatically).`nApproval Process Tool/Method:`n How are documents reviewed and approved? (e.g., email chain for approvals, a document management system workflow, physical signatures). For a small company, this could be quite manual or utilize common tools.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-006-G: Document Control Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-006-G`nStandards Name: Document Control Procedure`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the systematic process for managing all documented information within `nCirque Corporation`n's Information Security Management System (ISMS). This includes creation, review, approval, distribution, control of changes, retention, and disposition, in accordance with IS-CIRQ-P-006-G: Documented Information Control Policy and ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause 7.5.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all documented information created, used, or maintained as part of `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS. This includes, but is not limited to, policies, procedures, forms, templates, records, and relevant external documents, regardless of their format (electronic or hard copy) or storage location (local drives, file servers, SharePoint).`n3. Responsibilities`nIT Manager (ISMS Manager):`n Overall accountability for the implementation and maintenance of this procedure. Assigns Document IDs, manages the master document list, controls master copies, and ensures correct versioning.`nDocument Owners:`n Individuals or departments responsible for the content accuracy, relevance, periodic review, and initiation of changes for specific documents.`nReviewers/Approvers:`n Authorized personnel responsible for reviewing and approving documented information before release or modification.`nAll Employees/Contractors:`n Responsible for using the current versions of documented information and adhering to documented procedures.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Document Creation and Identification`n a. `nTemplate Use:`n All new ISMS documents (policies, procedures, forms) shall be created using the official `nCirque Corporation`n ISMS document templates, which include fields for Document ID, Version, Effective Date, Review Date, and Approval. b. `nDocument ID Assignment:`n The IT Manager shall assign a unique Document ID to each new ISMS document. The ID format shall follow the established convention (e.g., IS-CIRQ-P-001-G for Policy, IS-CIRQ-PR-001-G for Procedure, IS-CIRQ-F-001-G for Form, IS-CIRQ-D-001-G for Document). c. `nVersioning:`n The initial version of any new document shall be `nv1.0`n. Subsequent minor changes will increment the decimal (e.g., v1.1, v1.2), while major revisions (e.g., significant content overhaul, change in scope) will increment the whole number (e.g., v2.0). The version number will be clearly displayed on the document.`n4.2. Document Review and Approval`n a. `nInitial Draft:`n The Document Owner prepares the initial draft. b. `nInternal Review:`n The Document Owner distributes the draft to identified reviewers (e.g., relevant department managers, IT personnel) via `nemail or shared file link`n for comments and feedback. c. `nFormal Approval:`n Once reviews are incorporated and the Document Owner deems the document ready, it is submitted to the designated Approver(s) (as specified in the document header, e.g., IT Manager, Executive Committee) via `nemail with the document attached or a secure file share link`n. d. `nEvidence of Approval:`n Approval shall be documented via `nemail confirmation`n from the designated Approver(s) or, if utilizing a system, through the system's audit trail. These email approvals shall be retained as a record. e. `nEffective Date:`n Upon approval, the IT Manager will set the "Effective Date" on the document.`n4.3. Document Distribution and Availability`n a. `nMaster Copies:`n The IT Manager shall maintain the master, approved versions of all ISMS documented information. b. `nOfficial Storage Locations:`n Approved ISMS documents shall be centrally stored on the `nSharePoint`n site designated for ISMS documentation. Copies may also be maintained on the `nFileserver`n for redundancy and accessibility for specific teams. c. `nAccessibility:`n Access to ISMS `ndocuments will be managed through SharePoint and file server permissions, ensuring that relevant personnel have read-only access to current versions. Write access is restricted to Document Owners and the IT Manager. d. `nCommunication of New/Updated Documents:`n The IT Manager will communicate the release of new or significantly updated ISMS documents to relevant personnel via `nTeams channels and/or email`n as per IS-CIRQ-PR-005-G: ISMS Communication Procedure.`n4.4. Control of Changes (Revisions)`n a. `nChange Request:`n Any request for a change to an existing ISMS document should be submitted to the Document Owner, ideally via email. b. `nReview and Update:`n The Document Owner reviews the change request, updates the document, and ensures necessary internal reviews are conducted. c. `nVersioning Update:`n The Document Owner, in consultation with the IT Manager, assigns the next logical version number (e.g., v1.1 to v1.2, or v1.9 to v2.0). d. `nRe-Approval:`n All changes to documents (including minor revisions) must undergo the formal approval process (Section 4.2.c-d) by the designated Approver(s). e. `nObsolete Documents:`n When a document is superseded by a new version, the previous version shall be clearly marked as "Obsolete" or "Superseded" and retained for historical purposes in a dedicated archive folder. Obsolete documents shall be removed from active use locations to prevent unintended use.`n4.5. Retention and Disposition of Documented Information`n a. `nRetention Periods:`n Documented information shall be retained for periods as defined in the "Standard Retention" field of each document's header or as specified by legal, regulatory, or contractual requirements. b. `nArchiving:`n Upon reaching the end of their active lifecycle but still within their retention period, documents may be moved to an archive location (e.g., a dedicated archive folder on SharePoint or file server) to reduce clutter in active repositories. c. `nDisposition:`n Once the retention period expires, documented information shall be disposed of in a secure and unrecoverable manner (e.g., digital shredding for electronic files, cross-shredding for hard copies). The IT Manager is responsible for coordinating the secure disposition.`n4.6. Control of External Documents`n a. External documents (e.g., ISO 27001 standard, relevant laws, customer security requirements) shall be identified, their relevance determined, and controlled. b. The IT Manager is responsible for ensuring that the most current versions of critical external documents are available and referenced. These documents will be stored on SharePoint or the file server with clear identification as "External Document."`n5. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are changes to IS-CIRQ-P-006-G: Documented Information Control Policy, `nCirque Corporation`n's document management tools (e.g., shift to a formal DMS), or legal/regulatory requirements.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-006-G: Documented Information Control Policy`nAll other ISMS policies, procedures, and forms that are subject to this control.`nIS-CIRQ-F-003-G: Document Change Request Form`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-F-003-G`nStandards Name: Document Change Request Form`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Form`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish a framework for the systematic identification, classification, ownership, and protection of all information assets at `nCirque Corporation`n. This policy aligns with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 requirements, ensuring that information assets are managed effectively to reduce risks, maintain their confidentiality, integrity, and availability, and comply with legal, regulatory, and contractual obligations.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all information assets owned by `nCirque Corporation`n, leased by `nCirque Corporation`n, or under the custody of `nCirque Corporation`n, regardless of their format (electronic, physical, intangible) or location (on-premises, cloud, employee devices). This includes, but is not limited to, hardware, software, data, services, networks, and personnel. `nSpecifically, this policy also covers the physical security aspects of `nCirque Corporation`n offices in the US and Taipei, recognizing them as critical physical information assets.`n3. Principles of Asset Management`nCirque Corporation`n is committed to managing its information assets based on the following principles:`nIdentification:`n All information assets within the ISMS scope shall be identified.`nOwnership:`n Clear ownership shall be assigned for each information asset.`nClassification:`n Information assets shall be classified according to their value, criticality, and sensitivity.`nProtection:`n Appropriate controls shall be implemented based on the asset's classification, value, and the results of risk assessments.`nLifecycle Management:`n Assets shall be managed throughout their entire lifecycle, from acquisition to secure disposal.`n4. Asset Identification`n4.1. Asset Register:`n `nCirque Corporation`n shall maintain an up-to-date `nInformation Asset Inventory`n. `nThe RMM (Remote Monitoring and Management) system and Intune are considered the authoritative sources for the asset inventory, with assets being automatically added as agents tag them.`n A supplementary spreadsheet may be used for planning or summary purposes, but the RMM/Intune data remains primary. The inventory shall include, at a minimum: * Unique asset identifier * Asset type (e.g., hardware, software, data, service) * Asset description * Location * Assigned owner * Classification level * Responsible department`n4.2. Asset Types:`n Assets to be identified include, but are not limited to: * `nData:`n Customer data, intellectual property (e.g., CAD drawings, firmware, software code, ASIC designs), financial records, HR data, project data (e.g., in Asana, GitLab), sales information, and email. * `nSoftware:`n Operating systems, applications (e.g., Omnify, Cadence, GitLab, Asana, QuickBooks, Microsoft 365 applications), databases, custom-developed software, firmware. * `nHardware:`n Servers, workstations, laptops, mobile devices, networking equipment, manufacturing equipment, storage devices. * `nServices:`n Cloud services, network services, telecommunication services. * `nPhysical Assets:`n Buildings, offices, rooms, and facilities in the `nUS and Taipei offices`n, and cabinets containing sensitive information. * `nPeople:`n Employees, contractors (in terms of their skills and knowledge, and their access to information).`n5. Asset Ownership`n5.1. Asset Owner Assignment:`n A clear owner shall be assigned for each information asset or group of assets. The asset owner is an individual (e.g., Department Manager, IT Manager) with authority and responsibility for managing the asset throughout its lifecycle and ensuring its appropriate protection.`n5.2. Responsibilities of Asset Owners:`n Asset owners are responsible for: * Determining the classification of their assets. * Ensuring appropriate `ncontrols are applied to their assets based on classification and risk assessment. * Reviewing asset records periodically. * Approving access to their assets. * Ensuring secure handling and disposal of their assets. * `nFor IT-managed assets (servers, endpoints, network devices), the IT Manager acts as the operational custodian, responsible for implementing and maintaining technical controls on behalf of the asset owner.`n6. Asset Classification`n6.1. Classification Scheme:`n Information assets shall be classified based on their sensitivity, value, and criticality to `nCirque Corporation`n, considering potential impact if their confidentiality, integrity, or availability were compromised. The primary classification scheme will be: * `nConfidential:`n Information whose unauthorized disclosure, modification, or destruction would have a `nsevere adverse impact`n on `nCirque Corporation`n. Access is strictly limited to authorized individuals on a need-to-know basis. Examples: Proprietary ASIC designs, unreleased product firmware, customer CAD files, sensitive financial data. * `nInternal Use:`n Information whose unauthorized disclosure, modification, or destruction would have a `nmoderate adverse impact`n on `nCirque Corporation`n. Access is limited to `nCirque Corporation`n employees and authorized contractors. Examples: Internal procedures, general business communications, non-sensitive project plans. * `nPublic:`n Information that is intended for public consumption and whose disclosure would have `nlittle or no adverse impact`n on `nCirque Corporation`n. Examples: Marketing materials, public website content, press releases.`n6.2. Classification Application:`n All information assets, including data, systems, and devices, shall be assigned a classification level. This classification will dictate the minimum security controls required for handling, storage, transmission, and disposal of the asset.`n7. Asset Handling and Protection`n7.1. Protection Controls:`n Appropriate information security controls shall be implemented for assets based on their classification and the results of risk assessments. This includes, but is not limited to: * Access controls (physical, logical) * Encryption for sensitive data * Backup and recovery procedures * Malware protection (e.g., Windows Defender for Business) * Secure configuration management (e.g., via Intune) * Secure disposal procedures * `nPhysical security measures for the US and Taipei offices (e.g., entry controls, visitor management, securing sensitive areas).`n7.2. Acceptable Use:`n All users are responsible for handling `nCirque Corporation`n's information assets in accordance with their classification, applicable policies (e.g., Acceptable Use Policy), and procedures.`n8. Secure Disposal`n8.1. Data and Media Sanitization:`n Information on all media (electronic and physical) shall be properly sanitized or destroyed when it is no longer required or becomes obsolete, according to its classification level, to prevent unauthorized disclosure.`n8.2. Equipment Disposal:`n Equipment containing information assets shall be securely disposed of, ensuring that all data is permanently removed or rendered unrecoverable before disposal or re-assignment.`n9. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register`nInformation Asset Inventory (managed via RMM and Intune)`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`n10. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if significant changes occur to `nCirque Corporation`n's business objectives, asset landscape, or legal/regulatory environment.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-007-G`nStandards Name: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure`nCategory: Information Management Regulations`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to describe the systematic process for classifying, handling, and protecting information assets at `nCirque Corporation`n, in accordance with IS-CIRQ-P-007-G: Asset Management Policy. This ensures that appropriate security controls are applied throughout the asset's lifecycle, from creation/acquisition to disposal.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors) and to all information assets within the defined ISMS scope, regardless of type, format, or location.`n3. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Manages the authoritative asset inventory (RMM/Intune), oversees the classification process, and ensures technical controls are implemented for assets.`nAsset Owners (e.g., Department Managers):`n Responsible for initially classifying their owned assets, ensuring adherence to handling requirements, and reviewing classifications periodically.`nAll Personnel:`n Responsible for understanding and adhering to the asset classification and handling requirements relevant to their roles.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Asset Identification and Inventory Management`n `na. `nInitial Identification:`n When a new information asset is acquired or created (e.g., new server, software license, significant new data set), it shall be identified and recorded. `nb. `nAutomated Tagging (for endpoints/servers):`n For IT-managed hardware and software, the `nRMM system and Intune agents will automatically tag and inventory assets`n. The IT Manager will ensure these systems are configured for comprehensive asset discovery. `nc. `nManual Addition:`n For assets not automatically detected (e.g., specific data sets, cloud services, physical documents, critical physical office spaces), the Asset Owner or IT Manager shall manually add them to the central asset inventory. `nd. `nAsset Inventory Maintenance:`n The IT Manager shall ensure the asset inventory, as managed by RMM/Intune, is regularly updated and accurate. `ne. `nMinimum Inventory Data:`n Each asset record shall include, at minimum: Unique Asset ID, Asset Type, Description, Location, Asset Owner, Date Acquired/Created, and Initial Classification.`n4.2. Asset Ownership Assignment`n `na. For each identified asset, a clear `nAsset Owner`n shall be assigned by the IT Manager in consultation with relevant Department Managers. `nb. The Asset Owner is responsible for the overall protection and classification of the asset, while the IT Manager acts as the operational custodian for IT-managed assets.`n4.3. Asset Classification`n `na. `nInitial Classification:`n The Asset Owner, with guidance from the IT Manager, is responsible for assigning an initial classification level (Confidential, Internal Use, Public) to each owned asset based on its sensitivity, value, and criticality. `nb. `nClassification Criteria:`n The classification shall be determined by assessing the potential impact of a compromise to the asset's confidentiality, integrity, and availability, as defined in IS-CIRQ-P-007-G: Asset Management Policy. `nc. `nDocumentation:`n The assigned classification level shall be recorded in the asset inventory. `nd. `nReview:`n Asset classifications shall be reviewed annually by the Asset Owner or when significant changes to the asset's use, value, or sensitivity occur.`n4.4. Asset Handling Requirements (Based on Classification)`nAll personnel handling information assets must adhere to the following controls based on the asset's classification:`n4.4.1. Confidential Information (e.g., CAD designs, firmware, ASIC designs, customer sales data):`n a. `nStorage:`n Must be stored in secured locations with restricted access (e.g., encrypted network drives, secure cloud storage like SharePoint with strict permissions, dedicated secured folders in GitLab for code). Physical confidential documents must be in locked cabinets or secure rooms. b. `nAccess:`n Strict Need-to-Know basis. Access granted only after formal approval by Asset Owner. Logical access controls (user authentication, strong passwords, MFA) are mandatory. c. `nTransmission:`n Must be transmitted using secure, encrypted channels (e.g., encrypted email, secure file transfer protocols, VPN for internal access). Avoid public file-sharing services. d. `nProcessing:`n Processed only on secured devices (e.g., company-managed workstations with Windows Defender for Business and Intune policies). Do not process on personal devices unless specifically authorized and secured. e. `nPhysical Handling:`n Keep out of sight when not in use. Do not leave unattended on desks. Shred or cross-shred physical documents when no longer needed. f. `nRemote Access:`n Only via secure VPN and on company-approved devices.`n4.4.2. Internal Use Information (e.g., internal procedures, non-sensitive project plans):`n a. `nStorage:`n Stored on company network drives, SharePoint, or approved cloud services. Physical documents in general office areas but not publicly accessible. b. `nAccess:`n Access is restricted to `nCirque Corporation`n employees and authorized contractors on a need-to-know basis for internal business purposes. c. `nTransmission:`n Typically via internal email or company messaging platforms (Teams). For external sharing, consider basic encryption if content is not public. d. `nProcessing:`n Processed on company devices. Exercise caution on personal devices. e. `nPhysical Handling:`n Keep in secure office areas. Shred physical documents before discarding.`n4.4.3. Public Information (e.g., marketing materials, public website content):`n a. `nStorage:`n May be stored in publicly accessible folders or on the company website. b. `nAccess:`n No restrictions on access. c. `nTransmission:`n May be shared via any unencrypted method. d. `nProcessing:`n Can be processed on any device. e. `nPhysical Handling:`n No specific handling requirements beyond general office waste.`n4.5. Physical Asset Handling and Protection (US and Taipei Offices)`n a. `nEntry Controls:`n All visitors must sign in and be escorted by an employee. Access badges for employees are `nmandatory. b. `nSecuring Areas:`n Critical areas (e.g., server rooms, manufacturing floors, design labs where CAD/ASIC work occurs) will have stricter access controls (e.g., keycard access, biometric scanners if applicable). c. `nClean Desk Policy:`n All personnel must maintain a clean desk, especially in areas where sensitive information is handled, ensuring no confidential documents are left unattended. d. `nEquipment Security:`n Workstations and equipment will be physically secured to prevent theft where practical. e. `nEnvironmental Controls:`n Monitoring for temperature, humidity, and fire suppression in server rooms and critical manufacturing areas.`n4.6. Secure Disposal of Assets`n a. `nData Sanitization:`n * `nElectronic Media (Hard Drives, SSDs, USBs):`n Data on media classified as Confidential or Internal Use must be securely wiped using industry-recognized methods (e.g., NIST SP 800-88 guidelines for sanitization) or physically destroyed (e.g., shredding, degaussing) prior to disposal or re-use. * `nCloud Data:`n Ensure data is properly deleted from cloud services following service provider's secure deletion processes. b. `nPhysical Documents:`n Confidential and Internal Use hard copy documents must be cross-shredded or incinerated when no longer required. c. `nHardware Disposal:`n All hardware (laptops, servers, mobile devices) must have their data-bearing components sanitized or destroyed before being recycled, sold, or discarded. The IT Manager will oversee this process.`n5. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are changes to IS-CIRQ-P-007-G: Asset Management Policy, changes in asset types, new threats, or updates to legal/regulatory requirements.`n6. Purpose`nThis `nAsset Register`n serves as a centralized record for identifying, classifying, and tracking all information assets relevant to `nCirque Corporation`n's Information Security Management System (ISMS). It supports the requirements of IS-CIRQ-P-007-G: Asset Management Policy and IS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure, enabling effective risk management and application of security controls.`n2. Instructions for Use`nThis register is primarily maintained by the `nIT Manager`n, leveraging data from the `nRMM system and Intune`n as authoritative sources.`nManual entries should be made for assets not automatically detected (e.g., specific data sets, cloud services, physical documents, critical physical office spaces).`nEnsure all fields are completed accurately for each asset.`nThis register will be reviewed and updated periodically as new assets are acquired/created, or existing assets undergo significant changes or are disposed of.`n4. Information Asset Register`nAsset ID`nAsset Type`nDescription`nLocation`nManufacturer/Vendor`nModel/Version`nSerial Number/License Key`nAsset Owner (Department/Individual)`nAssigned Classification (Confidential/Internal Use/Public)`nDate Acquired/Created`nLast Review Date`nDisposal Date (if applicable)`nNotes (e.g., critical system, specific software dependencies)`nExample Entries:`nHW-SRV-001`nServer (Physical)`nProduction Web Server`nUS Office - Server Room`nDell`nPowerEdge R650`nABC123DEF456`nIT Department`nConfidential`n2024-01-15`n2025-06-15`nHosts critical customer-facing applications.`nSW-`nDB-001`nDatabase`nCustomer `nDatabase`nAzure `nCloud`nMicrosoft`nSQL Serve`nr 2019`nN/A`nSales Department`nConfidential`n2023-09-01`n2025-`n06-15`nContains sensit`nive customer PII.`nIP-CAD-001`nData (IP)`nASIC Design Files`nGitLab / Fileserver`nN/A`nV1.2`nN/A`nEngineering Department`nConfidential`n2024-03-20`n2025-06-15`nCore intellectual property. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to define `nCirque Corporation`n's requirements for controlling access to all information assets, systems, applications, networks, and physical facilities. This policy aims to protect `nCirque Corporation`n's information from unauthorized access, modification, disclosure, or destruction, in accordance with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.8.2. It ensures that access is granted based on the principle of least privilege and need-to-know.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors, temporary staff), external users requiring access, and all `nCirque Corporation`n information assets, IT systems, applications (e.g., Omnify, Cadence, GitLab, Asana, QuickBooks, Microsoft 365 services), networks, and physical facilities (e.g., US and Taipei offices).`n3. Principles of Access Control`nCirque Corporation`n implements access controls based on the following principles:`nLeast Privilege:`n Users shall be granted only the minimum access rights necessary to perform their legitimate job functions.`nNeed-to-Know:`n Access to information and systems shall be granted only to individuals who require it to perform their assigned duties.`nSegregation of Duties (SoD):`n Conflicting duties and areas of responsibility shall be segregated to reduce the risk of fraud, error, or unauthorized access.`nAccountability:`n All access to information assets shall be logged and attributable to individual users.`nRegular Review:`n Access rights shall be regularly reviewed and updated.`n4. User Access Management`n4.1. User Registration and De-registration:`n a. A formal process shall be in place for managing user accounts, covering their entire lifecycle from creation to de-activation/deletion. b. User accounts for computers and internal systems are primarily managed through `nActive Directory`n. c. User accounts shall be created only upon receipt of formal authorization (e.g., from HR for new hires, from Department Managers for contractors). d. User accounts shall be promptly modified (e.g., role change) or de-activated/deleted upon termination of employment, contract, or change in job function, or if prolonged inactivity is detected.`n4.2. User Access Provisioning:`n a. Access rights shall be formally provisioned based on role, job function, and approved requests. b. Generic or shared accounts are generally prohibited, and individual accountability must be maintained where exceptional use of shared accounts is approved. c. Access to highly sensitive systems or data (e.g., source code in GitLab, ASIC designs in Cadence, customer sales data in Omnify/QuickBooks) requires specific, documented authorization from the Asset Owner or IT Manager. d. `nAccess to all internal hosted tools (e.g., GitLab, Asana, QuickBooks, Omnify, Cadence) is restricted to employees only; no external collaborators are permitted.`n e. `nGuest access to SharePoint for specific documents may be granted on an as-needed basis, strictly controlled by the IT Manager or designated document owner.`n4.3. User Access Review:`n a. Access rights shall be reviewed periodically (at least annually) or when a user's role changes. This review ensures that access remains consistent with job responsibilities and the principle of least privilege. b. The IT Manager, in collaboration with Department Managers and Asset Owners, is responsible for conducting these reviews, using reports from Active Directory, application logs, and other relevant systems.`n5. System and Application Access Control`n5.1. Authentication:`n a. All users shall authenticate to systems and applications using unique credentials managed via `nActive Directory or individual application `nauthentication where Active Directory integration is not feasible.`n b. Strong password policies (complexity, length, expiry) shall be enforced. c. Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) shall be implemented for all remote access, privileged access, and access to critical cloud services (e.g., Microsoft 365, GitLab).`n5.2. Privileged Access Management:`n a. Privileged accounts (e.g., administrative accounts within Active Directory or specific applications) shall be strictly controlled and used only when absolutely necessary for system administration tasks. b. Access to privileged accounts shall be logged, monitored, and reviewed regularly. c. Where possible, dedicated privileged accounts, separate from standard user accounts, shall be used.`n5.3. Network Access Control:`n a. Network access shall be controlled through firewalls, network segmentation, and secure network configurations. b. Wireless networks shall be secured with strong encryption (e.g., WPA2 Enterprise) and authentication. c. Remote access to `nCirque Corporation`n's internal networks shall only be permitted via a secure VPN connection from company-managed devices, with MFA enforced.`n5.4. Operating System and Application Access Control:`n a. Operating systems and applications shall be configured to enforce access controls (e.g., user groups, permissions lists within Active Directory). b. Default passwords shall be changed, and unnecessary default accounts disabled or removed. c. Session timeouts shall be implemented to lock idle user sessions. d. Device management tools like `nIntune`n shall be used to enforce security configurations and access policies on endpoints.`n6. Physical Access Control`n6.1. Secure Areas:`n a. Access to physical locations containing sensitive information assets (e.g., server rooms, critical engineering labs, storage areas for physical documents) shall be restricted to authorized personnel only. b. Entry to the `nUS and Taipei offices`n shall be controlled via the `nUnifi Access system`n. c. Visitors shall be controlled (e.g., sign-in, escorted by staff, temporary badges provided by Unifi Access or manually).`n6.2. Monitoring:`n a. Access to secure areas shall be logged by the `nUnifi Access system`n. b. CCTV surveillance may be used in critical areas.`n7.
