Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if significant changes occur to `nCirque Corporation`n's IT environment, organizational structure, or legal/regulatory requirements.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-008-G: Access Control Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-008-G`nStandards Name: Access Control Procedure`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to detail the systematic process for granting, modifying, reviewing, and revoking user access to `nCirque Corporation`n's information assets, systems, applications, networks, and physical facilities. This procedure ensures adherence to IS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy and the principles of least privilege and need-to-know.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors), external parties requiring limited access (e.g., SharePoint guest access), and all relevant information assets, IT systems, applications, networks, and physical facilities across all `nCirque Corporation`n locations (US, Japan, China).`n3. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall owner of this procedure. Manages user accounts in Active Directory and other systems. Configures and manages Unifi Access. Conducts access reviews.`nHR Department:`n Initiates onboarding and offboarding processes for employees and contractors, providing necessary documentation for account creation/deletion.`nDepartment Managers/Asset Owners:`n Responsible for approving access requests for their direct reports and for assets they own.`nAll Personnel:`n Responsible for protecting their login credentials and adhering to all access control policies and procedures.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. User Access Request and Provisioning`n4.1.1. New User Onboarding (Employees)`n a. `nHR Notification:`n HR notifies the IT Manager of a new hire, providing start date, role, department, and initial access requirements (e.g., standard employee access profile). b. `nActive Directory Account Creation:`n IT Manager creates a unique user account in `nActive Directory`n. c. `nSystem Access Provisioning:`n IT Manager provisions access to standard applications (e.g., Microsoft 365, Teams) based on the employee's role. Access to specialized applications (e.g., Omnify, Cadence, GitLab, Asana) requires specific approval from the relevant Department Manager/Asset Owner. d. `nMFA Enrollment:`n New users are required to enroll in Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for their Microsoft 365/Azure AD account and any other critical systems. e. `nPhysical Access (US/Taipei Offices):`n HR coordinates with the IT Manager to provision physical access credentials (e.g., keycard in `nUnifi Access system`n) based on the employee's role and need for access to specific office areas. f. `nInitial Security Awareness:`n New users receive initial information security awareness training, including access control policies.`n4.1.2. Contractor/Temporary Staff Onboarding`n a. `nDepartment Manager Request:`n Department Manager submits a formal request to the IT Manager, specifying contractor name, start/end dates, required access (least privilege basis), and the business justification. b. `nAccount Creation/Provisioning:`n IT Manager creates temporary accounts in `nActive Directory`n and provisions access based on the approved request. All contractor access is time-limited. c. `nPhysical Access:`n If physical office access is required, the Department Manager requests it, and the IT Manager provisions temporary access in `nUnifi Access`n. d. `nSecurity Acknowledgement:`n Contractors must acknowledge understanding of `nCirque Corporation`n's security policies before access is granted.`n4.1.3. Role Change / Access Modification`n a. `nManager Request:`n Employee's manager submits a formal request to the IT Manager detailing the change in role and the corresponding access modifications (additions or removals). b. `nAccess Adjustment:`n IT Manager adjusts permissions in `nActive Directory`n and relevant applications based on the principle of least privilege. Permissions no longer needed are immediately revoked.`n4.2. User Access De-registration / Offboarding`n4.2.1. Employee Termination`n a. `nHR Notification:`n HR notifies the IT Manager of an employee's termination date and time. b. `nImmediate Account De-activation:`n The IT Manager shall immediately de-activate (disable) the employee's `nActive Directory account`n and all associated system/application accounts (e.g., Microsoft 365, GitLab, Omnify) upon notification. Accounts will not be deleted immediately but retained as disabled for a defined period (e.g., 90 days) for auditing and data retention purposes. c. `nPhysical Access Revocation:`n Physical access credentials (e.g., Unifi Access keycard) are immediately revoked/collected. d. `nDevice Retrieval:`n Company-issued devices are collected by HR or IT. e. `nData Transfer/Backup:`n Relevant data from the user's accounts/devices is transferred to the manager or backed up as per data retention policies.`n4.2.2. Contractor/Temporary Staff Offboarding`n a. `nManager Notification:`n Department Manager notifies the IT Manager of the contractor's end date. b. `nAccount De-activation/Deletion:`n The IT Manager de-activates accounts immediately upon the contract end date. For contractors, accounts may be deleted sooner than employee accounts if no data retention requirements exist. c. `nPhysical Access Revocation:`n Immediate revocation of Unifi Access credentials.`n4.3. Access Review (Regular)`n a. `nFrequency:`n The IT Manager shall conduct formal access reviews at least annually for all Active Directory users, application-specific accounts, and physical access permissions. Ad-hoc reviews may be conducted for privileged accounts or high-risk systems more frequently. b. `nReview Process:`n * The IT Manager generates reports of current access rights for systems, applications, and physical locations. * These reports are sent to the respective Department Managers and Asset Owners for their review and confirmation. * Managers/Owners confirm that all listed access rights are still required and align with the principle of least privilege. * Any unauthorized or unnecessary access identified during the review shall be immediately revoked by the IT Manager. c. `nDocumentation:`n Records of access reviews (e.g., signed confirmations, audit trail from review tools) shall be maintained.`n4.4. Privileged Access Management`n a. `nDedicated Accounts:`n Where possible, separate, non-email-enabled, privileged accounts will be created for administrative tasks in `nActive Directory`n and critical applications. b. `nJustification:`n Use of privileged accounts requires specific justification and is logged. c. `nMonitoring:`n All activities performed with privileged accounts shall be logged and monitored for suspicious behavior. d. `nReview:`n Privileged access rights will be reviewed more frequently (e.g., quarterly) than standard user access.`n4.5. External Access (SharePoint Guest Access)`n a. `nRequest and Approval:`n Guest access to SharePoint is granted only upon documented request from an employee, approved by the relevant Document Owner/Department Manager, and the IT Manager. b. `nLeast `nPrivilege:`n Access is granted only to the specific documents or folders required, with read-only permissions by default, unless write access is explicitly justified and approved. c. `nTime-Limited:`n Guest access accounts shall be time-limited and reviewed periodically for continued necessity. d. `nMonitoring:`n Guest access activity should be logged and monitored within SharePoint.`n5. Documentation`nAll access requests, approvals, modifications, and revocations shall be documented. This includes:`nUser access request forms (manual or electronic).`nEmail approvals.`nLogs from Active Directory, Unifi Access, SharePoint, and other application access management systems.`nAccess review reports.`n6. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are changes to IS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy, `nCirque Corporation`n's IT environment (e.g., new identity management systems), or legal/regulatory requirements.`n7. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-007-G: Asset Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-XXX-G: Acceptable Use Policy `nUser Access Request Form `nIS-CIRQ-PR-009-G: Privileged Access Management Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-009-G`nStandards Name: Privileged Access Management Procedure`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the requirements and processes for the secure management of privileged access within `nCirque Corporation`n's IT environment. This procedure aims to minimize the risks associated with the misuse or compromise of privileged accounts, in accordance with IS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy and ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.8.2.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all privileged accounts and credentials across `nCirque Corporation`n's information systems, networks, applications, and infrastructure, regardless of location (on-premises, cloud services). This includes, but is not limited to:`nDomain Administrator accounts (Active Directory)`nLocal Administrator accounts on servers and workstations`nDatabase Administrator accounts`nApplication Administrator accounts (e.g., Omnify, Cadence, GitLab, Asana, Microsoft 365, QuickBooks)`nNetwork device administrator accounts (e.g., firewalls, switches)`nCloud service provider administrative accounts (e.g., Azure, AWS)`nPhysical access control system administrators (e.g., Unifi Access)`nService accounts with elevated privileges`n3. Definitions`nPrivileged Account:`n Any user account, service account, or shared account that has elevated permissions beyond those of a standard user, allowing it to perform critical system functions, access sensitive data, or configure/modify system settings.`nJust-in-Time (JIT) Access:`n A method of granting elevated privileges for a limited time and specific task, typically expiring automatically.`nNeed-to-Use:`n The principle that privileged access is only granted when it is absolutely necessary for a specific, authorized task.`n4. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall owner of this procedure. Responsible for managing privileged accounts, enforcing privileged access policies, conducting regular reviews, and monitoring privileged activity.`nSystem Administrators / IT Personnel:`n Responsible for using privileged accounts strictly in accordance with this procedure.`nExecutive Committee:`n Approves high-level strategy for privileged access and provides necessary resources.`n5. Procedure`n5.1. Identification of Privileged Accounts`n a. The IT Manager shall maintain a comprehensive inventory of all identified privileged accounts, including their purpose, associated systems, and the individuals authorized to use them. b. This inventory shall be reviewed at least quarterly.`n5.2. Creation and Management of Privileged Accounts`n a. `nDedicated Accounts:`n Wherever possible, separate, individual accounts shall be created for each administrator requiring privileged access. These accounts shall be distinct from their standard user accounts. * Example: An administrator named "John Doe" would have a standard user account (johndoe) for daily tasks and a separate privileged account (jd-admin) for administrative functions. b. `nUnique Credentials:`n Each privileged account shall have a `nunique, strong password. c. `nMinimal Privileges:`n Privileged accounts shall be configured with the absolute minimum set of permissions necessary to perform their intended function (least privilege principle). d. `nGeneric/Shared Accounts:`n Use of generic or shared privileged accounts (e.g., "administrator") is strictly prohibited unless technically unavoidable. In such cases, usage must be strictly controlled, logged, monitored, and reviewed, with individual accountability ensured through other means (e.g., session recording, strict check-out/check-in process).`n5.3. Authentication for Privileged Access`n a. `nStrong Passwords:`n All privileged accounts shall enforce `nCirque Corporation`n's strong password policy (complexity, length, non-repetition). b. `nMulti-Factor Authentication (MFA):`n MFA shall be mandatory for all privileged accounts, including `nActive Directory domain administrator accounts`n, access to critical cloud platforms (e.g., Azure, Microsoft 365 Admin Centers), and network device administration. c. `nSecure Workstations:`n Privileged administrative tasks shall ideally be performed from designated, hardened administrative workstations that are logically separated from standard user networks and services.`n5.4. Granting and Revoking Privileged Access`n a. `nFormal Request &amp; Approval:`n All requests for new or modified privileged access must be formally documented (e.g., via a ticketing system or email approval) and approved by the IT Manager and/or relevant Department Manager. The request must include a clear business justification and the duration for which access is required. b. `nTime-Limited Access (Just-in-Time):`n Where possible, privileged access shall be granted on a time-limited (Just-in-Time) basis, automatically expiring after a specified duration (e.g., 4 or 8 hours) or upon completion of the task. c. `nPrompt Revocation:`n Privileged access shall be immediately revoked when no longer required due to role changes, termination of employment/contract, or completion of a specific task.`n5.5. Monitoring and Logging of Privileged Activity`n a. `nComprehensive Logging:`n All activities performed using privileged accounts shall be extensively logged on all relevant systems (`nActive Directory, server event logs, application logs, network device logs, cloud platform audit logs`n). Logs shall include user, date/time, action performed, and system/resource accessed. b. `nCentralized Logging:`n Logs from privileged activity should be sent to a centralized log management system for correlation and analysis. c. `nAlerting:`n Alerts shall be configured for suspicious or unauthorized activities involving privileged accounts (e.g., multiple failed login attempts, access to unusual resources, changes to critical configurations). d. `nRegular Review:`n The IT Manager shall regularly review privileged access logs (e.g., daily for critical systems, weekly for others) for anomalies or policy violations.`n5.6. Periodic Review of Privileged Access`n a. The IT Manager shall conduct a formal review of all privileged accounts and their associated access rights at least `nquarterly`n. b. This review includes: * Verifying continued need for each privileged account. * Confirming that assigned permissions align with least privilege. * Identifying and disabling dormant or unnecessary privileged accounts. * Reviewing audit trails of privileged activity. c. Documentation of the review findings and any actions taken shall be maintained.`n5.7. Secure Configuration and Maintenance`n a. Privileged accounts, systems, and tools shall be securely configured (e.g., following hardening guides like CIS benchmarks) and regularly patched. b. Shared secrets (e.g., service account passwords) shall be managed securely within a password vault solution if applicable.`n6. Training and Awareness`nAll personnel granted privileged access shall receive specific training on the requirements of this procedure, the responsible use of privileged accounts, and the potential impact of misuse.`n7. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-008-G: Access Control Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-006-G: Document Control Procedure (for managing this procedure)`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`n8. Procedure Review`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if significant changes occur to `nCirque Corporation`n's IT infrastructure, threat landscape, or relevant regulations.`nIS-CIRQ-P-009-G: Cryptography Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-009-G`nStandards Name: Cryptography Policy`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish `nCirque Corporation`n's requirements for the effective and appropriate use of cryptographic controls to protect the confidentiality, integrity, and authenticity of information. This policy aligns with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.8.24, ensuring that cryptographic solutions are implemented to mitigate risks to information assets throughout their lifecycle, including storage, processing, and transmission.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors), and to all information, information systems, applications, and communications, where cryptographic controls are deemed necessary based on risk assessments, legal, regulatory, or contractual requirements. This includes information related to intellectual property (e.g., CAD drawings, firmware, ASIC designs), customer data, and general business operations, regardless of its location (on-premises, cloud, mobile devices).`n3. Principles of Cryptography Use`nCirque Corporation`n's use of cryptography is guided by the following principles:`nRisk-Based Approach:`n Cryptographic controls shall be applied where justified by information security risk assessments and the classification of information assets (e.g., Confidential, Internal Use).`nStrength and Appropriateness:`n Cryptographic algorithms, key lengths, and protocols shall be of appropriate strength for the sensitivity and longevity of the information they protect, and aligned with industry best practices and standards.`nKey Management:`n Cryptographic keys shall be securely generated, stored, distributed, used, backed up, and recovered, and destroyed.`nCompliance:`n The use of cryptography shall comply with all applicable legal, regulatory, and contractual obligations.`nTransparency and Auditability:`n The implementation and use of cryptographic controls shall be documented and auditable.`n4. Requirements for Cryptographic Controls`n4.1. Data at Rest Encryption:`n a. `nSensitive Data Storage:`n Information classified as "Confidential" or "Internal Use" shall be encrypted when stored on endpoints (laptops, workstations), file servers, or portable media. * `nEndpoints:`n Full Disk Encryption (FDE) shall be mandated for all company-issued laptops and workstations, managed via `nIntune`n. * `nFile Servers:`n Sensitive data stored on network file shares shall be encrypted at rest. * `nCloud Storage:`n Data stored in cloud services (e.g., Microsoft 365 SharePoint) shall leverage the native encryption capabilities provided by the service provider, with appropriate configuration. * `nDatabases:`n Databases containing "Confidential" information (e.g., customer data, financial records) shall utilize encryption at rest mechanisms (e.g., Transparent Data Encryption - TDE). b. `nIntellectual Property:`n All intellectual property, including CAD drawings, firmware, software code stored in `nGitLab`n, and ASIC designs developed using `nCadence`n, must be stored encrypted, both at rest and in transit, within designated secure repositories.`n4.2. Data in Transit Encryption:`n a. `nRemote Access:`n All remote access to `nCirque Corporation`n's internal networks shall be secured using strong cryptographic protocols (e.g., IPsec VPN, TLS 1.2 or higher). b. `nExternal Communications:`n Communications with external parties involving "Confidential" or "Internal Use" information (e.g., email, file transfers) shall be encrypted. This includes: * `nEmail:`n Use of secure email gateways enforcing TLS for transit or end-to-end encryption for highly sensitive content. * `nWebsite/Web Applications:`n All `nCirque Corporation`n websites and web-based applications that collect or transmit sensitive information must use HTTPS with strong TLS protocols. * `nFile Transfers:`n Secure file transfer protocols (e.g., SFTP, FTPS over TLS, secure cloud-`nbased file sharing services with encryption) shall be used for transferring sensitive files externally.`n4.3. Cryptographic Key Management:`n a. `nKey Generation:`n Cryptographic keys shall be generated using approved, cryptographically strong random number generators. b. `nKey Storage:`n Keys shall be stored securely, segregated from the encrypted data, and protected against unauthorized access, disclosure, or modification (e.g., hardware security modules (HSMs), secure key vaults, encrypted containers). c. `nKey Distribution:`n Key distribution shall be performed through secure, authenticated channels. d. `nKey Usage:`n Keys shall be used only for their intended purpose and by authorized entities. e. `nKey Backup and Recovery:`n Procedures for secure backup and recovery of cryptographic keys shall be established and tested. f. `nKey Destruction:`n Keys shall be securely destroyed when they are no longer required. g. `nKey Rotation:`n Critical cryptographic keys shall be rotated periodically as defined by industry best practices and risk assessments.`n4.4. Cryptographic Algorithm Selection:`n a. `nCirque Corporation`n shall use cryptographic algorithms, key lengths, and protocols that are recognized as strong and secure by current industry standards (e.g., NIST, FIPS 140-2 validated modules). b. Deprecated or weak cryptographic algorithms (e.g., MD5, SHA-1 for digital signatures, DES, RC4, SSLv2/v3, TLS 1.0/1.1) shall not be used. The IT Manager is responsible for identifying and mitigating their use.`n4.5. Cryptographic Controls for Authentication:`n a. Cryptographic techniques (e.g., hashing, digital signatures, strong authentication protocols) shall be used to protect authentication information (e.g., passwords) and verify user identities. b. Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) will be used for privileged access and remote access.`n5. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Responsible for the overall implementation, management, and oversight of cryptographic controls, including selection of algorithms, key management, and ensuring compliance with this policy.`nSystem Administrators / IT Personnel:`n Responsible for configuring and maintaining cryptographic solutions as per this policy and related procedures.`nAll Personnel:`n Responsible for adhering to policies and procedures related to the use of encrypted systems and data (e.g., ensuring full disk encryption is active, using VPN for remote access).`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-007-G: Asset Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-008-G: Access Control Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-009-G: Privileged Access Management Procedure`n7. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if significant changes occur to `nCirque Corporation`n's IT environment, the threat landscape, or new cryptographic vulnerabilities are discovered.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-010-G: Key Management Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-010-G`nStandards Name: Key Management Procedure`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to establish the systematic process for managing cryptographic keys throughout their entire lifecycle, from generation to destruction. This procedure ensures the secure and effective use of cryptography in accordance with IS-CIRQ-P-009-G: Cryptography Policy and ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.8.24, safeguarding the confidentiality, integrity, and authenticity of `nCirque Corporation`n's information.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all cryptographic keys used by `nCirque Corporation`n to protect its information assets, including but not limited to:`nKeys for Full Disk Encryption (FDE) (e.g., managed by Intune).`nKeys for data encryption at rest (e.g., database encryption, file server encryption).`nKeys for data in transit encryption (e.g., VPNs, TLS certificates for websites, secure email gateways).`nDigital signature keys.`nKeys used for Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA).`n3. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall owner of this procedure. Responsible for ensuring the secure implementation and operation of key management practices, including key generation, storage, distribution, and destruction.`nSystem Administrators / IT Personnel:`n Responsible for executing key management tasks as directed by the IT Manager and in accordance with this procedure.`nCloud Service Providers:`n Responsible for their underlying key management practices where `nCirque Corporation`n utilizes their native encryption features (e.g., Microsoft 365, Azure). `nCirque Corporation`n remains responsible for policy enforcement and configuration of customer-managed keys.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Key Generation`n a. Cryptographic keys shall be generated using approved, cryptographically strong algorithms and robust random number generators. b. Keys for high-assurance applications (e.g., root Certificate Authority keys, master encryption keys) shall be generated in secure, controlled environments, ideally using Hardware Security Modules (HSMs) or equivalent secure cryptographic devices if available. c. Key lengths shall adhere to current industry best practices and IS-CIRQ-P-009-G: Cryptography Policy.`n4.2. Key Storage`n a. `nSecure Storage:`n Cryptographic keys shall be stored securely, segregated from the encrypted data, and protected against unauthorized access, disclosure, or modification. b. `nDesignated Locations:`n * `nProduction Keys:`n Stored in dedicated key management systems, secure key vaults (e.g., Azure Key Vault), or HSMs. * `nSystem Keys:`n Passwords for service accounts or system-level keys may be stored in an approved, encrypted password manager or vault accessible only to authorized IT personnel. * `nEndpoint FDE Keys:`n Recovery keys for Full Disk Encryption (e.g., BitLocker recovery keys) shall be securely stored and managed (e.g., within Intune or Active Directory). c. `nAccess Control:`n Access to key storage locations shall be strictly controlled on a need-to-know basis, with multi-factor authentication for privileged access. d. `nEncryption of Stored Keys:`n Keys, especially those stored outside of HSMs, shall themselves be encrypted where technically feasible.`n4.3. Key Distribution`n a. `nSecure Channels:`n Keys shall be distributed only through secure, authenticated, and encrypted channels. b. `nNo Unsecured Transmission:`n Keys must never be transmitted via unencrypted email or other unsecured communication methods. c. `nAutomated Distribution:`n For endpoints, FDE keys are managed and distributed via `nIntune`n. d. `nManual Distribution:`n For manual key exchange (e.g., for VPN pre-shared keys), secure out-of-band methods shall be used (e.g., encrypted communication, physical delivery).`n4.4. Key Usage`n a. `nIntended Purpose:`n Keys shall be used only for their intended cryptographic purpose (e.g., an encryption key for encryption, a signing key for digital signatures). b. `nLeast Privilege:`n Access to use keys shall be granted based on the principle of least privilege. c. `nLogging:`n All uses of critical cryptographic keys shall be logged where possible, for auditing purposes.`n4.5. Key Backup and Recovery`n a. `nRegular Backups:`n All critical cryptographic keys shall be regularly backed up in a secure, encrypted format to ensure availability in case of loss or corruption. b. `nSecure Storage for Backups:`n Key backups shall be stored in a separate, secure location, distinct from the primary key storage. c. `nRecovery Procedures:`n Documented procedures for secure key recovery shall be established and regularly tested to ensure integrity and functionality.`n4.6. Key Archiving and Rotation`n a. `nKey Archiving:`n Keys that are no longer actively used but are still required for decryption of archived data (e.g., for legal retention purposes) shall be securely archived. b. `nKey Rotation:`n Critical cryptographic keys (e.g., server certificates, VPN keys, database encryption keys) shall be periodically rotated as defined by risk assessments and industry best practices. The rotation frequency will be determined by the IT Manager.`n4.7. Key Destruction`n a. `nSecure Destruction:`n Keys shall be securely destroyed when they are no longer required and their retention period has expired. b. `nMethods:`n Destruction methods shall ensure that the keys are irretrievable (e.g., cryptographic erasure for software keys, physical destruction for hardware tokens/HSMs if necessary). c. `nDocumentation:`n The destruction of critical keys shall be documented.`n5. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's cryptographic controls, IT environment, threat landscape, or legal/regulatory requirements.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-009-G: Cryptography Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-007-G: Asset Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-008-G: Access Control Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-009-G: Privileged Access Management Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-010-G: Physical and Environmental Security Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-010-G`nStandards Name: Physical and Environmental Security Policy`nCategory: Physical and Environmental Security`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish `nCirque Corporation`n's requirements for physical and environmental security to protect its information assets, facilities, and personnel from unauthorized access, damage, theft, and environmental hazards. This policy aligns with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.7, ensuring the continued confidentiality, integrity, and availability of information by controlling physical access and protecting against environmental threats.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n premises, including its `nUS office in South Jordan, Utah, and its Taipei office`n, as well as any other locations where `nCirque Corporation`n information assets are stored or processed. It covers all physical information assets, IT equipment, documentation, and personnel within these environments.`n3. Principles of Physical and Environmental Security`nCirque Corporation`n is committed to maintaining a secure physical and environmental infrastructure based on the following principles:`nLayered Defense:`n Employing multiple layers of physical security controls to deter, detect, delay, and respond to unauthorized access or environmental threats.`nRisk-Based Approach:`n Implementing physical and environmental controls proportionate to the risks identified through risk assessments and the classification of assets.`nAccountability:`n Ensuring clear responsibilities for physical security management and monitoring.`nCompliance:`n Adhering to relevant legal, regulatory, and contractual requirements related to physical and environmental security.`n4. Secure Areas`n4.1. Identification of Secure Areas:`n a. Critical areas containing sensitive information assets (e.g., server rooms, network closets, engineering labs where CAD/ASIC designs are developed, secured storage for physical documents) shall be identified as "Secure Areas." b. The `nUS office and Taipei office premises`n themselves are considered controlled secure areas.`n4.2. Physical Entry Controls:`n a. Access to `nCirque Corporation`n facilities and Secure Areas shall be restricted to authorized personnel only. b. `nUnifi Access System:`n The `nUnifi Access system`n shall be utilized for managing access control to office buildings and designated secure areas within the `nUS and Taipei offices`n. c. `nAuthentication:`n Access to secure areas shall require proper authentication (e.g., key cards, biometric scanners where implemented). d. `nVisitor Control:`n All visitors to `nCirque Corporation`n premises shall be required to sign in, be issued a temporary badge (e.g., via Unifi Access), and be escorted by authorized personnel at all times when in controlled areas. e. `nDelivery and Loading Areas:`n Access points for deliveries and loading shall be controlled and monitored to prevent unauthorized entry.`n4.3. Physical Protection Against Threats:`n a. `nIntrusion Detection:`n Alarm systems and surveillance (CCTV) shall be installed and monitored in and around `nCirque Corporation`n facilities and Secure Areas. b. `nPerimeter Security:`n Physical barriers (e.g., walls, fences, secure doors, locks) shall be in place to define the secure perimeter and deter unauthorized access. c. `nProtection from Natural Disasters:`n Facilities shall be selected and maintained to minimize risks from natural disasters (e.g., flooding, earthquakes) and equipped with appropriate protective measures.`n5. Equipment Security`n5.1. Equipment Placement and Protection:`n a. Equipment processing or storing sensitive information shall be physically protected from unauthorized access, environmental damage, and power fluctuations. b. Critical equipment shall be located in areas with appropriate environmental controls (e.g., temperature, humidity, fire suppression). c. Equipment shall be placed to minimize unauthorized viewing of information (e.g., screen privacy).`n5.2. Power and Cabling Security:`n a. Equipment shall be protected from power failures and fluctuations through uninterruptible power supplies (UPS) and surge protectors. b. Power and telecommunications cabling carrying sensitive data shall be protected from interception or damage.`n5.3. Equipment Maintenance:`n a. Maintenance of equipment shall be performed by authorized personnel only. b. Security controls shall remain in place during and after maintenance activities.`n5.4. Equipment Removal and Disposal:`n a. Equipment containing storage media shall not be removed from `nCirque Corporation`n premises without explicit authorization. b. Prior to disposal or repurposing, all data on equipment storage media shall be securely erased or destroyed in accordance with IS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure.`n6. Environmental Controls`n6.1. Climate Control:`n a. Server rooms and other critical IT infrastructure areas shall have appropriate heating, ventilation, and air conditioning (HVAC) systems to maintain optimal operating temperatures and humidity levels. b. Environmental monitoring systems shall be in place to alert personnel to significant deviations from optimal conditions.`n6.2. Fire Safety:`n a. Fire detection and suppression systems (e.g., smoke detectors, fire extinguishers, inert gas suppression for server rooms) shall be installed and regularly tested. b. Emergency exit routes shall be clearly marked and kept free from obstruction.`n6.3. Water and Other Hazards:`n a. Measures shall be in place to detect and prevent damage from water leaks, floods, and other liquids in areas where information assets are stored or processed. b. Protection against other environmental hazards (e.g., dust, vibrations) shall be implemented where necessary.`n7. Clear Desk and Clear Screen Policy`n7.1. Clear Desk:`n All personnel shall maintain a clear desk policy, ensuring that sensitive or confidential documents and removable media are secured when not in use or when leaving their workspace. `n7.2. Clear Screen:`n All personnel shall employ clear screen practices, `nensuring computer screens displaying sensitive information are locked or protected from unauthorized viewing when left unattended.`n8. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if significant changes occur to `nCirque Corporation`n's facilities, environmental conditions, or legal/regulatory requirements.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-011-G: Physical Access Control Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-011-G`nStandards Name: Physical Access Control Procedure`nCategory: Physical and Environmental Security`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the systematic process for managing and controlling physical access to `nCirque Corporation`n's facilities and secure areas, specifically the US and Taipei offices. This procedure implements IS-CIRQ-P-010-G: Physical and Environmental Security Policy and IS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy, ensuring that only authorized individuals gain entry and that physical assets are protected from unauthorized access, damage, or theft.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors, temporary staff), visitors, and service personnel requiring physical access to `nCirque Corporation`n premises, including the `nUS office in South Jordan, Utah, and the Taipei office`n. It covers all physical access points, entry controls, and visitor management processes.`n3. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall owner of this procedure. Manages the `nUnifi Access system`n, issues and revokes access credentials, monitors access logs, and conducts physical access reviews.`nHR Department:`n Notifies IT of new hires, employee terminations, and role changes that impact physical access.`nDepartment Managers:`n Responsible for approving physical access requests for their personnel and ensuring their compliance with this procedure.`nAll Employees/Contractors:`n Responsible for maintaining the security of their access credentials, challenging unknown individuals, and adhering to all physical security protocols.`nReception/Front Desk Personnel:`n Responsible for visitor management and initial access control.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Physical Access Provisioning (Employees &amp; Contractors)`n4.1.1. New Hires (Employees)`n a. `nHR Notification:`n Upon a new hire's approval and start date confirmation, HR submits a request to the IT Manager for physical access provisioning. b. `nAccess Level Determination:`n The IT Manager, in consultation with the employee's Department Manager, determines the appropriate physical access level (e.g., general office access, specific lab access) based on job function and need-to-know. c. `nCredential Issuance:`n The IT Manager or designated personnel creates and issues an access keycard (or equivalent credential) through the `nUnifi Access system`n. The keycard is configured with the approved access level and effective dates. d. `nAcknowledgement:`n The new employee acknowledges receipt of the keycard and understanding of physical access policies.`n4.1.2. Contractors/Temporary Staff`n a. `nManager Request:`n The sponsoring Department Manager submits a formal request to the IT Manager, specifying the contractor's name, company, start/end dates, specific areas requiring access, and business justification. b. `nTime-Limited Credential:`n The IT Manager issues a time-limited access keycard via the `nUnifi Access system`n, configured for the approved duration and specific access areas. c. `nSecurity Briefing:`n Contractors receive a brief on `nCirque Corporation`n's physical security policies and must agree to abide by them.`n4.1.3. Role Change / Access Modification`n a. `nManager Request:`n An employee's or contractor's manager requests changes to physical access privileges (addition or removal of access to specific areas) via email to the IT Manager. b. `nAccess Adjustment:`n The IT Manager updates the individual's access permissions within the `nUnifi Access system`n to reflect the new requirements, adhering to the principle of least privilege. Permissions no longer needed are immediately revoked.`n4.2. Physical Access De-provisioning (Offboarding)`n4.2.1. Employee Termination`n a. `nHR Notification:`n HR notifies the IT Manager of an employee's termination date and time. b. `nImmediate Credential Revocation:`n The IT Manager shall immediately revoke the employee's access keycard in the `nUnifi Access system`n upon notification. c. `nKeycard Collection:`n The keycard shall be collected by HR or the employee's manager on the last day of employment.`n4.2.2. Contractor/Temporary Staff Offboarding`n a. `nManager Notification:`n The sponsoring Department Manager notifies the IT Manager of the contractor's end date. b. `nCredential Revocation:`n The IT Manager immediately revokes the contractor's access keycard in the `nUnifi Access system`n on or before the contract end date. c. `nKeycard Collection:`n The keycard shall be collected by the sponsoring manager or IT.`n4.3. Visitor Management`na. **Entry Point:** All visitors must enter through the main reception area of the US or Taipei office.`nb. **Sign-In:** Visitors must sign in upon arrival, providing their name, company, person visiting, and purpose of visit. The **Unifi Access system** may facilitate guest sign-in and temporary badge issuance.`nc. **Badge Issuance:** Visitors shall be issued a clearly visible temporary visitor badge.`nd. **Escort Policy:** All visitors must be escorted by an ``nCirque Corporation`n` employee while within controlled areas (e.g., offices, labs, manufacturing floors). Visitors are not permitted to wander unescorted.`ne. **Sign-Out:** Visitors must sign out upon departure and return their temporary badge.`n4.4. Physical Access Controls for Secure Areas`na. **Designated Areas:** Server rooms, network closets, critical engineering labs (e.g., for ASIC design, firmware development), and secure storage rooms for physical confidential documents are designated as Secure Areas.`nb. **Strict Access:** Access to these Secure Areas is restricted to personnel with explicit authorization based on their job function and approved by the IT Manager and/or relevant Department Manager.`nc. **Unifi Access System Logging:** All entries and exits to Secure Areas are logged by the **Unifi Access system**. These logs are regularly reviewed by the IT Manager.`nd. **No Tailgating:** All personnel, including those with authorized access, must ensure that no unauthorized individuals "tailgate" or follow them into secure areas.`n4.5. Monitoring and Review`na. **Access Log Review:** The IT Manager shall regularly review physical access logs generated by the **Unifi Access system** for suspicious activity, unauthorized attempts, or anomalies.`nb. **CCTV Monitoring:** CCTV systems (if installed) in and around ``nCirque Corporation`n` premises shall be monitored, and footage retained as per retention policies for security and incident investigation purposes.`nc. **Physical Access Reviews:** The IT Manager shall conduct a formal review of all physical access rights at least annually, confirming continued necessity with Department Managers.`n4.6. Lost/Stolen Access Credentials`na. Any lost or stolen access keycard or physical key must be reported immediately to the IT Manager.`nb. The IT Manager will promptly revoke the lost/stolen credential in the **Unifi Access system** and issue a replacement, if necessary.`nc. An incident report may be initiated for investigation.`n5. Documentation`nAll physical access requests, approvals, modifications, and revocations shall be documented. This includes:`nAccess request emails/forms.`nUnifi Access system logs and reports.`nVisitor sign-in/out logs.`nRecords of access reviews.`n6. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are changes to IS-CIRQ-P-010-G: Physical and Environmental Security Policy, `nCirque Corporation`n's physical security systems (e.g., Unifi Access updates), or changes in facility layout.`n7. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-010-G: Physical and Environmental Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-008-G: Access Control Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-007-G: Asset Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-XXX-G: Visitor Log (Future form, if formalizing beyond Unifi Access native log)`nIS-CIRQ-PR-012-G: Equipment Security Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-012-G`nStandards Name: Equipment Security Procedure`nCategory: Physical and Environmental Security`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the systematic process for protecting `nCirque Corporation`n's IT equipment from theft, damage, unauthorized access, and environmental hazards throughout its lifecycle. This procedure implements IS-CIRQ-P-010-G: Physical and Environmental Security Policy and IS-CIRQ-P-007-G: Asset Management Policy, ensuring the continued confidentiality, integrity, and availability of information processed, stored, or transmitted by these assets.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n-owned or leased IT equipment, including but not limited to:`nServers, network devices, and data storage systems.`nWorkstations, laptops, and mobile devices.`nManufacturing equipment with IT components or data storage.`nPeripherals (printers, scanners, external drives).`nCables, power supplies, and environmental control equipment (e.g., UPS units).`nThis procedure covers equipment located in `nCirque Corporation`n's offices (US and Taipei), remote work locations, and equipment in transit.`n3. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall owner of this procedure. Responsible for implementing and overseeing equipment security controls, maintaining the equipment inventory, and managing equipment disposal processes.`nAll Employees/Contractors:`n Responsible for safeguarding company-issued equipment entrusted to them and adhering to all equipment security policies and procedures.`nFacilities Management:`n Responsible for physical environmental controls (HVAC, fire suppression) and general building security in coordination with IT.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Equipment Acquisition and Inventory`n a. `nProcurement:`n All IT equipment shall be procured through approved channels, ensuring compatibility with `nCirque Corporation`n's security standards. b. `nAsset Tagging:`n Upon acquisition, all new IT equipment shall be immediately tagged with a unique asset ID and recorded in the `nInformation Asset Inventory (RMM and Intune)`n, as per IS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure. c. `nInitial Configuration:`n Equipment shall be configured securely before deployment, including changing default passwords, applying security baselines (via `nIntune`n for endpoints), and installing necessary security software (`nWindows Defender for Business`n).`n4.2. Equipment Placement and Protection`n a. `nSecure Location:`n All critical IT equipment (e.g., servers, network devices, sensitive manufacturing equipment) shall be placed in designated secure areas with restricted physical access (e.g., server rooms, locked cabinets) as defined in IS-CIRQ-PR-011-G: Physical Access Control Procedure. b. `nEnvironmental Protection:`n Equipment in secure areas shall be protected from environmental threats: * `nTemperature/Humidity:`n Ensure HVAC systems maintain optimal operating conditions. * `nFire:`n Position equipment away from flammable materials; ensure fire detection and suppression systems are in place and regularly tested. * `nWater:`n Avoid placing equipment directly under water pipes or in areas prone to leaks. c. `nPower Protection:`n All critical equipment shall be connected to Uninterruptible Power Supplies (UPS) and surge protectors to guard against power fluctuations and outages. UPS systems shall be regularly tested. d. `nVisual Protection:`n Equipment displaying sensitive information (e.g., monitors in public view) shall be positioned or equipped with privacy screens to prevent unauthorized viewing. e. `nPhysical Securing:`n Servers and critical network `nequipment shall be physically secured within racks. Laptops issued to employees are equipped with full disk encryption (managed by Intune) to mitigate data breach risk if stolen.`n4.3. Equipment Maintenance`n a. `nAuthorized Personnel:`n Only authorized `nCirque Corporation`n IT personnel or approved third-party vendors shall perform maintenance on IT equipment. b. `nSupervision:`n Third-party maintenance personnel shall be supervised by an `nCirque Corporation`n employee when accessing secure areas or sensitive equipment. c. `nSecurity Controls:`n All existing security controls (e.g., access controls, encryption) shall remain in place and operational during and after maintenance activities. Any temporary disabling of controls must be documented and immediately re-enabled. d. `nLogging:`n Maintenance activities on critical equipment shall be logged, including date, time, personnel involved, and actions taken.`n4.4. Off-Site Equipment and Remote Work`n a. `nCompany-Issued Equipment:`n Employees working remotely shall use only company-issued and managed equipment (e.g., laptops managed by `nIntune`n with FDE and `nWindows Defender for Business`n). b. `nSecure Connectivity:`n Remote access to `nCirque Corporation`n's network and systems must only occur via secure VPN connections. c. `nPhysical Security at Remote Locations:`n Employees are responsible for the physical security of company equipment at remote locations, including: * Keeping equipment in secure locations when not in use. * Not leaving equipment unattended in public places. * Reporting lost or stolen equipment immediately to the IT Manager.`n4.5. Equipment Removal and Transfer`n a. `nAuthorization:`n No IT equipment shall be permanently removed from `nCirque Corporation`n premises or transferred between offices without explicit authorization from the IT Manager. b. `nDocumentation:`n All equipment movements shall be documented in the asset inventory. c. `nSecurity During Transit:`n Sensitive equipment in transit shall be protected appropriately (e.g., secure packaging, tracking, encryption of data if present).`n4.6. Secure Equipment Disposal and Re-use`n a. `nData Sanitization:`n Before disposal, re-assignment, or return to a vendor, all storage media (hard drives, SSDs, USBs, mobile devices) on the equipment must be securely sanitized or destroyed. This includes: * `nConfidential/Internal Use Data:`n Wiping using industry-recognized methods (e.g., NIST SP 800-88 guidelines for media sanitization) or physical destruction (e.g., shredding, degaussing) to render data irretrievable. * `nEndpoint Data:`n For devices managed by `nIntune`n, remote wipe capabilities can be used prior to physical destruction. b. `nPhysical Destruction:`n For equipment no longer functional or deemed beyond secure data sanitization, physical destruction methods may be employed (e.g., shredding, crushing). c. `nCertification:`n `nCirque Corporation`n shall engage certified IT asset disposition (ITAD) vendors for large-scale equipment disposal where appropriate, ensuring chain of custody and data destruction certificates. d. `nAsset Inventory Update:`n The disposal of equipment shall be promptly recorded in the `nInformation Asset Inventory`n.`n5. Clear Desk and Clear Screen`n a. All personnel shall adhere to a "Clear Desk Policy" (as per IS-CIRQ-P-010-G) by securing sensitive documents and removable media in locked drawers/cabinets when not in use or when leaving their workspace. b. All personnel shall adhere to a "Clear Screen Policy" (as per IS-CIRQ-P-010-G) by locking their computer screens when leaving their workstation unattended.`n6. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's IT equipment, physical security infrastructure, or legal/regulatory requirements.`n7. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-010-G: Physical and Environmental Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-011-G: Physical Access Control Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-007-G: Asset Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-008-G: Access Control Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-011-G: Operations Security Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-011-G`nStandards Name: Operations Security Policy`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish `nCirque Corporation`n's requirements for ensuring the secure operation of information processing facilities, information systems, applications, and networks. This policy aligns with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.8, aiming to protect against loss of confidentiality, integrity, and availability of information due to operational failures, unauthorized access, or malicious activity.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors) involved in the operation and maintenance of information systems, applications (e.g., Omnify, Cadence, GitLab, Asana, Microsoft 365, QuickBooks), networks, and information processing facilities across all `nCirque Corporation`n locations (US, Taipei). It covers all operational activities that impact information security.`n3. Principles of Operations Security`nCirque Corporation`n manages its operations security based on the following principles:`nSecure Operation:`n Information systems and applications shall be operated securely, with appropriate controls to prevent and detect security incidents.`nChange Management:`n All changes to information systems and services shall be controlled through a formal change management process.`nBackup and Restoration:`n Information and software shall be regularly backed up and securely stored, with tested restoration capabilities.`nLogging and Monitoring:`n System events and user activities shall be logged and monitored to detect security incidents and operational issues.`nNetwork Security:`n Networks shall be designed, implemented, and managed to protect networked services and information.`nCapacity Management:`n Resources (e.g., storage, processing power, network bandwidth) shall be adequately provisioned and monitored to ensure performance and availability.`nSeparation of Environments:`n Development, testing, and production environments shall be logically and, where appropriate, physically separated.`nMalware Protection:`n Systems shall be protected from malware.`n4. Operations Security Requirements`n4.1. Change Management:`n a. All changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's information systems, applications, networks, and infrastructure (including hardware, software, firmware, and configurations) shall be managed through a formal `nChange Management Procedure`n. b. Changes must be justified, reviewed, tested, approved, implemented, and documented, with rollback plans where necessary. c. Urgent changes required for business continuity or critical security fixes shall follow an expedited, but still documented, process.`n4.2. Backup and Restoration:`n a. Critical information, software, and system configurations shall be regularly backed up in accordance with `nCirque Corporation`n's `nBackup and Restoration Procedure`n. b. Backups shall be stored securely (encrypted, if sensitive data is included) and off-site where appropriate. c. Restoration procedures shall be regularly tested to ensure data integrity and availability.`n4.3. Logging and Monitoring:`n a. System logs, application logs, and user activity logs shall be collected, stored, and protected in accordance with the `nLogging and Monitoring Procedure`n. b. Logs shall be reviewed regularly for security incidents, anomalies, and operational issues. c. Alerts shall be configured for critical security events and system failures.`n4.4. Network Security Management:`n a. Networks shall be segmented to separate sensitive areas (e.g., production, development, corporate, guest networks) and controlled `nthrough firewalls and access controls, as detailed in the `nNetwork Security Management Procedure`n. b. All network devices shall be securely configured and regularly patched. c. Network traffic shall be monitored for suspicious activity.`n4.5. Capacity Management:`n a. The capacity of information processing resources (e.g., servers, storage, network bandwidth) shall be monitored to ensure that current and future business requirements can be met, preventing performance degradation or system failures. b. Capacity planning shall anticipate peak demands and growth.`n4.6. Separation of Development, Test, and Production Environments:`n a. Development, testing, and production environments shall be logically separated to prevent unauthorized changes or accidental interference with operational systems. b. Production data shall not be used in development or test environments unless appropriately sanitized or anonymized. c. Access to these environments shall be controlled based on the principle of least privilege.`n4.7. Malware Protection:`n a. All information systems, including servers, workstations, and mobile devices, shall be protected against malware. b. Anti-malware software (`nWindows Defender for Business`n) shall be installed, regularly updated, and configured to perform scheduled scans. c. Users shall be educated on the risks of malware and safe computing practices.`n4.8. Technical Vulnerability Management:`n a. Information systems and applications shall be regularly assessed for technical vulnerabilities. b. Identified vulnerabilities shall be remediated or mitigated in a timely manner according to their risk level.`n5. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall responsible for the implementation and oversight of this policy and its related procedures. Ensures that IT operations adhere to security requirements.`nSystem Administrators / IT Personnel:`n Responsible for executing operational security tasks in accordance with established procedures.`nAll Personnel:`n Responsible for understanding and adhering to the operational security requirements relevant to their roles.`n6. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if significant changes occur to `nCirque Corporation`n's operational environment, IT systems, or the threat landscape.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-013-G: Change Management Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-013-G`nStandards Name: Change Management Procedure`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define a standardized and controlled process for managing all changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's information systems, applications, networks, and infrastructure. This procedure aims to minimize the risks associated with changes, prevent unauthorized modifications, reduce disruptions to business operations, and ensure that changes are securely implemented and documented, in accordance with IS-CIRQ-P-011-G: Operations Security Policy and ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.8.2.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all planned changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's IT environment that could impact the confidentiality, integrity, or availability of information assets. This includes, but is not limited to:`nHardware installations, upgrades, or decommissioning (e.g., servers, network devices, workstations).`nSoftware installations, upgrades, patches, or configurations (e.g., operating systems, applications like Omnify, Cadence, GitLab, Asana, Microsoft 365, QuickBooks).`nNetwork changes (e.g., firewall rules, routing, VPN configurations).`nDatabase schema changes or upgrades.`nCloud service configurations or changes.`nPhysical infrastructure changes impacting IT (e.g., power, cooling for server rooms).`nChanges to security controls or policies.`n3. Definitions`nChange:`n Any addition, modification, or removal of an authorized, planned, or supported IT service or service component.`nChange Request (CR):`n A formal proposal for a change.`nChange Advisory Board (CAB):`n A group of stakeholders (e.g., IT Manager, Department Managers, relevant technical personnel) responsible for reviewing, evaluating, and approving significant changes.`n4. Responsibilities`nChange Initiator:`n The individual proposing the change.`nChange Approver:`n The individual(s) authorized to approve a change (e.g., IT Manager, CAB).`nIT Manager:`n Overall owner of this procedure, responsible for overseeing the change management process and acting as the primary change approver, or leading the CAB.`nImplementation Team:`n The IT personnel responsible for executing the change.`nAffected Parties:`n Stakeholders (e.g., end-users, department heads) who will be impacted by the change.`n5. Procedure`n5.1. Change Request (CR) Submission`n a. All proposed changes shall be formally submitted via a designated ticketing system (if available) or an email to the IT Manager, containing the following information: * Unique Change Request (CR) ID. * Date of request. * Change Initiator. * Detailed description of the proposed change. * Business justification for the change. * Expected impact of the change (e.g., services affected, users impacted, downtime). * Risk assessment (potential security implications, operational risks). * Resources required (personnel, time, budget). * Proposed implementation date/time. * Back-out plan (rollback procedure in case of failure). * Test plan (how the change will be validated). * Affected parties to be notified.`n5.2. Change Review and Assessment`n a. The IT Manager (or designated IT personnel) reviews the CR for completeness and initial feasibility. b. `nImpact Analysis:`n A detailed impact analysis is performed to understand the potential effects on systems, services, and security. This includes reviewing security implications to ensure the change does not introduce new vulnerabilities or weaken existing controls. c. `nRisk Assessment:`n The risks associated with the change are formally assessed (referencing the IS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register). d. `nTechnical Review:`n Technical personnel review the proposed change for technical soundness and adherence to existing architecture/standards.`n5.3. Change Approval`n a. `nMinor Changes (Low Risk/Impact):`n Changes deemed low risk and low impact (e.g., routine software updates, minor configuration changes) may be approved directly by the IT Manager. b. `nMajor Changes (Medium/High Risk/Impact):`n Changes with medium to high risk or significant business impact (e.g., core system upgrades, network reconfigurations, changes affecting sensitive data like CAD, ASIC designs, or customer data) shall require approval from a `nChange Advisory Board (CAB)`n, which includes the IT Manager and representatives from affected business units or executive management as needed. c. `nDocumentation:`n All approvals (or rejections) shall be documented in the CR.`n5.4. Change Implementation`n a. `nScheduling:`n Approved changes are scheduled in a maintenance window to minimize disruption. b. `nPre-Implementation Checks:`n Before implementation, all necessary preparations (e.g., backups, verification of pre-requisites) are completed. c. `nImplementation:`n The change is implemented according to the approved plan. All steps are documented. d. `nBack-out Plan:`n If issues arise during implementation, the back-out plan is initiated to restore the system to its previous state. e. `nMonitoring:`n Systems are actively monitored during and after the change for any adverse effects.`n5.5. Post-Implementation Review (PIR)`n a. `nVerification:`n After the change is implemented, the change initiator and/or a designated reviewer verify that the change achieved its intended outcome without unintended side effects. b. `nTesting:`n The test plan is executed to confirm functionality and security. c. `nDocumentation Update:`n All relevant documentation (e.g., system configurations, network diagrams, procedures) is updated to reflect the change. d. `nClosure:`n The Change Request is formally closed once the change is verified and documentation is updated.`n5.6. Emergency Changes`n a. In situations requiring immediate action to resolve critical incidents (e.g., security breach, major system outage) where standard change procedures cannot be followed, an `nEmergency Change`n may be initiated. b. `nAuthorization:`n Emergency changes must be verbally approved by the IT Manager, or in their absence, a designated alternate, before implementation. c. `nDocumentation (Post-Facto):`n All emergency changes `nmust be fully documented retroactively, immediately after the critical situation is resolved. This documentation must include the reason for the emergency, the actions taken, the impact, and post-implementation verification. d. `nReview:`n All emergency changes will be reviewed by the IT Manager and/or CAB to identify lessons learned and improve future processes.`n6. Tools`nTicketing system (if available) for managing Change Requests.`nVersion control systems (`nGitLab`n for code and configuration files).`nConfiguration management tools (`nIntune`n for endpoints).`n7. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's IT environment, operational processes, or identified areas for improvement in change management.`n8. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-011-G: Operations Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure (for monitoring changes`nIS-CIRQ-PR-014-G: Backup and Restoration Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-014-G`nStandards Name: Backup and Restoration Procedure`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the systematic process for performing, managing, and testing backups and restoration of `nCirque Corporation`n's critical information and software. This procedure aims to ensure the availability and integrity of information assets, facilitate recovery from data loss events (e.g., hardware failure, human error, cyber-attack), and comply with IS-CIRQ-P-011-G: Operations Security Policy and ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.8.13.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all critical information, software, and system configurations identified as requiring backup within `nCirque Corporation`n's IT environment. This explicitly includes:`nServers:`n All production servers.`nFile Server:`n The central file server where critical company data is stored.`nThis procedure does NOT cover backup of individual user laptops or workstations, which are considered end-user responsibility for personal files, though company data on them should reside on backed-up network drives or cloud services.`n3. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall owner of this procedure. Responsible for defining backup strategies, ensuring backup systems are properly configured and monitored, and overseeing restoration tests.`nSystem Administrators / IT Personnel:`n Responsible for executing backup jobs, monitoring backup success/failure, performing restoration tests, and managing backup media.`nDepartment Managers/Asset Owners:`n Responsible for identifying critical data and systems within their purview that require inclusion in backup schedules.`nAll Personnel:`n Responsible for saving critical work-related data to designated network drives or approved cloud storage that is covered by this backup procedure.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Backup Strategy and Scope`na. **Critical Data Identification:** Department Managers and Asset Owners, in conjunction with the IT Manager, shall identify and classify critical data and systems requiring backup based on their value, criticality, and impact of loss (refer to `IS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure`).`nb. **Backup Scope:** Backups shall cover all data and configurations on:`n    * **All production servers.**`n    * **The central file server.**`nc. **Exclusions:** Individual user laptops are excluded from this centralized backup procedure. Users are responsible for ensuring critical company data is stored on network drives or approved cloud services (e.g., SharePoint, GitLab) which are included in backups.`nd. **Backup Tool:** **Veeam** is the designated software for backing up servers and the file server.`ne. **Retention Periods:** Backup retention periods shall be defined based on data classification, regulatory requirements, and recovery time objectives (RTO) / recovery point objectives (RPO).`n4.2. Backup Execution`na. **Scheduling:** Backup jobs shall be scheduled to run automatically using **Veeam**, typically during off-peak hours to minimize impact on operational systems.`nb. **Types of Backups:** A combination of full, incremental, or differential backups will be utilized as appropriate to meet RPO/RTO requirements and optimize storage.`nc. **Media Management:**`n    * Backups shall be stored on designated backup media (e.g., network attached storage (NAS), cloud storage).`n    * Critical backups shall be replicated to an off-site location or cloud storage for disaster recovery purposes.`n    * Backup media containing sensitive data shall be encrypted.`nd. **Monitoring:**`n    * System Administrators shall regularly monitor **Veeam** for backup job success or failure.`n    * Automated alerts for backup failures shall be configured and addressed promptly.`n4.3. Data Integrity and Security of Backups`na. **Encryption:** All backups containing sensitive or confidential information shall be encrypted both in transit and at rest.`nb. **Access Control:** Access to backup systems, backup software (**Veeam**), and backup media shall be strictly controlled on a need-to-know basis, following `IS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy` and `IS-CIRQ-PR-009-G: Privileged Access Management Procedure`.`nc. **Integrity Checks:** Regular integrity checks on backup sets shall be performed to ensure data can be successfully restored.`n4.4. Restoration Process`na. **Restoration Request:** In the event of data loss or system failure, restoration requests are submitted to the IT Manager or designated System Administrator.`nb. **Prioritization:** Restoration efforts shall be prioritized based on the criticality of the system/data and impact on business operations.`nc. **Restoration Execution:**`n    * System Administrators shall use **Veeam** to restore data or systems from the appropriate backup set.`n    * Restoration steps shall be documented.`nd. **Verification:** After restoration, the integrity and functionality of the restored data/system shall be verified by the IT team and confirmed by the requesting party.`n4.5. Backup and Restoration Testing`na. **Regular Testing:** Restoration procedures shall be regularly tested at least annually, or more frequently for highly critical systems.`nb. **Test Scope:** Tests shall involve restoring a representative sample of data and/or systems from backup media to a non-production environment.`nc. **Documentation:** Test results, including any issues encountered and resolutions, shall be documented.`nd. **Review and Improvement:** Test failures shall trigger a review of backup strategies and procedures to implement necessary improvements.`n4.6. Secure Disposal of Backup Media`na. When backup media is no longer required or has reached the end of its lifecycle, it shall be securely disposed of in accordance with `IS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure` to prevent unauthorized data recovery.`n5. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's IT infrastructure, data classification, backup tools (`nVeeam`n), or storage locations.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-011-G: Operations Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-007-G: Asset Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-009-G: Cryptography Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-010-G: Key Management Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-015-G`nStandards Name: Logging and Monitoring Procedure`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the systematic process for logging, storing, and reviewing events related to `nCirque Corporation`n's information systems, applications, and networks. This procedure ensures that sufficient information is available for audit trails, incident investigation, problem diagnosis, and compliance monitoring, in accordance with IS-CIRQ-P-011-G: Operations Security Policy and ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.8.14.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n information systems, applications, network devices, cloud services, and physical access systems that generate logs of security-relevant events, operational activities, or user actions. This includes, but is not limited to:`nOperating Systems:`n Windows Servers, workstations (managed by `nIntune`n).`nNetwork Devices:`n Firewalls, switches, wireless access points.`nApplications:`n Omnify, Cadence, GitLab, Asana, QuickBooks, Microsoft 365 services (e.g., SharePoint, Exchange Online).`nDirectory Services:`n Active Directory.`nSecurity Tools:`n `nWindows Defender for Business`n.`nPhysical Access Control Systems:`n `nUnifi Access system`n.`nCloud Infrastructure:`n Azure logs.`n3. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall owner of this procedure. Responsible for defining logging requirements, ensuring logging systems are properly configured, and overseeing log review and incident response processes related to logs.`nSystem Administrators / IT Personnel:`n Responsible for configuring systems to generate appropriate logs, ensuring logs are collected and stored securely, and performing initial log reviews and investigations.`nAll Personnel:`n Responsible for reporting suspicious activities or anomalies observed on systems they use.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Identification of Logging Requirements`n a. The IT Manager, in conjunction with system owners, shall identify and define the types of events that must be logged for each system, based on: * Business criticality of the system. * Data classification (Confidential, Internal Use). * Legal, regulatory, or contractual requirements. * Risk assessments (IS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register). b. Key events to be logged include, but are not limited to: * All successful and failed logon attempts. * Changes to system configuration, security settings, or access rights. * Use of privileged accounts. * Attempts to access unauthorized resources. * Execution of critical system commands. * Malware detection and quarantine events. * Physical access attempts (successful and failed) via `nUnifi Access system`n.`n4.2. Log Generation and Collection`n a. `nSystem Configuration:`n All relevant systems shall be configured to generate necessary logs with sufficient detail. b. `nCentralized Logging (where applicable):`n Logs from critical systems (e.g., servers, network devices, firewalls) should be forwarded to a centralized log management system or Security Information and Event Management (SIEM) solution (if implemented) to facilitate aggregation, correlation, and analysis. c. `nEndpoint Logging:`n For workstations and laptops, relevant logs (e.g., security events, `nWindows Defender for Business`n alerts) are collected and monitored via `nIntune`n and integrated with Microsoft 365 security features. d. `nCloud Service Logging:`n `nCirque Corporation`n shall utilize and configure the native logging capabilities of cloud services (e.g., Azure Activity Logs, Microsoft 365 Unified Audit Log, GitLab audit logs) to capture relevant events.`n4.3. Log Storage and Retention`n a. `nSecure Storage:`n Logs shall be stored securely to prevent unauthorized access, modification, or deletion. Access to log storage shall be strictly controlled based on need-to-know. b. `nIntegrity:`n Mechanisms shall be in place to ensure the integrity and authenticity of logs (e.g., hashing, read-only access for archived logs). c. `nRetention Periods:`n Logs shall be retained for a period consistent with legal, regulatory, and business requirements. The default retention period for security-relevant logs is [X] days/months/years (e.g., 90 days for operational, 1 year for security incidents, 7 years for compliance). d. `nTime Synchronization:`n All systems shall have their time synchronized to a central, reliable time source (e.g., NTP server) to ensure accurate timestamps in logs for correlation purposes.`n4.4. Log Review and Analysis`n a. `nRegular Review:`n Logs shall be reviewed regularly for security events, operational issues, and anomalies: * `nDaily:`n Critical system logs (e.g., firewall, server authentication logs), privileged access logs. * `nWeekly/Monthly:`n General system logs, application logs, physical access logs from `nUnifi Access system`n. * `nAd-hoc:`n In response to alerts, suspected incidents, or during investigations. b. `nAutomated Monitoring and Alerts:`n * Automated monitoring tools shall be configured to identify and alert on pre-defined critical security events (e.g., multiple failed logins, unauthorized access attempts, malware outbreaks detected by `nWindows Defender for Business`n, significant changes in `nActive Directory`n). * Alerts shall be escalated to the IT Manager or designated personnel for immediate investigation. c. `nIncident Response Integration:`n Log analysis is a critical component of the Incident Response process (IS-CIRQ-PR-XXX-G: Incident Response Procedure - future document).`n4.5. Protection of Logging Facilities`n a. Logging facilities and log data shall be protected from tampering and unauthorized access. b. Only authorized personnel shall have access to configure, review, or modify logging systems.`n5. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's IT environment, logging tools, or legal/regulatory requirements.`n6.
