Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-011-G: Operations Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-009-G: Privileged Access Management Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-010-G: Physical and Environmental Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-011-G: Physical Access Control Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-016-G: Network Security Management Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-016-G`nStandards Name: Network Security Management Procedure`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the systematic process for managing the security of `nCirque Corporation`n's networks, including wired, wireless, and remote access connections. This procedure aims to protect network services and information from unauthorized access, misuse, disclosure, or disruption, in accordance with IS-CIRQ-P-011-G: Operations Security Policy and ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.8.19.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n network infrastructure, devices, and services across all locations, including the US, Japan, and China offices, and remote access points. This includes, but is not limited to:`nNetwork devices (routers, switches, firewalls, wireless access points).`nNetwork services (DNS, DHCP).`nNetwork protocols.`nNetwork cabling.`nVirtual Private Network (VPN) solutions.`nCloud network configurations (e.g., Azure VNETs).`n3. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall owner of this procedure. Responsible for network architecture, security design, approval of network changes, and oversight of network security monitoring.`nSystem Administrators / IT Personnel:`n Responsible for implementing, configuring, maintaining, and monitoring network devices and services in accordance with this procedure.`nAll Personnel:`n Responsible for adhering to network usage policies, especially concerning remote access.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Network Segmentation and Architecture`n a. `nCirque Corporation`n networks shall be logically segmented to isolate different functional areas, sensitive data, and system classifications (e.g., corporate network, production network, development network, guest wireless). b. Network segmentation shall be enforced using firewalls and VLANs (Virtual Local Area Networks). c. Network diagrams, illustrating segmentation and key security zones, shall be maintained and regularly updated.`n4.2. Firewall Management`n a. `nFirewall Deployment:`n Firewalls shall be deployed at critical network boundaries, including the perimeter between `nCirque Corporation`n's internal network and external networks (e.g., the Internet) and between network segments. b. `nRule Management:`n * Firewall rules shall be explicitly defined, approved by the IT Manager, and based on a least-privilege principle (deny-all by default, permit-by-exception). * Rules shall be documented, including justification, source/destination IP, port, and protocol. * Rules shall be regularly reviewed (at least quarterly) and unnecessary rules removed. c. `nSecure Configuration:`n Firewalls shall be securely configured, with default passwords changed, unnecessary services disabled, and management interfaces protected. d. `nLogging:`n Firewall logs shall be enabled and forwarded to a centralized logging system as per IS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure.`n4.3. Secure Network Device Configuration`n a. `nHardening:`n All network devices (routers, switches, wireless access points) shall be hardened according to industry best practices and vendor recommendations. This includes: * Changing all default passwords. * Disabling unnecessary services and ports. * Implementing strong authentication and authorization for device management. * Restricting management access to designated administrative networks or hosts. b. `nPatch Management:`n Network device firmware and operating systems `nshall be regularly updated with security patches. c. `nConfiguration Backup:`n Device configurations shall be regularly backed up and stored securely.`n4.4. Wireless Network Security`n a. `nStrong Encryption:`n Wireless networks used for corporate access shall utilize strong encryption protocols (e.g., WPA2 Enterprise or WPA3) with EAP-TLS or PEAP for user authentication. b. `nSeparate Guest Network:`n A separate, isolated guest wireless network shall be provided for visitors, ensuring it has no access to `nCirque Corporation`n's internal corporate or production networks. c. `nAuthentication:`n All wireless access points shall require strong authentication.`n4.5. Remote Access Security (VPN)`n a. `nMandatory VPN:`n All remote access to `nCirque Corporation`n's internal networks and resources shall be conducted exclusively through an approved Virtual Private Network (VPN) solution. b. `nStrong Encryption:`n The VPN solution shall use strong cryptographic protocols (e.g., IPsec, OpenVPN, or TLS VPN) for data in transit encryption as per IS-CIRQ-P-009-G: Cryptography Policy. c. `nMulti-Factor Authentication (MFA):`n MFA shall be mandatory for all VPN connections. d. `nDevice Compliance:`n Only company-managed devices (e.g., laptops managed by `nIntune`n) with current security software (`nWindows Defender for Business`n) and up-to-date patches shall be permitted to connect via VPN.`n4.6. Network Monitoring`n a. `nTraffic Analysis:`n Network traffic shall be monitored for suspicious activity, intrusions, and anomalies using network intrusion detection/prevention systems (IDS/IPS) or similar tools. b. `nBandwidth Monitoring:`n Network bandwidth usage shall be monitored to ensure adequate capacity and detect unusual traffic patterns. c. `nAlerting:`n Alerts shall be configured for critical network security events and performance issues.`n4.7. Network Service Security`n a. `nDNS Security:`n DNS services shall be configured securely, and internal DNS servers protected from external access. b. `nDHCP Security:`n DHCP services shall be managed to prevent unauthorized IP address assignment. c. `nTime Synchronization:`n Network devices shall be synchronized to a central, reliable time source (NTP) to ensure accurate logging.`n4.8. Segregation of Duties for Network Administration`n a. Where feasible, duties related to network administration (e.g., firewall rule changes, router configuration) shall be segregated to prevent a single individual from being able to compromise network security without detection.`n5. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's network architecture, security technologies, or the threat landscape.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-011-G: Operations Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-008-G: Access Control Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-009-G: Cryptography Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-013-G: Change Management Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-012-G: Secure System Acquisition, Development and Maintenance Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-012-G`nStandards Name: Secure System Acquisition, Development and Maintenance Policy`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish `nCirque Corporation`n's requirements for incorporating information security into the entire lifecycle of its information systems and applications, from acquisition and development through operation and maintenance. This policy aims to minimize security vulnerabilities in systems and software, protect intellectual property (e.g., ASIC designs, firmware, source code), and ensure that security is an integral part of all system-related activities, in accordance with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.8.25, A.8.28, and A.8.29.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors) involved in the acquisition, development, testing, implementation, and maintenance of all information systems, software applications (including in-house developed, commercial off-the-shelf - COTS, and cloud-based applications), and infrastructure components. This includes specific applications like `nCadence`n (for ASIC design), `nGitLab`n (for source code management), `nOmnify`n, `nAsana`n, `nQuickBooks`n, and `nMicrosoft 365 services`n.`n3. Principles of Secure System Lifecycle`nCirque Corporation`n integrates security into its system lifecycle based on the following principles:`nSecurity by Design:`n Security considerations shall be embedded at every stage of the system lifecycle, from initial concept and requirements gathering through design, development, testing, deployment, and decommissioning.`nSecure Development Practices:`n Development practices shall adhere to secure coding standards and principles to minimize vulnerabilities in software and systems.`nTesting and Validation:`n All systems and software shall undergo thorough security testing before deployment or significant changes to identify and remediate vulnerabilities.`nSupply Chain Security:`n Security requirements shall be specified and enforced for third-party acquired software, services, and components.`nContinuous Improvement:`n Security processes related to system acquisition, development, and maintenance shall be continuously reviewed and improved.`n4. Secure System Acquisition and Development Requirements`n4.1. Information Security Requirements Specification:`n a. Information security requirements shall be identified and documented at the initial stages of any new system acquisition or development project. b. These requirements shall address confidentiality, integrity, availability, accountability, and compliance obligations. c. Specific security requirements shall be defined for sensitive IP such as ASIC designs and firmware developed using tools like `nCadence`n and managed in `nGitLab`n.`n4.2. Secure Development Environment:`n a. Development environments shall be logically and, where necessary, physically separated from production environments. b. Access to development and testing environments shall be strictly controlled, based on the principle of least privilege. c. Production data shall not be used in development or test environments unless it has been adequately anonymized or sanitized. d. Development tools (e.g., `nGitLab`n, `nCadence`n) shall be securely configured and regularly updated.`n4.3. Secure Coding and Development Practices:`n a. Developers shall adhere to secure coding standards and guidelines (e.g., OWASP Top 10 for web applications, specific guidelines for hardware/firmware development). b. Code reviews shall incorporate security vulnerability checks. c. Use of open-source software and third-party components shall be managed to assess and mitigate associated security risks.`n4.4. Security in Development Processes (e.g., GitLab):`n a. All source code, including firmware and ASIC design files, managed in `nGitLab`n shall be subject to version control. b. Access to sensitive repositories in `nGitLab`n shall be restricted to authorized developers on a need-to-know basis. c. Automated security scanning tools (e.g., Static Application Security Testing - SAST, Dynamic Application Security Testing - DAST) shall be integrated into the Continuous Integration/Continuous Delivery (CI/CD) pipeline where applicable.`n4.5. Acquired Software Security:`n a. Prior to acquiring or adopting commercial off-the-shelf (COTS) software or cloud services, security due diligence shall be performed to assess inherent risks and vendor security posture. b. Contractual agreements with software vendors and cloud service providers shall include clear information security clauses. c. Default security settings of acquired software shall be hardened before deployment.`n5. System Testing and Acceptance Requirements`n5.1. Security Testing:`n a. All new systems, applications, and significant changes shall undergo comprehensive security testing before being moved to production. This includes: * Vulnerability scanning. * Penetration testing (internal and/or external) for critical systems. * Code reviews. * Functional security testing. b. Testing shall verify that all identified security requirements are met and that no new vulnerabilities have been introduced.`n5.2. Acceptance Criteria:`n a. Systems and applications shall only be accepted into the production environment after demonstrating that they meet predefined security requirements and have successfully passed all security tests. b. Any identified vulnerabilities must be remediated or formally accepted with a clear mitigation plan.`n6. System Maintenance and Support`n6.1. Vulnerability Management:`n a. A systematic process shall be in place to identify, assess, and remediate technical vulnerabilities in all operational systems and applications. b. Regular vulnerability scans and penetration tests shall be performed (as per IS-CIRQ-PR-XXX-G: Vulnerability Management Procedure - future document). c. Patches and updates shall be applied in a timely manner as per the IS-CIRQ-PR-013-G: Change Management Procedure.`n6.2. System Monitoring:`n a. Operational systems shall be continuously monitored for security events, anomalies, and potential intrusions, as per IS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure.`n6.3. Information System Audit Controls:`n a. Audit logs shall be maintained to record system activities and user actions, facilitating accountability and incident investigation.`n7. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall responsible for the implementation and enforcement of this policy. Oversees the security of development environments and processes.`nDevelopment Teams:`n Responsible for implementing secure coding practices, conducting security reviews, and ensuring security requirements are met during development.`nProject Managers:`n Responsible for ensuring security is integrated into project plans and budgets for system acquisition and development.`nSystem Administrators:`n Responsible for securely configuring, maintaining, and patching operational systems.`n8. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-PR-017-G: Secure Development Procedure (To be drafted next)`nIS-CIRQ-PR-018-G: System Testing and Acceptance Procedure (To be drafted next)`nIS-CIRQ-P-011-G: Operations Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-013-G: Change Management Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-007-G: Asset Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-009-G: Cryptography Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy`n9. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if significant changes occur to `nCirque Corporation`n's system development methodologies, technology stack, or the threat landscape.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-017-G: Secure Development Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-017-G`nStandards Name: Secure Development Procedure`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the systematic process for integrating information security into the software development lifecycle (SDLC), including the development of firmware and ASIC designs. This procedure aims to minimize vulnerabilities, ensure the confidentiality, integrity, and availability of developed systems and intellectual property (IP), and comply with IS-CIRQ-P-012-G: Secure System Acquisition, Development and Maintenance Policy and ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.8.25.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors) involved in the design, development, coding, and integration of software, firmware, and hardware designs (e.g., ASIC designs). This includes internal development using tools like `nGitLab`n and `nCadence`n, as well as customization or integration of third-party applications.`n3. Responsibilities`nDevelopment Managers/Leads:`n Responsible for ensuring their teams adhere to this procedure and secure coding practices.`nDevelopers/Engineers:`n Responsible for implementing secure coding, design, and testing practices as defined herein.`nIT Manager/Security Personnel:`n Responsible for providing security guidance, tools, and reviewing security vulnerabilities during development.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Secure Development Environment`n a. `nSeparation:`n Development, testing, and production environments shall be logically and, where technically feasible, physically separated. Access between environments shall be strictly controlled. b. `nAccess Control:`n Access to development environments, including source code repositories (e.g., `nGitLab`n), design tools (e.g., `nCadence`n), and associated data, shall be restricted to authorized personnel on a need-to-know basis. c. `nWorkstation Hardening:`n Development workstations shall be securely configured and regularly patched, equipped with anti-malware software (`nWindows Defender for Business`n), and protected by encryption where applicable (e.g., Full Disk Encryption via `nIntune`n). d. `nProduction Data:`n Use of actual production data in development or test environments is strictly prohibited unless it has been adequately anonymized, pseudonymized, or sanitized to remove sensitive information.`n4.2. Secure Design and Requirements`n a. `nSecurity Requirements Gathering:`n For every new system or significant feature, information security requirements shall be defined at the design phase. This includes: * Identification of sensitive data processed or stored. * Required authentication and authorization mechanisms. * Input validation and output encoding needs. * Error handling strategies. * Resilience and availability considerations. * Protection of intellectual property (e.g., ASIC/firmware design integrity). b. `nThreat Modeling:`n Threat modeling or security design reviews shall be conducted for critical systems to identify potential vulnerabilities early in the design phase.`n4.3. Secure Coding Practices (Software and Firmware)`n a. `nCoding Standards:`n Developers shall adhere to secure coding guidelines and best practices relevant to the programming language and platform being used (e.g., OWASP Top 10 for web applications, MISRA C/C++ for firmware). b. `nInput Validation:`n All user input and data from untrusted sources shall be rigorously validated to prevent injection attacks (e.g., SQL injection, command injection) and buffer overflows. c. `nError Handling:`n Applications shall handle errors gracefully without revealing sensitive information or internal system details. d. `nSecure Configuration:`n Code shall be designed to operate with secure default configurations and minimize reliance on insecure settings. e. `nLogging:`n Relevant security events and application errors shall be logged appropriately as per IS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure.`n4.4. Secure Development Practices (ASIC/Hardware Design via Cadence)`n a. `nIP Protection:`n Rigorous access controls and versioning shall be applied to all `nCadence`n `ndesign files and related IP in `nGitLab`n. b. `nSecure Design Principles:`n Security considerations (e.g., side-channel attack resistance, secure boot, tamper detection) shall be incorporated into the ASIC design process where applicable. c. `nVerification:`n Design verification methodologies shall include checks for security vulnerabilities at the hardware level.`n4.5. Version Control and Code Management (GitLab)`n a. All source code, firmware, and design files (e.g., `nCadence`n files) shall be managed in `nGitLab`n using appropriate version control practices. b. `nBranching Strategy:`n A secure branching strategy shall be enforced (e.g., protected branches for master/main, requiring pull requests and code reviews for merges). c. `nAccess Control:`n Access to `nGitLab`n repositories shall be managed on a need-to-know basis, with granular permissions. d. `nCommit Hygiene:`n Developers shall be encouraged to write clear and concise commit messages, avoiding sensitive information.`n4.6. Code Review and Peer Review`n a. All code (software, firmware, and significant hardware design changes) shall undergo peer review or formal code review before being merged into main branches or deployed. b. Code reviews shall include a focus on identifying security vulnerabilities, adherence to coding standards, and correct implementation of security requirements.`n4.7. Use of Third-Party Components and Libraries`n a. `nVulnerability Scanning:`n Where applicable, third-party libraries and open-source components used in development shall be scanned for known vulnerabilities. b. `nLicensing:`n Ensure compliance with licensing requirements for all external components. c. `nUpdates:`n Components shall be regularly updated to address security vulnerabilities.`n4.8. Static Application Security Testing (SAST)`n a. SAST tools shall be integrated into the development pipeline (e.g., in `nGitLab CI/CD`n) to automatically scan source code for common vulnerabilities. b. Identified issues shall be addressed and remediated by developers according to severity.`n5. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's development tools (e.g., `nGitLab`n, `nCadence`n), methodologies, or emerging security threats in software/hardware development.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-012-G: Secure System Acquisition, Development and Maintenance Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-013-G: Change Management Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-018-G: System Testing and Acceptance Procedure (To be drafted next)`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-018-G: System Testing and Acceptance Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-018-G`nStandards Name: System Testing and Acceptance Procedure`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the systematic process for testing and formally accepting information systems, applications, firmware, and hardware designs before their deployment into the production environment. This procedure ensures that systems meet predefined functional, performance, and, critically, security requirements, thereby minimizing risks to `nCirque Corporation`n's information assets, in accordance with IS-CIRQ-P-012-G: Secure System Acquisition, Development and Maintenance Policy and ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.8.29.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all new information systems, applications (including commercial off-the-shelf - COTS, in-house developed, or customized), significant upgrades, major configuration changes, and new firmware or hardware designs (e.g., ASIC designs developed with `nCadence`n) prior to their release or deployment into `nCirque Corporation`n's production environment.`n3. Definitions`nUser Acceptance Testing (UAT):`n Testing performed by end-users or business representatives to verify that the system meets business requirements.`nSecurity Testing:`n Testing specifically focused on identifying vulnerabilities and verifying the effectiveness of security controls.`nVulnerability Scan:`n An automated test to identify known vulnerabilities in systems, applications, or networks.`nPenetration Test:`n A simulated attack against a system, application, or network to evaluate its security.`n4. Responsibilities`nProject Manager/System Owner:`n Overall responsible for ensuring that systems undergo appropriate testing and meet acceptance criteria.`nDevelopment Teams:`n Responsible for developing testable code/designs, addressing identified issues, and supporting testing activities.`nIT Manager/Security Personnel:`n Responsible for defining security testing requirements, overseeing security tests, and evaluating security test results.`nEnd-Users/Business Owners:`n Responsible for participating in UAT and providing feedback on system functionality.`n5. Procedure`n5.1. Test Environment Preparation`n a. A dedicated test environment, logically and (where practical) physically separated from the production environment, shall be prepared for testing. b. The test environment shall replicate the production environment as closely as possible in terms of hardware, software, and network configuration. c. Test data shall be used, which must not contain actual sensitive production information unless adequately anonymized or sanitized. d. Access to the test environment shall be restricted to authorized testing and development personnel.`n5.2. Test Planning`n a. A comprehensive test plan shall be developed, outlining: * Test objectives (functional, performance, security). * Test cases and scenarios. * Roles and responsibilities for testing. * Required test data. * Test schedule. * Criteria for successful completion of tests. * Procedures for documenting and tracking defects. b. `nSecurity Test Plan:`n A specific section of the test plan shall address security testing, including: * Vulnerability scanning targets and scope. * Specific security features to be tested (e.g., authentication, authorization, input validation, encryption). * Types of security tests to be conducted (e.g., code review, SAST, DAST, penetration testing).`n5.3. Test Execution`n5.3.1. Functional and Integration Testing:`n a. Development teams shall conduct unit, integration, and system testing to ensure the system functions as designed and integrates correctly with other systems.`n5.3.2. Security Testing:`n a. `nVulnerability Scanning:`n Systems and applications shall undergo automated vulnerability scanning to identify common security weaknesses. * For in-house developed software, SAST tools integrated with `nGitLab CI/CD`n (as per IS-CIRQ-PR-017-G) should be leveraged. * For web applications, DAST tools should be utilized. b. `nPenetration Testing:`n Critical systems (e.g., those handling sensitive data, external-facing applications, core infrastructure) shall undergo internal and/or external penetration testing by qualified personnel (internal or third-party). c. `nConfiguration Review:`n Security configurations of the system, including operating system, database, and application settings, shall be reviewed against hardening baselines. d. `nAccess Control Testing:`n Verification that access controls function as intended, enforcing the principle of least privilege. e. `nCryptography Testing:`n If cryptographic controls are implemented, verify their correct usage and strength (e.g., certificate validity, algorithm strength). f. `nASIC/Firmware Security Testing (for Cadence designs):`n Specific tests for hardware vulnerabilities (e.g., side-channel analysis, fault injection) shall be conducted where applicable, alongside firmware integrity checks.`n5.3.3. User Acceptance Testing (UAT):`n a. End-users and business owners shall conduct UAT to ensure the system meets business requirements and is suitable for operational use.`n5.4. Defect Management and Remediation`n a. All identified defects and vulnerabilities shall be formally documented, tracked, and prioritized based on their severity and impact. b. Critical and high-severity security vulnerabilities must be remediated promptly before deployment. c. Remediation actions shall be re-tested to confirm effectiveness.`n5.5. System Acceptance`n a. A system can only be accepted for deployment into the production environment if: * All critical and high-severity defects and vulnerabilities have been remediated or formally accepted by the IT Manager and relevant business owner with an approved mitigation plan and residual risk acceptance. * All security requirements outlined in the design phase have been met and verified by security testing. * UAT has been successfully completed, and business owners approve the system for release. b. Formal sign-off for acceptance shall be obtained from the IT Manager and relevant business owner(s).`n5.6. Documentation and Handover`n a. All testing results, defect logs, and acceptance documents shall be archived. b. Updated system documentation, including security `nconfigurations, operational procedures, and user manuals, shall be prepared and handed over to the operations team (refer to IS-CIRQ-PR-006-G: Document Control Procedure).`n6. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's system development lifecycle, testing tools, or regulatory requirements.`n7. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-012-G: Secure System Acquisition, Development and Maintenance Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-017-G: Secure Development Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-011-G: Operations Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-013-G: Change Management Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register`nIS-CIRQ-PR-006-G: Document Control Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-013-G: Supplier Relationships Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-013-G`nStandards Name: Supplier Relationships Policy`nCategory: Related to Outsourcing`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish `nCirque Corporation`n's requirements for managing information security risks associated with supplier relationships. This includes ensuring that the security of `nCirque Corporation`n's information and information systems is protected when suppliers have access to them, or when `nCirque Corporation`n's processes are outsourced to suppliers. This policy aligns with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.15, ensuring consistent information security controls across the supply chain.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors) involved in selecting, engaging, managing, and terminating relationships with suppliers who:`nHave access to `nCirque Corporation`n's information, information systems, or facilities.`nProcess, store, or transmit `nCirque Corporation`n's information on its behalf.`nProvide IT products, components, or services that are critical to `nCirque Corporation`n's information systems or business operations.`nThis includes, but is not limited to, cloud service providers (e.g., Microsoft 365, Azure), software vendors (e.g., Omnify, Cadence, GitLab, Asana, QuickBooks), IT service providers, `nand any other third party with a significant impact on `nCirque Corporation`n's information security.`n3. Principles of Supplier Relationship Security`nCirque Corporation`n manages supplier relationships with information security in mind, based on the following principles:`nRisk-Based Approach:`n Supplier relationships shall be managed based on the level of information security risk they pose to `nCirque Corporation`n.`nDue Diligence:`n Appropriate due diligence shall be performed on potential suppliers before engagement.`nContractual Obligations:`n Information security requirements shall be clearly defined and legally enforced through contractual agreements.`nMonitoring and Review:`n Supplier compliance with information security requirements shall be regularly monitored and reviewed.`nLifecycle Management:`n Information security shall be considered throughout the entire supplier relationship lifecycle, from selection to termination.`n4. Requirements for Supplier Relationship Security`n4.1. Supplier Selection and Assessment:`n a. Before engaging a new supplier, especially those with access to sensitive information or critical systems, a comprehensive security assessment shall be conducted to evaluate their information security posture. b. The level of assessment shall be proportionate to the risk posed by the supplier and the sensitivity of the information involved. This assessment is detailed in IS-CIRQ-PR-019-G: Supplier Security Review Procedure. c. Due diligence may include, but is not limited to, reviewing security certifications (e.g., ISO 27001, SOC 2), security policies, incident response plans, and conducting security questionnaires or audits.`n4.2. Contractual Agreements:`n a. All contracts with suppliers handling `nCirque Corporation`n's information or providing critical services shall include explicit information security clauses. b. These clauses shall cover, at minimum: * Confidentiality and data protection requirements. * Roles and responsibilities for information security. * Compliance with applicable laws and regulations (e.g., data privacy laws). * Incident management and reporting obligations. * Right to audit clauses (where appropriate). * Security breach notification requirements. * Data retention and disposal requirements upon contract termination. * Requirements for subcontracting (if applicable). c. Legal review of such contracts is mandatory.`n4.3. Information Sharing and Access Control:`n a. Information shared with suppliers shall be limited to the minimum necessary for the fulfillment of the contract (principle of least privilege). b. Access to `nCirque Corporation`n's information systems and facilities granted to suppliers shall be strictly controlled and monitored, with access rights provisioned and de-provisioned promptly. c. Remote access by suppliers shall be secured using strong cryptographic controls (e.g., VPNs with MFA) as per IS-CIRQ-P-009-G: Cryptography Policy.`n4.4. Monitoring and Review of Supplier Performance:`n a. `nCirque Corporation`n shall regularly monitor and review suppliers' adherence to contractual information security requirements. b. The frequency and depth of monitoring shall be based on the risk level of the supplier and the criticality of the services or data they handle. c. Monitoring activities may include: * Review of supplier's security reports or audit results. * Periodic security questionnaires. * Performance reviews with security as a key metric. * Audits or inspections by `nCirque Corporation`n (where contractually permitted).`n4.5. Managing Changes to Supplier Services:`n a. Any significant changes to the services provided by a supplier, or to their security controls, shall be communicated to and reviewed by `nCirque Corporation`n to assess potential information security impacts. b. Changes impacting `nCirque Corporation`n's information security shall be managed through the IS-CIRQ-PR-013-G: Change Management Procedure.`n4.6. Termination of Supplier Relationships:`n a. Upon termination or expiration of a supplier contract, `nCirque Corporation`n shall ensure that: * All access rights to `nCirque Corporation`n's information systems and facilities are immediately revoked. * All `nCirque Corporation`n's information held by the supplier is securely returned, destroyed, or transferred, in accordance with contractual agreements and IS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure. * Confirmation of secure data destruction is obtained from the supplier.`n5. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall responsible for the implementation and oversight of this policy. Manages the process for assessing, contracting, and monitoring suppliers from an information security perspective.`nProcurement Department:`n Responsible for ensuring that information security requirements and clauses are included in all relevant supplier contracts.`nLegal Counsel:`n Reviews all supplier contracts to ensure legal enforceability of security clauses.`nDepartment Managers/Service Owners:`n Responsible for identifying and managing suppliers relevant to their operations and ensuring their teams comply with this policy.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-PR-019-G: Supplier Security Review Procedure (To be drafted next)`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-007-G: Asset Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-009-G: Cryptography Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-013-G: Change Management Procedure`n7. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's supplier relationships, business operations, or relevant legal/regulatory requirements.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-019-G: Supplier Security Review Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-019-G`nStandards Name: Supplier Security Review Procedure`nCategory: Related to Outsourcing`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the systematic process for conducting information security reviews of existing and prospective suppliers who will have access to `nCirque Corporation`n's information, information systems, or facilities, or who will process, store, or transmit `nCirque Corporation`n's information on its behalf. This procedure ensures that suppliers meet `nCirque Corporation`n's information security requirements and helps manage supply chain risks, in accordance with IS-CIRQ-P-013-G: Supplier Relationships Policy and ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.15.1.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n departments and personnel involved in engaging with, or managing, suppliers that meet the criteria defined in the IS-CIRQ-P-013-G: Supplier Relationships Policy. This includes all types of suppliers, from cloud service providers to IT service providers and software vendors.`n3. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall owner of this procedure. Leads and oversees supplier security reviews, assesses security risks, and approves supplier security postures.`nRequesting Department/Service Owner:`n Initiates the supplier engagement process and provides details regarding the data/systems the supplier will access or handle.`nLegal Counsel:`n Reviews contractual terms and conditions related to information security.`nProcurement Department:`n Facilitates communication with suppliers and ensures security requirements are included in contracts.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Initial Supplier Risk Classification`na. **Trigger:** This procedure is triggered when a new supplier is considered, or when there is a significant change in the services provided by an existing supplier that impacts information security (e.g., increased data access, new data types, critical system access).`nb. **Information Gathering:** The Requesting Department/Service Owner, in consultation with the IT Manager, will gather initial information about the supplier and the proposed services, including:`n    * Type of service/product provided.`n    * What ``nCirque Corporation`n` information (data classification, e.g., Confidential, Internal Use) the supplier will access, process, or store.`n    * Which ``nCirque Corporation`n` systems or facilities the supplier will access.`n    * Criticality of the service to ``nCirque Corporation`n`'s operations.`nc. **Risk Classification:** Based on the gathered information, the IT Manager will classify the supplier's security risk as:`n    * **High Risk:** Access to Confidential data (e.g., intellectual property like ASIC designs, customer PII), critical systems (e.g., core financial systems, production servers), or direct management of ``nCirque Corporation`n`'s IT infrastructure.`n    * **Medium Risk:** Access to Internal Use data, non-critical systems, or providing services that are important but not core to ``nCirque Corporation`n`'s immediate operations.`n    * **Low Risk:** No access to ``nCirque Corporation`n`'s information or systems, or only to Public information; typically involves generic, non-IT related services.`n4.2. Security Review Activities (Based on Risk Classification)`na. **High-Risk Suppliers:**`n    * **Security Questionnaire:** The supplier must complete a detailed security questionnaire (e.g., based on ISO 27001, CSA STAR, or industry-specific frameworks).`n    * **Documentation Review:** ``nCirque Corporation`n` will request and review relevant security documentation (e.g., ISO 27001 certificate, SOC 2 Type 2 report, penetration test reports, security policies, incident response plans).`n    * **Virtual/On-site Audit (Optional, based on need):** If concerns remain or for extremely critical suppliers, a virtual or on-site security audit may be conducted by ``nCirque Corporation`n`'s IT/Security personnel.`n    * **Security Architecture Review:** For suppliers providing critical IT services or cloud infrastructure, ``nCirque Corporation`n`'s IT Manager will review their security architecture and controls.`n    * **Legal Review:** Comprehensive review of contract security clauses by Legal Counsel.`nb. **Medium-Risk Suppliers:**`n    * **Abbreviated Security Questionnaire:** The supplier must complete a concise security questionnaire focusing on key control areas.`n    * **Documentation Review:** Review of basic security certifications or publicly available security statements.`n    * **Contractual Clauses:** Ensure inclusion of standard information security clauses in the contract.`nc. **Low-Risk Suppliers:**`n    * **Minimal Review:** Basic due diligence may suffice, primarily focused on ensuring no unforeseen access to ``nCirque Corporation`n`'s sensitive information occurs. Standard contractual terms (if any) apply.`n4.3. Risk Assessment and Decision`na. **Vulnerability Identification:** The IT Manager will analyze the information gathered during the review to identify any security vulnerabilities or gaps in the supplier's controls.`nb. **Risk Scoring:** Identified vulnerabilities and risks will be assessed and scored (referencing `IS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register`).`nc. **Remediation Plan:** If significant risks or control gaps are identified, a remediation plan with timelines must be agreed upon with the supplier before engagement.`nd. **Approval:** The IT Manager will provide a formal security approval or rejection of the supplier based on the comprehensive review and risk assessment. For high-risk suppliers, executive committee approval may be required.`n4.4. Contractual Inclusion of Security Requirements`na. Following security approval, the Procurement Department, with input from the IT Manager and Legal Counsel, will ensure all agreed-upon information security requirements and commitments are accurately reflected and legally binding within the supplier contract.`n4.5. Ongoing Monitoring and Re-evaluation`na. **Regular Monitoring:** The IT Manager and Requesting Department will monitor the supplier's ongoing compliance with security requirements as per `IS-CIRQ-P-013-G: Supplier Relationships Policy`. This may include periodic review of security reports or performance.`nb. **Re-evaluation Triggers:** A new security review (following this procedure) shall be triggered if:`n    * The supplier's service changes significantly, increasing their access or data handling responsibilities.`n    * A security incident occurs involving the supplier.`n    * The supplier's security posture is publicly questioned or known to have been compromised.`n    * At a minimum, for high and medium-risk suppliers, a re-evaluation should occur at least every two years, or as determined by contractual terms.`n5. Documentation`nAll supplier security review activities, including risk classifications, questionnaires, documentation reviews, remediation plans, and approval decisions, shall be documented and retained for audit purposes.`n6. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's supplier engagement processes, risk appetite, or relevant legal/regulatory requirements.`n7. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-013-G: Supplier Relationships Policy`nIS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register`nIS-CIRQ-P-007-G: Asset Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-007-G: Asset Classification and Handling Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-014-G: Information Security Incident Management Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-014-G`nStandards Name: Information Security Incident Management Policy`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish `nCirque Corporation`n's requirements for the effective management of information security incidents. This includes defining a structured approach for reporting, assessing, responding to, resolving, and learning from security incidents. This policy aims to minimize the impact of security incidents, ensure timely recovery, and prevent recurrence, in accordance with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.16.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors, temporary staff), all information assets, information systems, applications, and networks owned or managed by `nCirque Corporation`n across all its global operations (US, Taipei, Japan, China). It covers all types of information security incidents, from minor security events to major breaches.`n3. Definitions`nInformation Security Event:`n An identified occurrence of a system, service, or network state indicating a possible breach of information security policy or failure of controls, or a previously unknown situation that may be security relevant.`nInformation Security Incident:`n A single or a series of unwanted or unexpected information security events that have a significant probability of compromising business operations and threatening information security (e.g., loss of confidentiality, integrity, or availability).`nInformation Security Breach:`n A security incident that results in the confirmed or suspected unauthorized access, use, disclosure, loss, or theft of sensitive or confidential information.`nIncident Response Team (IRT):`n A designated group of individuals responsible for managing and responding to information security incidents.`n4. Principles of Information Security Incident Management`nCirque Corporation`n is committed to managing information security incidents effectively based on the following principles:`nTimeliness:`n Incidents shall be reported, assessed, and responded to as quickly as possible to minimize potential damage.`nContainment and Recovery:`n Efforts shall focus on containing the incident and restoring affected services and data to normal operation in a timely manner.`nLearning and Improvement:`n Incidents shall be analyzed to identify root causes and implement corrective actions to prevent recurrence.`nCompliance:`n All incident management activities shall comply with relevant legal, regulatory, and contractual obligations, including data breach notification requirements.`nCommunication:`n Clear and effective communication shall be maintained with relevant stakeholders throughout the incident lifecycle.`n5. Information Security Incident Management Requirements`n5.1. Incident Reporting:`n a. All `nCirque Corporation`n personnel are responsible for reporting any suspected or actual information security event or incident immediately upon discovery. b. Reporting channels shall be clearly communicated (e.g., direct contact with IT Manager, dedicated email alias, ticketing system). c. Reports shall include as much detail as possible about the event, including time, location, affected systems, and observations.`n5.2. Incident Assessment and Classification:`n a. Upon receiving an incident report, the IT Manager (or designated IT personnel) shall promptly assess the event to determine if it constitutes an information security incident. b. Incidents shall be classified based on their `nseverity, impact on business operations, and potential for data compromise (e.g., Low, Medium, High, Critical). This classification will guide response prioritization.`n5.3. Incident Response and Management:`n a. An `nIncident Response Team (IRT)`n, led by the IT Manager, shall be established with clearly defined roles and responsibilities for handling incidents. b. The incident response process shall follow defined steps, typically including: * `nPreparation:`n Establishing tools, processes, and training for incident handling. * `nDetection &amp; Analysis:`n Identifying and understanding the scope and nature of the incident. * `nContainment:`n Limiting the damage and preventing further spread of the incident. * `nEradication:`n Removing the cause of the incident (e.g., malware, unauthorized access). * `nRecovery:`n Restoring affected systems and data to normal operations. * `nPost-Incident Activity:`n Lessons learned, reporting, and preventive actions. c. Specific procedures shall be developed for common incident types (e.g., malware infection, unauthorized access, data loss, denial of service attacks, phishing).`n5.4. Communication and Escalation:`n a. Clear communication channels shall be established for internal and, where necessary, external stakeholders (e.g., customers, regulatory authorities, law enforcement). b. Incidents shall be escalated based on their classification and potential impact. Critical incidents (e.g., data breaches) require immediate notification to executive management. c. Legal counsel shall be consulted for incidents that may have legal or regulatory implications, particularly concerning data breach notification requirements.`n5.5. Evidence Collection and Preservation:`n a. During an incident, all relevant evidence (e.g., logs, forensic images, physical devices) shall be collected and preserved in a forensically sound manner to support investigation and potential legal action. b. The chain of custody for evidence shall be maintained.`n5.6. Post-Incident Review and Improvement:`n a. After an incident is resolved, a post-incident review (lessons learned) shall be conducted, especially for significant incidents. b. The review shall identify: * What happened, when, and how. * The root cause of the incident. * Effectiveness of the response actions. * Impact of the incident. * Recommendations for preventing recurrence and improving incident response capabilities. c. Corrective actions identified during the review shall be tracked and implemented (refer to IS-CIRQ-PR-013-G: Change Management Procedure).`n5.7. Compliance and Reporting:`n a. `nCirque Corporation`n shall comply with all applicable laws and regulations regarding information security incident reporting and data breach notifications (e.g., GDPR, CCPA, industry-specific regulations). b. Records of all incidents, their handling, and outcomes shall be maintained for audit and compliance purposes.`n6. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Acts as the Incident Response Team Lead. Responsible for coordinating all incident management activities, ensuring resources are available, and reporting to executive management.`nAll Personnel:`n Responsible for prompt reporting of suspected incidents.`nExecutive Committee:`n Provides strategic oversight and approves significant resources for incident management.`nLegal Counsel:`n Provides guidance on legal and regulatory compliance, particularly for data breaches.`n7. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-PR-XXX-G: Information Security Incident Response Procedure (Future document outlining specific steps)`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-011-G: Operations Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-013-G: Change Management Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register`n8. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's operations, legal/regulatory landscape, or lessons learned from incidents.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-020-G: Incident Response Procedure (Global Core)`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-020-G`nStandards Name: Incident Response Procedure (Global Core)`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the global core process for responding to information security incidents within `nCirque Corporation`n. It provides a systematic framework for detecting, analyzing, containing, eradicating, recovering from, and conducting post-incident activities for security incidents, thereby minimizing their impact and facilitating timely recovery, in accordance with IS-CIRQ-P-014-G: Information Security Incident Management Policy and ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.16.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors, temporary staff) and all information assets, information systems, applications, and networks owned or managed by `nCirque Corporation`n across all its global operations (US, Taipei, Japan, China). It covers all suspected or confirmed information security incidents. Localized procedures (IS-CIRQ-PR-020-US, IS-CIRQ-PR-020-`nASIA`n) will provide region-specific details and contacts, but the core steps remain consistent.`n3. Definitions`nInformation Security Incident:`n A single or a series of unwanted or unexpected information security events that have a significant probability of compromising business operations and threatening information security (e.g., loss of confidentiality, integrity, or availability).`nIncident Response Team (IRT):`n A designated group of individuals responsible for managing and responding to information security incidents.`nContainment:`n The act of limiting the scope and impact of an incident.`nEradication:`n The act of removing the cause of the incident.`nRecovery:`n The act of restoring affected systems and services to normal operation.`n4. Incident Response Team (IRT)`na. The **Incident Response Team (IRT)** shall be led by the IT Manager and comprise key personnel from IT, Legal, Human Resources, and relevant business units as required by the nature and scope of the incident.`nb. The IT Manager is the primary Incident Coordinator.`nc. Contact information for IRT members and escalation paths shall be maintained and readily accessible.`n5. Incident Response Lifecycle`nThe incident response process follows a six-phase lifecycle:`n5.1. Phase 1: Preparation`na. **Policy &amp; Procedure Development:** Maintain and review this procedure and the `IS-CIRQ-P-014-G: Information Security Incident Management Policy`.`nb. **IRT Training:** Ensure IRT members receive regular training on incident response procedures, tools, and roles.`nc. **Tools &amp; Resources:** Maintain necessary tools for incident response (e.g., forensic tools, secure communication channels, network diagrams, asset inventories).`nd. **Communication Plan:** Develop and maintain communication templates and escalation matrices for internal and external stakeholders.`ne. **Testing:** Periodically test incident response capabilities through drills or tabletop exercises.`n5.2. Phase 2: Detection and Analysis`na. **Incident Reporting:** Any individual who suspects or confirms an information security event or incident shall report it immediately to the IT Manager or through designated channels (e.g., `security@cirq.com`, or the IT ticketing system).`nb. **Initial Triage:** The IT Manager or designated IT personnel performs initial assessment to determine if a reported event constitutes an information security incident.`nc. **Gather Information:** Collect relevant information: What happened? When? Where? How was it discovered? Who is affected? What is the potential impact?`nd. **Classification and Prioritization:** Classify the incident based on its severity and potential impact (e.g., Critical, High, Medium, Low) using defined criteria. This determines the urgency and resources required.`ne. **Initial Notification:** Based on severity, notify relevant IRT members and leadership as per the escalation matrix.`n5.3. Phase 3: Containment`na. **Strategy:** Develop a containment strategy to prevent further damage and limit the scope of the incident. This strategy should balance business continuity with security needs.`nb. **Execution:** Implement containment measures, which may include:`n    * Isolating affected systems or networks.`n    * Disabling compromised accounts.`n    * Blocking malicious IP addresses or URLs at firewalls.`n    * Disconnecting affected devices from the network.`n    * Applying temporary patches or workarounds.`nc. **Evidence Preservation:** Preserve digital and physical evidence in a forensically sound manner throughout the containment process. Maintain a strict chain of custody.`n5.4. Phase 4: Eradication`na. **Root Cause Analysis:** Identify and address the root cause of the incident to prevent recurrence. This involves analyzing logs (`IS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure`), forensic evidence, and system configurations.`nb. **Malware Removal:** Remove malware, backdoors, or unauthorized configurations.`nc. **Vulnerability Remediation:** Remediate exploited vulnerabilities (e.g., patching systems, reconfiguring firewalls, hardening applications). Implement changes following `IS-CIRQ-PR-013-G: Change Management Procedure`.`n5.5. Phase 5: Recovery`na. **Restoration:** Restore affected systems and data from clean backups (`IS-CIRQ-PR-014-G: Backup and Restoration Procedure`) or re-image systems.`nb. **Verification:** Thoroughly test restored systems and data to ensure full functionality and integrity before returning them to production.`nc. **Monitoring:** Increase monitoring of recovered systems to detect any resurgence of malicious activity.`nd. **Phased Return:** For complex incidents, recovery may be phased, gradually returning systems and services to full operation.`n5.6. Phase 6: Post-Incident Activity (Lessons Learned)`na. **Post-Incident Review (PIR):** For all significant incidents, conduct a formal PIR meeting with relevant IRT members and stakeholders.`nb. **Documentation:** Document all aspects of the incident, including:`n    * Timeline of events.`n    * Actions taken during each phase.`n    * Root cause analysis and contributing factors.`n    * Impact assessment.`n    * Lessons learned.`nc. **Recommendations:** Develop actionable recommendations for improving security controls, policies, procedures, and incident response capabilities.`nd. **Tracking:** Track the implementation of recommendations and corrective actions.`ne. **Reporting:** Prepare a final incident report for management and other relevant stakeholders.`nf. **Compliance:** Ensure all regulatory and legal notification requirements are met.`n6. Communication Strategy`na. **Internal Communication:** Maintain regular internal communication with affected departments, management, and the IRT.`nb. **External Communication:** The IT Manager, in consultation with Legal and Executive Management, will manage all external communications (e.g., to law enforcement, customers, regulatory bodies, media). No unauthorized external communication is permitted.`n7. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's IT environment, lessons learned from incidents, or changes in legal/regulatory requirements.`n8. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-014-G: Information Security Incident Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-013-G: Change Management Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-014-G: Backup and Restoration Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-010-G: Physical and Environmental Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-020-US: Incident Response Procedure (US Localized)`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-020-US`nStandards Name: Incident Response Procedure (US Localized)`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Localized (US)`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager, US General Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to provide localized guidelines for managing information security incidents affecting `nCirque Corporation`n's operations, assets, and information within the United States. It supplements the IS-CIRQ-PR-020-G: Incident Response Procedure (Global Core) by detailing US-specific legal, regulatory, and reporting requirements, as well as local contacts and resources for incident response.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel, information assets, information systems, applications, and networks located within or primarily serving the United States. It covers all suspected or confirmed information security incidents impacting US operations or data related to US citizens or residents.`n3. Responsibilities (US-Specific)`nUS General Manager:`n Responsible for awareness of incidents affecting US operations and ensuring local compliance. May act as a primary point of contact for US-specific legal or regulatory inquiries.`nUS IT Lead/Local Administrator:`n Primary local contact for incident detection, initial containment, and evidence collection within US operations. Works under the direction of the Global IT Manager.`nUS Legal Counsel (or designated external counsel):`n Provides specific legal guidance on US federal and state data breach notification laws (e.g., HIPAA, CCPA, New York SHIELD Act, state-specific breach notification laws) and other relevant regulations.`nUS Human Resources:`n Involved in incidents affecting US employees or requiring internal investigations related to personnel.`n4. US-Specific Incident Response Guidelines`nThis procedure follows the six phases outlined in IS-CIRQ-PR-020-G: Incident Response Procedure (Global Core). The following sections detail US-specific considerations for each phase:`n4.1. Phase 1: Preparation (US Localized)`na. **Regulatory Awareness:** US IT Lead and US General Manager must stay updated on relevant US federal and state data breach notification laws, industry-specific regulations (e.g., if handling healthcare or financial data), and privacy laws (e.g., CCPA, state privacy laws).`nb. **Local Contacts:** Maintain an up-to-date list of US-specific contacts:`n    * Local law enforcement (FBI field office, local police cybercrime unit).`n    * Relevant regulatory bodies (e.g., FTC, state Attorneys General, specific industry regulators).`n    * Designated external US Legal Counsel for breach response.`n    * Local IT support vendors/partners, if applicable.`nc. **Incident Response Plan Exercise:** Include US-specific scenarios in tabletop exercises (e.g., a breach affecting California residents, a system outage in the US office).`n4.2. Phase 2: Detection and Analysis (US Localized)`na. **Local Reporting Channels:** While global channels remain primary, US personnel should also be aware of any local IT or management contacts for immediate reporting during US business hours.`nb. **Initial Data Point:** For incidents originating or detected within the US, the US IT Lead will be a key resource for initial data collection from local systems, network devices, and user devices.`n4.3. Phase 3: Containment (US Localized)`na. **Local Coordination:** Containment efforts within US facilities (e.g., disconnecting specific US-based servers, isolating US network segments) will be coordinated by the US IT Lead under the direction of the Global IT Manager.`n4.4. Phase 4: Eradication (US Localized)`na. **Local Remediation:** Remediation steps on US-based systems, such as patching US servers or reimaging US workstations, will be executed by the US IT Lead or designated local IT personnel.`n4.5. Phase 5: Recovery (US Localized)`na. **US System Restoration:** The recovery of US-specific systems and data will involve US IT personnel to ensure local verification and operational readiness.`n4.6. Phase 6: Post-Incident Activity (Lessons Learned - US Localized)`na. **US Legal Review:** All incident reports potentially involving personal data of US residents must undergo review by US Legal Counsel to determine specific notification obligations under relevant federal and state laws.`nb. **Regulatory Reporting:** The IT Manager, in consultation with US Legal Counsel and US General Manager, is responsible for fulfilling any mandatory data breach notification requirements to affected individuals, state Attorneys General, or other relevant US regulatory bodies within specified timelines.`nc. **Lessons Learned Integration:** US-specific findings and recommendations from post-incident reviews will be incorporated into this localized procedure and communicated to global IT.`n5. US-Specific Communication and External Reporting`na. **Notification to Affected Individuals:** In the event of a data breach involving personal information of US residents, notifications shall be made in accordance with the specific state laws where the individuals reside. This includes content, timing, and method of notification.`nb. **Regulatory Notifications:** Specific regulatory bodies in the US (e.g., Department of Health and Human Services for HIPAA, state Attorneys General, specific industry `nregulators) may require direct notification. This is to be handled by the IT Manager, US General Manager, and US Legal Counsel.`nc. **Law Enforcement:** For criminal incidents, collaboration with US federal (e.g., FBI Cyber Division) or local law enforcement will be managed by the IT Manager and US Legal Counsel.`n6. Review and Update`nThis localized procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's US operations, new US federal or state privacy/breach notification laws, or lessons learned from incidents impacting US entities.`n7. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-014-G: Information Security Incident Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-020-G: Incident Response Procedure (Global Core)`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nState-specific data breach notification laws (external reference, to be monitored by Legal)`nRelevant US federal regulations (external reference)`nIS-CIRQ-PR-020-ASIA: Incident Response Procedure (Asia Localized)`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-020-ASIA`nStandards Name: Incident Response Procedure (Asia Localized)`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Localized (Asia - China, Taiwan)`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager, Regional Asia General Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to provide localized guidelines for managing information security incidents affecting `nCirque Corporation`n's operations, assets, and information within the Asia region, specifically covering entities in `nChina`n and `nTaiwan`n. It supplements the IS-CIRQ-PR-020-G: Incident Response Procedure (Global Core) by detailing region-specific legal, regulatory, and reporting requirements, as well as local contacts and resources for incident response in these locations.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel, information assets, information systems, applications, and networks located within or primarily serving China and Taiwan. It covers all suspected or confirmed information security incidents impacting operations in these regions or data related to their citizens or residents.`n3. Responsibilities (Asia-Specific)`nRegional Asia General Manager:`n Responsible for awareness of incidents affecting Asia operations and ensuring local compliance. Serves as a primary point of contact for regional legal or regulatory inquiries.`nLocal IT Lead (China/Taiwan):`n Primary local contact for incident detection, initial containment, and evidence collection within their respective Asia operations. Works under the direction of the Global IT Manager.`nRegional Legal Counsel (or designated external counsel):`n Provides specific legal guidance on relevant data protection laws, cybersecurity laws, and data breach notification requirements for both `nChina (e.g., Cybersecurity Law, Data Security Law, PIPL)`n and `nTaiwan (e.g., Personal Data Protection Act)`n.`nRegional Human Resources:`n Involved in incidents affecting regional employees or requiring internal investigations related to personnel.`n4. Asia-Specific Incident Response Guidelines`nThis procedure follows the six phases outlined in IS-CIRQ-PR-020-G: Incident Response Procedure (Global Core). The following sections detail Asia-specific considerations for each phase, noting distinctions between China and Taiwan where critical.`n4.1. Phase 1: Preparation (Asia Localized)`na. **Regulatory Awareness:**`n    * **China:** US IT Lead and Regional Asia General Manager must be acutely aware of China's Cybersecurity Law (CSL), Data Security Law (DSL), Personal Information Protection Law (PIPL), and other relevant regulations, including cross-border data transfer rules.`n    * **Taiwan:** Awareness of Taiwan's Personal Data Protection Act (PDPA) and any specific industry regulations.`nb. **Local Contacts:** Maintain an up-to-date list of regional and country-specific contacts:`n    * **China:** Local Cybersecurity Administration of China (CAC), Ministry of Public Security, local law enforcement. Designated external China Legal Counsel.`n    * **Taiwan:** Relevant regulatory bodies, local law enforcement. Designated external Taiwan Legal Counsel.`n    * Local IT support vendors/partners, if applicable, for both regions.`nc. **Incident Response Plan Exercise:** Include Asia-specific scenarios in tabletop exercises (e.g., a data breach impacting personal information of Chinese citizens, a cyberattack targeting the Taiwan office).`n4.2. Phase 2: Detection and Analysis (Asia Localized)`na. **Local Reporting Channels:** While global channels remain primary, personnel in China and Taiwan should also be aware of any local IT or management contacts for immediate reporting during local business hours.`nb. **Initial Data Point:** For incidents originating or detected within China or Taiwan, the respective Local IT Lead will be a key resource for initial data collection from local systems, network devices, and user devices.`n4.3. Phase 3: Containment (Asia Localized)`na. **Local Coordination:** Containment efforts within China/Taiwan facilities (e.g., disconnecting specific local servers, isolating network segments) will be coordinated by the respective Local IT Lead under the direction of the Global IT Manager. Special consideration must be given to potential implications of data transfer restrictions during containment in China.`n4.4. Phase 4: Eradication (Asia Localized)`na. **Local Remediation:** Remediation steps on local systems, such as patching servers or reimaging workstations, will be executed by the respective Local IT Lead or designated local IT personnel.`n4.5. Phase 5: Recovery (Asia Localized)`na. **Local System Restoration:** The recovery of China/Taiwan-specific systems and data will involve local IT personnel to ensure local verification and operational readiness.`n4.6. Phase 6: Post-Incident Activity (Lessons Learned - Asia Localized)`na. **Regional Legal Review:**`n    * **China:** All incident reports potentially involving personal information of Chinese citizens must undergo review by Regional Legal Counsel (or designated external China Legal Counsel) to determine specific notification obligations under CSL, DSL, PIPL, and other relevant laws. **Strict rules apply for cross-border data transfer related to incident response and evidence collection.**`n    * **Taiwan:** All incident reports potentially involving personal information of Taiwan residents must undergo review by Regional Legal Counsel (or designated external Taiwan Legal Counsel) to determine specific notification obligations under PDPA.`nb. **Regulatory Reporting:** The IT Manager, in consultation with the Regional Asia General Manager and Regional Legal Counsel, is responsible for fulfilling any mandatory data breach notification requirements to affected individuals, relevant government authorities `n(e.g., CAC, public security bureaus in China; relevant ministries in Taiwan), or other regional regulatory bodies within specified timelines.`nc. **Lessons Learned Integration:** Asia-specific findings and recommendations from post-incident reviews will be incorporated into this localized procedure and communicated to global IT.`n5. Asia-Specific Communication and External Reporting`na. **Notification to Affected Individuals:** In the event of a data breach involving personal information of residents in China or Taiwan, notifications shall be made in accordance with the specific laws of that region. This includes content, timing, and method of notification.`nb. **Regulatory Notifications:** Specific regulatory bodies in China and Taiwan may require direct notification. This is to be handled by the IT Manager, Regional Asia General Manager, and Regional Legal Counsel. Particular attention must be paid to the strict reporting requirements in China.`nc. **Law Enforcement:** For criminal incidents, collaboration with regional law enforcement (e.g., public security bureaus in China) will be managed by the IT Manager and Regional Legal Counsel, adhering to local legal frameworks.`n6. Review and Update`nThis localized procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's Asia operations, new regional data protection or cybersecurity laws (e.g., changes to PIPL or PDPA), or lessons learned from incidents impacting Asia entities.`n7.
