Distribution`nOriginal: Incident Response Team (IT Manager)`nCopy: Legal (if applicable)`nCopy: Relevant Business Unit Manager (if applicable)`nIS-CIRQ-P-015-G: Information Security Continuity Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-015-G`nStandards Name: Information Security Continuity Policy`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish `nCirque Corporation`n's framework for information security continuity. It ensures that information security is maintained at an acceptable level during and after disruptive events, thereby supporting the overall business continuity and disaster recovery objectives. This policy aims to minimize the impact of disruptions on `nCirque Corporation`n's critical information assets and processes, in accordance with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.17.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n information assets, information systems, applications, and networks deemed critical for the continued operation of `nCirque Corporation`n's business processes across all global locations (US, China, Taiwan). It covers the information security aspects of business continuity planning, disaster recovery planning, and crisis management.`n3. Definitions`nBusiness Continuity (BC):`n The capability of the organization to continue delivery of products or services at pre-defined acceptable levels following a disruptive incident.`nDisaster Recovery (DR):`n The process, policies, and procedures related to preparing for recovery or continuation of technology infrastructure critical to an organization after a natural or human-induced disaster.`nDisruptive Event:`n Any event, internal or external, that could interrupt or disrupt `nCirque Corporation`n's business operations or compromise its information security (e.g., natural disaster, cyberattack, equipment failure, power outage).`nRecovery Time Objective (RTO):`n The maximum tolerable length of time that a computer system, network, or application can be down after a disaster or failure.`nRecovery Point Objective (RPO):`n The maximum tolerable period in which data might be lost from an IT service due to a major incident.`n4. Principles of Information Security Continuity`nCirque Corporation`n is committed to maintaining information security during disruptions based on the following principles:`nRisk-Based Approach:`n Information security continuity plans shall be developed based on identified risks to critical information assets and processes.`nIntegration with BC/DR:`n Information security continuity planning shall be integrated with overall business continuity and disaster recovery planning.`nResilience by Design:`n Systems and processes shall be designed with inherent resilience and redundancy to minimize the impact of disruptions.`nRegular Testing:`n Continuity plans shall be regularly tested and reviewed to ensure their effectiveness and relevance.`nClear Responsibilities:`n Roles and responsibilities for information security during disruptive events shall be clearly defined.`n5. Information Security Continuity Requirements`n5.1. Planning for Information Security Continuity:`n a. `nCirque Corporation`n shall develop and maintain information security continuity plans that address the protection and recovery of critical information assets and systems during and after disruptive events. b. These plans shall be based on a comprehensive Business Impact Analysis (BIA) and Risk Assessment to identify critical business processes, their dependencies on information systems, and associated RTOs and RPOs. c. Specific recovery strategies shall be documented, including fallback procedures, hot/warm/cold site considerations, and data restoration processes.`n5.2. Integration with Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery Plans:`n a. Information security continuity plans shall be an integral part of `nCirque Corporation`n's overarching Business Continuity Plan (BCP) and Disaster Recovery Plan (DRP). b. Security requirements for recovery environments (e.g., alternative sites, cloud recovery instances) shall be as stringent as those for primary operational environments.`n5.3. Protection and Availability of Information:`n a. Critical information and systems shall be protected through appropriate controls to ensure their availability and integrity during disruptive events. This includes: * Regular backups of critical data and system configurations as per IS-CIRQ-PR-014-G: Backup and Restoration Procedure. * Implementation of redundant systems and network components where necessary. * Diversification of data storage locations (e.g., off-site backups). * Protection of critical infrastructure (e.g., power, cooling).`n5.4. Incident Management Integration:`n a. This policy is closely linked with the IS-CIRQ-P-014-G: Information Security Incident Management Policy and related Incident Response Procedures. Incident response aims to contain and eradicate a security event, while continuity focuses on restoring affected business functions and data.`n5.5. Roles and Responsibilities During Disruptions:`n a. Clear roles and responsibilities for managing information security during a disruptive event shall be defined within the BCP/DRP and communicated to relevant personnel. b. This includes roles for security monitoring, access control management, data recovery, and incident communication during crisis situations.`n5.6. Testing and Review:`n a. Information security continuity plans shall be regularly tested (e.g., through tabletop exercises, simulated disruptions, or full-scale recovery tests) to ensure their effectiveness. b. Test results shall be documented, and any identified weaknesses or areas for improvement shall be addressed through corrective actions. c. Plans shall be reviewed and updated at least annually, or following significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's business processes, IT infrastructure, or identified risks.`n5.7. Communications During Disruptions:`n a. Communication protocols shall be established to ensure timely and effective communication with internal stakeholders (employees, management) and external stakeholders (customers, suppliers, regulators, emergency services) during a disruptive event, including clear procedures for security-related communications.`n6. Responsibilities`nExecutive Committee:`n Approves the overall Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery strategy and allocates necessary resources.`nIT Manager:`n Responsible for developing, implementing, and testing information security continuity plans, and integrating them with IT Disaster Recovery Plans.`nBusiness Unit Managers:`n Responsible for identifying critical business processes and their information security requirements for continuity within their respective areas.`nAll Personnel:`n Responsible for understanding their roles in information security continuity plans and reporting any information security concerns that could impact business continuity.`n7. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-014-G: Information Security Incident Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-020-G: Incident Response Procedure (Global Core)`nIS-CIRQ-PR-014-G: Backup and Restoration Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-011-G: Operations Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register`nOverall Business Continuity Plan (External document, to be maintained by Management)`nDisaster Recovery Plan (External document, to be maintained by IT)`n8. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's critical business processes, IT infrastructure, risk profile, or regulatory requirements.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-021-G: Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-021-G`nStandards Name: Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery Procedure`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the structured process for planning, developing, maintaining, and executing `nCirque Corporation`n's Business Continuity (BC) and Disaster Recovery (DR) plans. This procedure aims to ensure the continued availability of critical business processes and the recovery of essential information systems and data in the event of a disruptive incident, minimizing downtime and data loss, in accordance with IS-CIRQ-P-015-G: Information Security Continuity Policy and ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.17.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n departments, critical business processes, information systems, applications, data, and infrastructure across all global locations (US, China, Taiwan). It specifically covers the recovery of servers and fileservers utilizing `nVeeam`n backup and replication solutions, including the immediate recovery of physical servers to virtual machines (P2V).`n3. Definitions`nBusiness Continuity Plan (BCP):`n A comprehensive plan outlining how `nCirque Corporation`n will maintain or quickly resume mission-critical business functions after a disruption.`nDisaster Recovery Plan (DRP):`n A detailed plan focused on the recovery of IT systems and infrastructure following a disruptive event.`nRecovery Time Objective (RTO):`n The maximum tolerable length of time that a business process or system can be down after a disaster or failure.`nRecovery Point Objective (RPO):`n The maximum tolerable period in which data might be lost from an IT service due to a major incident.`nDisaster Recovery Site:`n An alternative location where IT operations can resume after a primary site disaster.`n4. Responsibilities`nExecutive Management:`n Overall responsibility for approving BCP/DRP strategy, funding, and ensuring adequate resources.`nIT Manager (DRP Lead):`n Primary responsible for the development, implementation, testing, and maintenance of the DRP. Coordinates IT recovery efforts.`nBusiness Unit Managers (BCP Owners):`n Responsible for identifying critical business processes, defining their RTO/RPO, and developing their specific continuity plans.`nSystem Administrators/Engineers:`n Responsible for implementing backup and recovery solutions (e.g., `nVeeam`n), maintaining recovery infrastructure, and executing recovery steps during a disaster.`nAll Personnel:`n Responsible for understanding their roles and responsibilities in BCP/DRP activation and execution.`n5. Procedure`n5.1. Business Impact Analysis (BIA) and Risk Assessment`n a. `nIdentify Critical Processes:`n Business Unit Managers shall identify and document all critical business processes that are essential for `nCirque Corporation`n's operation. b. `nDefine RTO/RPO:`n For each critical process, define the maximum acceptable downtime (RTO) and maximum acceptable data loss (RPO). This shall be documented (e.g., in IS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register). c. `nIdentify Dependencies:`n Document dependencies of critical processes on specific information systems, applications, infrastructure, and personnel. d. `nRisk Assessment:`n Conduct a risk assessment to identify potential threats to critical processes and their supporting IT infrastructure, and evaluate existing controls and residual risks.`n5.2. Business Continuity Plan (BCP) Development`n a. Based on the BIA, each Business Unit Manager shall develop a BCP detailing: * Manual workarounds for critical processes if IT systems are unavailable. * Alternate communication methods (e.g., outside normal network). * Designated alternative work locations or remote work capabilities. * Key personnel roles and contact information during a disruption. * Procedures for essential functions that must continue without IT.`n5.3. Disaster Recovery Plan (DRP) Development`n a. The IT Manager shall develop a comprehensive DRP focusing on IT systems recovery, including: * `nRecovery Team:`n Defined roles, responsibilities, and contact information for the DR team. * `nCritical Systems Inventory:`n A prioritized list of critical servers, applications, and data with their RTOs/RPOs. * `nBackup and Recovery Strategy:`n Detailed procedures for data backup and restoration using `nVeeam`n for servers and fileservers, as per IS-CIRQ-PR-014-G: Backup and Restoration Procedure. * `nAlternative Site Strategy:`n Documentation of the designated disaster recovery site(s) (e.g., cloud environment, co-location, warm site). * `nSystem Recovery Procedures:`n Step-by-step instructions for recovering operating systems, applications, databases, and network services. * `nPhysical-to-Virtual (P2V) Recovery:`n Specific procedures for recovering physical servers as virtual machines using `nVeeam's Instant VM Recovery`n feature to ensure immediate operational capability. * `nNetwork Recovery:`n Procedures for restoring network connectivity at the recovery site. * `nApplication Recovery:`n Procedures for restoring and configuring critical applications. * `nData Synchronization:`n Procedures for synchronizing data to the recovery environment. * `nHardware Requirements:`n List of minimum hardware requirements for recovery (if applicable). * `nVendor Contacts:`n List of critical vendor support contacts.`n5.4. Backup and Data Management`n a. All critical data and system configurations shall be regularly backed up using `nVeeam Backup &amp; Replication`n. b. Backups shall be stored securely both on-site and off-site, with appropriate encryption. c. Backup integrity shall be verified periodically. d. Retention periods for backups shall be defined based on data criticality and regulatory requirements.`n5.5. Incident Detection and Declaration of Disaster`n a. Critical incidents that escalate beyond immediate incident response capabilities and significantly disrupt operations shall trigger the BCP/DRP. b. The IT Manager, in consultation with Executive Management, is responsible for declaring a "Disaster" and initiating the DRP.`n5.6. DRP Execution (Recovery Phases)`n a. `nActivation:`n The DR team is convened, and the DRP is activated. b. `nDamage Assessment:`n Initial assessment of damage and impact to IT infrastructure. c. `nSite Mobilization:`n If an alternate site is required, the team moves to or activates the designated DR site/cloud environment. d. `nInfrastructure Recovery:`n Restore core infrastructure components (e.g., networking, virtualization platform). e. `nSystem and Data Recovery (Veeam Specific):`n * Prioritize recovery of systems based on RTO/RPO. * Utilize `nVeeam Instant VM Recovery`n to immediately power on physical server backups as VMs on the recovery site to meet critical RTOs. * Restore other critical servers and fileservers from `nVeeam`n backups. * Restore data volumes and databases. f. `nApplication Recovery:`n Reconfigure and test critical applications. g. `nTesting and Verification:`n Rigorously test all recovered systems and data to ensure functionality and integrity. h. `nUser Access Restoration:`n Re-establish user access to systems and applications.`n5.7. Return to Normal Operations (Reversion)`n a. Once the primary site or systems are restored and verified as stable and secure, a planned reversion process will be executed to return operations from the DR site to the primary environment. b. This process must also be detailed in the DRP to ensure a smooth, secure transition without further disruption or data loss.`n5.8. Testing and Maintenance`n a. `nRegular Testing:`n BCPs and DRPs shall be tested at least annually, or following significant changes to IT infrastructure or business processes. Tests may include: * Tabletop exercises. * Component testing (e.g., backup restoration tests, individual system recovery). * Full-scale disaster recovery drills (end-to-end testing of recovery site, `nVeeam`n recovery processes, and critical applications). b. `nDocumentation Updates:`n Test results shall be documented, and any identified gaps or areas for improvement in the BCP/DRP shall be addressed and incorporated into revised plans (IS-CIRQ-PR-006-G: Document Control Procedure). c. `nPlan Review:`n Review the BCP/DRP at least annually to ensure accuracy, relevance, and alignment with business objectives and RTO/RPO requirements.`n6. Review and Update`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's IT infrastructure, critical business processes, or lessons learned from tests or actual incidents.`n7. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-015-G: Information Security Continuity Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-014-G: Information Security Incident Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-020-G: Incident Response Procedure (Global Core)`nIS-CIRQ-PR-014-G: Backup and Restoration Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-011-G: Operations Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-F-001-G: Risk Assessment Register`nIS-CIRQ-PR-006-G: Document Control Procedure`nVeeam Documentation`n (External reference for specific technical recovery steps)`nIS-CIRQ-D-008-G: Business Continuity Plan (BCP) - TEMPLATE`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-D-008-G`nStandards Name: Business Continuity Plan (BCP)`nCategory: IT Security Related`nDivision: Document`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 (Template) `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee (for actual BCP)`nPurpose:`n This document serves as a template and high-level guide for `nCirque Corporation`n's comprehensive Business Continuity Plan (BCP). The actual BCP will contain detailed, living operational information necessary to maintain or quickly restore mission-critical business functions following a disruptive incident, ensuring adherence to IS-CIRQ-P-015-G: Information Security Continuity Policy and IS-CIRQ-PR-021-G: Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery Procedure.`nInstructions for Use:`n This is a template. It specifically integrates the use of `nVeeam`n for server and fileserver recovery, including the immediate recovery of physical servers to virtual machines (P2V).`nInstructions for Use:`n This is a template. The full DRP should be maintained as a highly detailed, operational document.`nTABLE OF CONTENTS`nIntroduction and Purpose`n 1.1. Purpose of the DRP 1.2. BCP Team / Business Unit Managers 6.4. `nDR Site Activation and Connectivity:`n * Procedures for activating DR site infrastructure. * Network re-configuration (DNS updates, routing changes, VPN establishment).`nSystem-Specific Recovery Procedures`n (Detailed, step-by-step instructions for each critical system, aligned with RTO/RPO) 7.1. `nCore Infrastructure Recovery:`n * `nVirtualization Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish `nCirque Corporation`n's commitment and framework for complying with all applicable legal, regulatory, contractual, and internal requirements related to information security. This policy ensures that `nCirque Corporation`n operates within the bounds of relevant laws and standards, protects its information assets, and avoids legal penalties, financial losses, and reputational damage resulting from non-compliance. It aligns with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.18 (Compliance).`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors, temporary staff), all information assets, information systems, applications, and networks, and all global operations (US, Taipei, China). It covers compliance with external requirements (laws, regulations, contracts) and internal requirements (policies, procedures, standards).`n3. Principles of Compliance`nCirque Corporation`n is committed to achieving and maintaining compliance through the following principles:`nCommitment:`n Executive management is committed to establishing and maintaining an information security compliance program.`nProactive Approach:`n Continuously identify and assess new and changing legal, regulatory, and contractual obligations.`nIntegration:`n Incorporate compliance requirements into all relevant information security policies, procedures, and controls.`nAccountability:`n Clearly define roles and responsibilities for compliance across all levels of the organization.`nMonitoring and Review:`n Regularly monitor compliance status and review the effectiveness of controls.`nRemediation:`n Promptly address any identified non-compliance or control deficiencies.`n4. Compliance Requirements`n4.1. Identification of Applicable Requirements:`n a. `nCirque Corporation`n shall establish a process to identify all relevant information security-related legal, regulatory, contractual, and internal requirements. This includes, but is not limited to: * `nLegal &amp; Regulatory:`n Data protection laws (e.g., GDPR if applicable for EU data, CCPA/CPRA in US, PIPL/CSL/DSL in China, PDPA in Taiwan), industry-specific regulations (e.g., SOX if publicly traded, specific financial or healthcare regulations), national cybersecurity laws. * `nContractual:`n Customer agreements, supplier contracts, non-disclosure agreements (NDAs) that contain specific information security clauses. * `nStandards &amp; Frameworks:`n ISO/IEC 27001:2022. * `nInternal:`n All `nCirque Corporation`n policies, procedures, and standards (e.g., IS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy).`n4.2. Establishment of Information Security Controls:`n a. Appropriate information security controls shall be designed, implemented, and maintained to meet identified compliance obligations. b. These controls shall be documented in relevant policies and procedures across the ISMS.`n4.3. Monitoring and Review of Compliance:`n a. Compliance status shall be regularly monitored through internal audits, reviews of logs (IS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure), and control effectiveness assessments. b. Independent reviews of the ISMS, including compliance with this policy, shall be conducted periodically (e.g., internal audits, external certification audits).`n4.4. Legal and Regulatory Compliance:`n a. `nIntellectual Property Rights:`n `nCirque Corporation`n shall ensure that intellectual property rights are respected and protected, including adherence to licensing agreements for software and digital assets. b. `nProtection of Records:`n Records shall be protected from loss, destruction, falsification, unauthorized `naccess, and unauthorized release, in accordance with legal, regulatory, and business requirements. c. `nPrivacy and Protection of PII:`n Compliance with all applicable data privacy laws and regulations concerning the collection, processing, storage, and transfer of Personally Identifiable Information (PII) is mandatory (refer to IS-CIRQ-P-017-G: Privacy Policy (Global Core) and localized versions). d. `nRegulation of Cryptographic Controls:`n The use of cryptographic controls shall comply with all relevant agreements, legislation, and regulations.`n4.5. Contractual Compliance:`n a. All contracts with customers and suppliers that involve access to or processing of `nCirque Corporation`n's information or information systems shall include appropriate information security clauses. b. Compliance with information security requirements specified in such contracts shall be regularly verified (refer to IS-CIRQ-P-013-G: Supplier Relationships Policy).`n4.6. Independent Review of Information Security:`n a. An independent review of the organization’s approach to managing information security and its implementation (e.g., internal audit, external audit, management review) shall be carried out at planned intervals.`n4.7. Information Security Policy Review:`n a. This policy, along with other ISMS documents, shall be reviewed and updated periodically to ensure continued relevance and compliance with evolving requirements.`n5. Non-Compliance and Corrective Actions`na. Any identified instances of non-compliance shall be documented and managed through a formal corrective action process.`nb. Root cause analysis shall be performed, and appropriate corrective and preventive measures shall be implemented to address the non-compliance and prevent recurrence.`nc. Significant non-compliance or breaches of law/regulation may result in disciplinary action as per `IS-CIRQ-P-003-G: Disciplinary Policy` (if such a policy exists) and may require reporting to legal authorities or regulatory bodies.`n6. Responsibilities`nExecutive Committee:`n Overall responsibility for ensuring `nCirque Corporation`n's compliance posture and approving significant compliance initiatives.`nLegal Counsel:`n Advises on legal and regulatory requirements, reviews contracts, and provides guidance on compliance issues.`nIT Manager:`n Responsible for implementing technical controls to meet compliance requirements and for monitoring system-level compliance.`nHuman Resources:`n Ensures employees are aware of and adhere to compliance policies, especially those related to privacy and data handling.`nAll Personnel:`n Responsible for understanding and adhering to all applicable policies, procedures, laws, and regulations related to information security.`n7. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-017-G: Privacy Policy (Global Core)`nIS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-013-G: Supplier Relationships Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-006-G: Document Control Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-003-G: Disciplinary Policy (if applicable)`n8. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's business operations, legal/regulatory environment, or contractual obligations.`nIS-CIRQ-P-017-G: Privacy Policy (Global Core)`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-017-G`nStandards Name: Privacy Policy (Global Core)`nCategory: Information Management Regulations`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish `nCirque Corporation`n's commitment to protecting the privacy of personal information collected, processed, and stored across its global operations. It sets forth the foundational principles and requirements for handling personal information responsibly, ethically, and in compliance with applicable global privacy laws and regulations. This policy aims to build trust with individuals whose data we handle and mitigate risks associated with privacy breaches.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors, temporary staff), all business processes, systems, applications, and networks involved in the collection, use, storage, transmission, and disposal of personal information, regardless of format, across all global locations (US, Taipei, China). It covers all types of personal information, including but not limited to employee data, customer data, and vendor data.`n3. Definitions`nPersonal Information (PI) / Personally Identifiable Information (PII):`n Any information that can be used to identify an individual, either directly or indirectly, in combination with other information. This includes, but is not limited to, names, `naddresses, email addresses, phone numbers, employee IDs, government-issued identification numbers, and biometric data.`nProcessing:`n Any operation performed on personal information, whether automated or not, such as collection, recording, organization, structuring, storage, adaptation, alteration, retrieval, consultation, use, disclosure by transmission, dissemination, or otherwise making available, alignment, combination, restriction, erasure, or destruction.`nData Subject:`n The identified or identifiable natural person to whom personal information relates.`nConsent:`n Any freely given, specific, informed, and unambiguous indication of the data subject's wishes by which he or she, by a statement or by a clear affirmative action, signifies agreement to the processing of personal information relating to him or her.`n4. Principles of Personal Information Processing`nCirque Corporation`n adheres to the following globally recognized privacy principles when processing personal information:`nLawfulness, Fairness, and Transparency:`n Personal information shall be processed lawfully, fairly, and in a transparent manner in relation to the data subject.`nPurpose Limitation:`n Personal information shall be collected for specified, explicit, and legitimate purposes and not further processed in a manner that is incompatible with those purposes.`nData Minimization:`n Personal information shall be adequate, relevant, and limited to what is necessary in relation to the purposes for which they are processed.   `nAccuracy:`n Personal information shall be accurate and, where necessary, kept up to date; every reasonable step shall be taken to ensure that personal information that are inaccurate, having regard to the purposes for which they are processed, are erased or rectified without delay.   `nStorage Limitation:`n Personal information shall be kept in a form which permits identification of data subjects for no longer than is necessary for the purposes for which the personal information are processed.`nIntegrity and Confidentiality:`n Personal information shall be processed in a manner that ensures appropriate security of the personal information, including protection `nagainst unauthorized or unlawful processing and against accidental loss, destruction, or damage, using appropriate technical or organizational measures.`nAccountability:`n `nCirque Corporation`n shall be responsible for, and be able to demonstrate compliance with, these principles.`n5. Personal Information Handling Requirements`n5.1. Collection of Personal Information:`n a. Personal information shall only be collected when there is a clear, legitimate business purpose and legal basis for doing so (e.g., consent, contract, legal obligation, legitimate interest). b. Data subjects shall be informed about the purposes of processing their personal information, the types of data collected, and their rights, typically through a privacy notice. c. Collection methods shall be fair and non-intrusive.`n5.2. Use of Personal Information:`n a. Personal information shall only be used for the purposes for which it was collected, or for compatible purposes as explicitly disclosed to the data subject. b. Any new use of personal information beyond the original purpose requires re-evaluation of the legal basis and, if necessary, obtaining new consent.`n5.3. Disclosure and Sharing of Personal Information:`n a. Personal information shall not be disclosed, shared, or sold to third parties without a legitimate business purpose and a valid legal basis. b. Where personal information is shared with third parties (e.g., vendors, partners), `nCirque Corporation`n shall ensure that appropriate data protection agreements are in place to safeguard the information, requiring third parties to adhere to similar privacy standards. c. Cross-border transfers of personal information shall comply with applicable international data transfer laws and regulations, ensuring adequate levels of protection.`n5.4. Storage and Retention of Personal Information:`n a. Personal information shall be stored securely using appropriate technical and organizational measures to prevent unauthorized access, loss, or destruction. b. Retention periods for personal information shall be established based on legal, regulatory, contractual, and business requirements. Personal information shall be securely disposed of when no longer needed.`n5.5. Data Subject Rights:`n a. `nCirque Corporation`n shall establish processes to facilitate data subjects' exercise of their rights concerning their personal information, which may include: * Right to access their data. * Right to rectification of inaccurate data. * Right to erasure ("right to be forgotten"). * Right to restriction of processing. * Right to data portability. * Right to object to processing. * Rights related to automated decision-making. b. Requests from data subjects shall be handled in a timely and compliant manner.`n5.6. Security of Personal Information:`n a. Robust information security controls shall be implemented to protect personal information from unauthorized access, alteration, disclosure, or destruction. This includes technical measures (e.g., encryption, access controls, network security) and organizational measures (e.g., training, policies, incident response). (Refer to IS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy and related procedures). b. In the event of a personal data breach, `nCirque Corporation`n shall follow its IS-CIRQ-P-014-G: Information Security Incident Management Policy and relevant localized procedures, including timely notification to affected individuals and regulatory authorities where required.`n5.7. Privacy by Design and by Default:`n a. Privacy considerations shall be integrated into the design and operation of all new systems, processes, and products that involve the processing of personal information. b. Default settings for new systems and services should prioritize privacy.`n5.8. Data Protection Impact Assessments (DPIA) / Privacy Impact Assessments (PIA):`n a. Where personal information processing is likely to result in a high risk to the rights and freedoms of data subjects, a DPIA/PIA shall be conducted prior to the processing.`n6. Training and Awareness`nAll personnel involved in the handling of personal information shall receive appropriate training on data privacy principles, this policy, and relevant procedures.`n7. Compliance and Monitoring`na. ``nCirque Corporation`n` shall regularly monitor its compliance with this policy and applicable privacy laws and regulations.`nb. Internal audits and external assessments may be conducted to ensure ongoing adherence.`n8. Responsibilities`nExecutive Committee:`n Overall responsibility for establishing and upholding `nCirque Corporation`n's commitment to data privacy.`nLegal Counsel:`n Provides expert advice on privacy laws and regulations, reviews privacy notices and data processing agreements.`nIT Manager:`n Responsible for implementing technical and organizational measures to protect personal information and for managing privacy-related aspects of IT systems.`nHuman Resources:`n Manages privacy relating to employee data.`nMarketing/Sales:`n Responsible for privacy considerations in customer data collection and communication.`nAll Personnel:`n Responsible for understanding and adhering to this policy and all related privacy procedures.`n9. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-014-G: Information Security Incident Management Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-020-G: Incident Response Procedure (Global Core)`nIS-CIRQ-P-016-G: Compliance Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-017-US: Privacy Policy (US Localized) (Next document)`nIS-CIRQ-P-017-ASIA: Privacy Policy (Asia Localized) (Future document)`nIS-CIRQ-PR-XXX-G: Data Subject Request Procedure (Future document)`n10. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to define `nCirque Corporation`n's specific requirements for protecting the privacy of personal information collected, processed, and stored within its operations in the United States. This policy supplements the IS-CIRQ-P-017-G: Privacy Policy (Global Core) by addressing US federal and state-specific privacy laws and regulations, ensuring compliance and enhancing trust with individuals whose data we handle in the US.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel, business processes, systems, applications, and networks located within or primarily serving the United States, that are involved in the collection, use, storage, transmission, and disposal of personal information related to US residents. It covers all types of personal information, including but not limited to employee data, customer data, and vendor data within the US jurisdiction.`n3. Definitions (US-Specific)`nConsumer:`n For the purposes of this policy, often refers to a natural person who is a California resident, as defined by the California Consumer Privacy Act (CCPA) and California Privacy Rights Act (CPRA), or a resident of other states with similar privacy laws.`nPersonal Information (PI) / Personally Identifiable Information (PII):`n As defined in the Global Core Policy, with specific attention to categories of personal information outlined by US state privacy laws (e.g., California, Virginia, Colorado).`nSale (of Personal Information):`n The sharing of personal information for monetary or other valuable consideration, as defined by applicable US state laws (e.g., CCPA/CPRA).`n4. US-Specific Privacy Principles and Requirements`nIn addition to the global principles outlined in IS-CIRQ-P-017-G: Privacy Policy (Global Core), `nCirque Corporation`n adheres to the following US-specific requirements:`n4.1. Compliance with US Federal Laws:`n a. `nHIPAA (Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act):`n If `nCirque Corporation`n processes Protected Health Information (PHI) as a covered entity or business associate, strict adherence to HIPAA Privacy, Security, and Breach Notification Rules is required. b. `nFERPA (Family Educational Rights and Privacy Act):`n If `nCirque Corporation`n handles educational records, compliance with FERPA rules concerning student privacy is mandatory. c. `nCOPPA (Children's Online Privacy Protection Act):`n If online services target or collect information from children under 13, compliance with COPPA is required, including parental consent mechanisms. d. `nGramm-Leach-Bliley Act (GLBA):`n If `nCirque Corporation`n processes non-public personal information of customers in the financial services sector, compliance with GLBA safeguards and privacy rules is required. e. `nCAN-SPAM Act:`n Compliance with regulations for commercial email, including opt-out mechanisms and accurate sender information.`n4.2. Compliance with US State Privacy Laws (Key Examples):`n a. `nCalifornia Consumer Privacy Act (CCPA) &amp; California Privacy Rights Act (CPRA):`n * `nConsumer Rights:`n Establish procedures to fulfill consumer rights, including the right to know, right to delete, right to opt-out of the sale/sharing of personal information, and the right to correct. * `nNotice at Collection:`n Provide clear notice to consumers about the categories of personal information collected and the purposes for which it will be used. * `nOpt-out of Sale/Sharing:`n Implement mechanisms for consumers to exercise their right to opt-out, including a clear "Do Not Sell or Share My Personal Information" link on relevant online interfaces. * `nService Provider Contracts:`n Ensure contracts with service providers meet CCPA/CPRA requirements. * `nData Minimization &amp; Storage Limitation:`n Emphasize principles of data minimization and purpose limitation in line with California requirements. b. `nVirginia Consumer Data Protection Act (VCDPA), Colorado Privacy Act (CPA), and other emerging state privacy laws:`n * Where applicable based on thresholds and operations, `nCirque Corporation`n will implement processes to address similar consumer `nrights (access, deletion, correction, opt-out of sale/targeted advertising) and duties of controllers (e.g., data protection assessments, purpose limitation).`n4.3. Employee Data Privacy (US-Specific):`n a. Personal information related to US employees shall be handled in accordance with federal laws (e.g., EEOC regulations, ADA, FCRA if background checks are involved) and applicable state employment and privacy laws. b. Specific privacy notices may be provided to employees regarding the collection and use of their data.`n4.4. Privacy Notices and Transparency:`n a. Provide clear, concise, and accessible privacy notices to individuals (e.g., website privacy policy, employee privacy notice) detailing: * Categories of personal information collected. * Purposes of collection and processing. * Categories of sources from which personal information is collected. * Categories of third parties with whom personal information is shared. * Instructions on how data subjects can exercise their rights. b. Keep privacy notices up-to-date with changes in data practices or applicable laws.`n4.5. Data Breach Notification:`n a. In the event of a personal data breach involving US residents, `nCirque Corporation`n shall comply with all applicable US federal and state data breach notification laws. This includes timely notification to affected individuals, and potentially state Attorneys General or other regulatory bodies, as required by law. (Refer to IS-CIRQ-PR-020-US: Incident Response Procedure (US Localized)).`n5. Responsibilities (US-Specific)`nUS General Manager:`n Oversees compliance with US privacy laws and acts as a key point of contact for US privacy matters.`nUS Legal Counsel (or designated external counsel):`n Provides specific legal interpretation and guidance on US federal and state privacy laws, reviews privacy notices, and advises on data subject requests and breach notifications.`nUS IT Lead:`n Responsible for implementing and maintaining technical controls to protect personal information within US systems.`nHuman Resources (US):`n Ensures compliance with employee privacy regulations specific to the US.`n6. Training and Awareness`nAll US personnel involved in handling personal information shall receive training specifically covering US federal and state privacy laws relevant to their roles, in addition to global privacy principles.`n7. Compliance and Monitoring`na. ``nCirque Corporation`n` will periodically assess its privacy practices against US federal and state legal requirements.`nb. Internal audits and privacy impact assessments (PIAs) may include US-specific legal compliance checks.`n8. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to define `nCirque Corporation`n's specific requirements for protecting the privacy of personal information collected, processed, and stored within its operations in the Asia region, particularly focusing on `nChina`n and `nTaiwan`n. This policy supplements the IS-CIRQ-P-017-G: Privacy Policy (Global Core) by addressing region-specific privacy laws and regulations, ensuring compliance and fostering trust with individuals whose data we handle in these jurisdictions.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel, business processes, systems, applications, and networks located within or primarily serving China and Taiwan, that are involved in the collection, use, storage, transmission, and disposal of personal information related to residents of these regions. It covers all types of personal information, including employee data, customer data, and vendor data within these jurisdictions.`n3. Definitions (Asia-Specific)`nPersonal Information (PI):`n As defined in the Global Core Policy, but specifically acknowledging the broader definitions and sensitive categories defined under China's Personal Information Protection Law (PIPL) and Taiwan's Personal Data Protection Act (PDPA).`nSensitive Personal Information (China PIPL):`n Personal information that, once leaked or illegally used, may easily lead to infringement of the personal dignity of natural persons or serious harm to personal or property safety (e.g., biometric data, religious beliefs, medical health, financial accounts, whereabouts, etc.). Strict rules apply to its processing.`nData Controller (Taiwan PDPA):`n A government agency, public school, or association, or a juridical person or natural person that collects, processes or uses personal data.`nCross-Border Transfer:`n Transfer of personal information outside the territory where it was collected (e.g., from China to the US).`n4. Asia-Specific Privacy Principles and Requirements`nIn addition to the global principles outlined in IS-CIRQ-P-017-G: Privacy Policy (Global Core), `nCirque Corporation`n adheres to the following Asia-specific requirements:`n4.1. Compliance with China's Laws (Key focus: PIPL, CSL, DSL):`n a. `nPersonal Information Protection Law (PIPL):`n * `nLegal Basis:`n Ensure clear legal basis (e.g., consent, contract, legal obligation) for processing personal information, with explicit and separate consent required for sensitive personal information, public disclosure, or cross-border transfers. * `nPurpose &amp; Necessity:`n Strict adherence to purpose limitation and data minimization; processing must be for a clear and reasonable purpose directly related to the business. * `nData Subject Rights:`n Implement mechanisms to facilitate data subjects' rights, including access, correction, deletion, withdrawal of consent, and the right to object to automated decision-making. * `nImpact Assessments:`n Conduct Personal Information Protection Impact Assessments (PIPIAs) for high-risk processing activities, including processing sensitive personal information, using personal information for automated decision-making, or large-scale processing. * `nCross-Border Data Transfer:`n Crucial requirements must be met for transferring personal information out of China, including: * Passing a security assessment organized by the Cyberspace Administration of China (CAC). * Obtaining a personal information protection certification. * Entering into a standard contract with the overseas recipient. * Obtain separate consent from the individual. * `nData Localization (Implicit):`n While not a direct localization law, for critical information infrastructure operators (CIIOs), PIPL and CSL require personal information and important data collected and generated within China to be stored within China. * `nData Classification &amp; Localization (Data Security Law - DSL):`n Recognize and comply with China's data classification system (ranging from ordinary to core data) and potential localization requirements for "important data" and "core data." b. `nCybersecurity Law `n(CSL):`n * Applies to network operators and critical information infrastructure operators (CIIOs). Requires data localization for personal information and important data collected and generated by CIIOs in China. * Mandatory security reviews for network products and services, and adherence to multi-level protection scheme (MLPS) requirements. c. `nData Security Law (DSL):`n * Establishes a national data classification and protection system. * Requires security assessments for cross-border data transfers of "important data."`n4.2. Compliance with Taiwan's Laws (Key focus: Personal Data Protection Act - PDPA):`n a. `nPersonal Data Protection Act (PDPA):`n * `nLegal Basis:`n Collection and processing of personal data generally require specific consent from the data subject, or other legal basis (e.g., contract, legal obligation). Special requirements apply to "sensitive personal data" (e.g., medical, genetic, sexual life, criminal records). * `nPurpose &amp; Notification:`n Data must be collected for a specific purpose, and individuals must be informed of the purpose, categories of data, and their rights. * `nData Subject Rights:`n Individuals have rights including inquiry, review, duplication, supplementation/correction, deletion, and cessation of collection/processing/use. * `nSecurity Measures:`n Implement appropriate security measures to prevent theft, alteration, damage, destruction, or unauthorized disclosure of personal data. * `nCross-Border Transfer:`n Generally permissible with appropriate safeguards (e.g., ensuring adequate protection in the recipient country, obtaining consent, or having a contractual basis). Transfers to countries prohibited by the central competent authority are not allowed.`n4.3. Privacy Notices and Transparency:`n a. Provide distinct, clear, and comprehensive privacy notices to individuals in China and Taiwan, tailored to the specific requirements of PIPL/CSL/DSL and PDPA respectively. This includes detailed information on: * Identity and contact information of the personal information handler/data controller. * Types of personal information collected. * Purposes and methods of processing. * Retention periods. * Means and procedures for exercising data subject rights. * Specific disclosures for cross-border transfers.`n4.4. Data Breach Notification:`n a. In the event of a personal data breach affecting residents in China or Taiwan, `nCirque Corporation`n shall comply with all applicable local data breach notification laws. This includes timely notification to affected individuals and relevant regulatory authorities (e.g., Cyberspace Administration of China, Ministry of Justice in Taiwan). (Refer to IS-CIRQ-PR-020-ASIA: Incident Response Procedure (Asia Localized)).`n5. Responsibilities (Asia-Specific)`nRegional Asia General Manager:`n Oversees compliance with Asia privacy laws and acts as a key point of contact for regional privacy matters.`nRegional Legal Counsel (or designated external counsel for China/Taiwan):`n Provides specific legal interpretation and guidance on China and Taiwan privacy laws, reviews privacy notices, and advises on data subject requests and breach notifications. Critical for navigating China's complex regulatory landscape.`nLocal IT Lead (China/Taiwan):`n Responsible for implementing and maintaining technical controls to protect personal information within their respective regional systems, particularly concerning data localization and cross-border transfer requirements.`nHuman Resources (Asia):`n Ensures compliance with employee privacy regulations specific to China and Taiwan.`n6. Training and Awareness`nAll personnel in China and Taiwan involved in handling personal information shall receive training specifically covering the detailed requirements of China's PIPL/CSL/DSL and Taiwan's PDPA, in addition to global privacy principles.`n7. Compliance and Monitoring`na. ``nCirque Corporation`n` will periodically assess its privacy practices against China and Taiwan legal requirements, including conducting regular PIPIAs/PIAs as necessary.`nb. Internal audits and external assessments may be conducted to ensure ongoing adherence, with a strong focus on China's audit and compliance mechanisms.`n8. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-017-G: Privacy Policy (Global Core)`nIS-CIRQ-P-016-G: Compliance Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-020-ASIA: Incident Response Procedure (Asia Localized)`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nChina Personal Information Protection Law (PIPL) (external reference)`nChina Cybersecurity Law (CSL) (external reference)`nChina Data Security Law (DSL) (external reference)`nTaiwan Personal Data Protection Act (PDPA) (external reference)`nWebsite Privacy Policy / Employee Privacy Notice (external facing/internal HR document, tailored for China/Taiwan)`n9. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's Asia operations, new privacy laws/regulations in China or Taiwan, or legal interpretations affecting data privacy practices in these regions.`nIS-CIRQ-P-018-G: Clear Desk and Clear Screen Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-018-G`nStandards Name: Clear Desk and Clear Screen Policy`nCategory: Operations Security`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to minimize the risk of unauthorized access, loss of, or damage to `nCirque Corporation`n's information and information assets. By establishing "Clear Desk" and "Clear Screen" practices, this policy aims to protect sensitive and confidential information from accidental disclosure, theft, or unauthorized viewing by third parties (e.g., visitors, cleaning staff, or unauthorized personnel), especially in shared work environments. This policy aligns with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A.11.2.3 (Clear desk and clear screen).`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors, temporary staff), visitors, and anyone working within `nCirque Corporation`n's offices or remote work locations where `nCirque Corporation`n's information assets are used. It covers all information, whether in hard copy (e.g., documents, notes) or electronic form (e.g., on computer screens, mobile devices).`n3. Definitions`nClear Desk:`n A practice where all sensitive or confidential documents, removable media, and other information assets are secured when not in use or when the workstation is unattended.`nClear Screen:`n A practice where computer screens displaying sensitive or confidential information are protected from unauthorized viewing, typically by locking the screen or activating a password-protected screensaver when leaving the workstation unattended.`nSensitive Information:`n Any information classified as Confidential, Restricted, or Internal Use Only (refer to IS-CIRQ-P-002-G: Information Classification Policy).`n4. Policy Requirements`n4.1. Clear Desk Policy:`n a. All sensitive or confidential documents, notes, removable media (e.g., USB drives, CDs/DVDs), and portable devices (e.g., laptops, tablets, mobile phones) must be secured in a locked drawer, cabinet, or other secure storage when leaving the workstation or office area unattended (even for short periods, such as lunch breaks or meetings). b. Non-sensitive internal documents (e.g., general notices, public reports) may be left on desks if they do not contain any confidential or internal-use-only information. c. At the end of the workday, or when leaving the office for an extended period, all desks must be cleared, and all information assets secured. d. Printers and fax machines should be cleared immediately after use to prevent sensitive documents from being left exposed. e. Whiteboards and other display surfaces used for confidential discussions should be erased or secured after use.`n4.2. Clear Screen Policy:`n a. All computer screens (desktops, laptops, monitors), mobile devices (tablets, smartphones), and other electronic displays used for `nCirque Corporation`n's business must be secured when the user leaves the workstation unattended. b. This can be achieved by: * Locking the computer (e.g., Windows Key + L, Ctrl+Alt+Del -&gt; Lock, Command+Control+Q for Mac). * Activating a password-protected screen saver. * Logging off the system. c. Users should configure their operating systems to automatically activate a password-protected screen saver after a short period of inactivity (e.g., 5-10 minutes). d. When working in public or semi-public areas (e.g., coffee shops, airports), extra care must be taken to prevent "shoulder surfing" (unauthorized viewing). Consider using privacy screens on devices.`n4.3. Waste Disposal:`n a. All documents containing sensitive or confidential information, whether hard copy or electronic media, must be disposed of securely (e.g., shredding, secure disposal bins, certified data destruction for electronic media) as per IS-CIRQ-PR-017-G: Secure Disposal and Media Sanitization Procedure. b. Under no circumstances should sensitive information be placed in regular trash bins.`n4.4. Remote Work and Home Office Considerations:`n a. The principles of Clear Desk and Clear Screen apply equally to employees working remotely or from home offices. b. `nSensitive documents or devices should not be left exposed where family members, guests, or others could view or access them. c. Ensure that work areas are free from visual or auditory eavesdropping when handling sensitive information or participating in confidential meetings.`n5. Responsibilities`nAll Personnel:`n Are responsible for adhering to this policy at all times to protect `nCirque Corporation`n's information assets.`nIT Manager:`n Responsible for configuring system defaults (e.g., screen saver activation) where technically feasible and providing guidance on secure work practices.`nManagers/Supervisors:`n Responsible for ensuring their teams understand and comply with this policy within their respective work areas.`n6. Non-Compliance`nFailure to comply with this policy may result in disciplinary action, up to and including termination of employment, as outlined in `nCirque Corporation`n's disciplinary procedures. Repeated non-compliance may also lead to security incidents, legal liabilities, or reputational damage for `nCirque Corporation`n.`n7. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's work environment, technologies, or security risks.`nIS-CIRQ-P-019-G: Acceptable Use Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-019-G`nStandards Name: Acceptable Use Policy`nCategory: Operations Security`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to define the acceptable and prohibited uses of `nCirque Corporation`n's information technology (IT) resources, including but not limited to computer systems, networks, applications, software, hardware, email, and internet access. This policy ensures that IT resources are used in a manner that supports `nCirque Corporation`n's business objectives, protects its information assets, complies with legal and ethical standards, and maintains a secure and productive work environment.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors, temporary staff), visitors, and any third party who uses or has access to `nCirque Corporation`n's IT resources, regardless of location (on-site or remote) or device ownership (company-issued or personal devices used for business purposes).`n3. Definitions`nIT Resources:`n Any computing or communication equipment, software, data, network services, email, internet access, or other technology owned or controlled by `nCirque Corporation`n, or to which `nCirque Corporation`n provides access.`nCompany Data:`n Any information created, stored, processed, or transmitted on `nCirque Corporation`n's IT resources, regardless of its classification.`n4. General Principles of Acceptable Use`nAll users of `nCirque Corporation`n's IT resources are expected to adhere to the following general principles:`nProfessional Conduct:`n Use IT resources in a professional, ethical, and lawful manner consistent with `nCirque Corporation`n's values and other company policies.`nBusiness Focus:`n Prioritize the use of IT resources for legitimate `nCirque Corporation`n business purposes. Limited personal use is generally permitted if it does not interfere with job duties, consume excessive resources, or violate other policies.`nSecurity:`n Take all reasonable steps to protect `nCirque Corporation`n's IT resources and information from unauthorized access, use, disclosure, disruption, modification, or destruction.`nCompliance:`n Comply with all applicable laws, regulations, and `nCirque Corporation`n policies, including those related to information security, privacy, and intellectual property.`n5. Acceptable Use Requirements`n5.1. Account and Password Security:`n a. Users must protect their user accounts, passwords, and other authentication credentials. b. Sharing of passwords or user accounts is strictly prohibited. c. Users are responsible for all activities conducted under their user accounts. d. Refer to IS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy for specific password requirements.`n5.2. Data Handling and Confidentiality:`n a. Users must handle Company Data in accordance with its classification (refer to IS-CIRQ-P-002-G: Information Classification Policy). b. Confidential and sensitive information must not be stored on unapproved personal devices or cloud services. c. Do not share or disclose confidential or sensitive Company Data to unauthorized individuals, internally or externally. d. Use secure methods for data transfer as outlined in IS-CIRQ-P-010-G: Information Transfer Policy.`n5.3. Email and Communication:`n a. Email and communication systems are primarily for business purposes. b. Do not send or forward chain letters, hoaxes, or solicitations. c. Do not send or store offensive, harassing, discriminatory, or unlawful content. d. Be mindful that all communications on company systems are `nCirque Corporation`n property and may be monitored.`n5.4. Internet Usage:`n a. Internet access is provided for business-related research and communication. b. Limited personal Browse is permitted if it does not impact productivity or bandwidth, or violate this policy. c. Do not visit websites containing illegal, unethical, or inappropriate content (e.g., pornography, hate speech, gambling, illegal downloading sites).`n5.5. Software and Applications:`n a. Only authorized and licensed software may be installed on `nCirque Corporation`n's IT resources. b. Do not download or install software from untrusted sources. c. Do not use pirated or unlicensed software. d. Software installation and updates on company-issued devices must be approved by and/or performed by IT.`n5.6. Use of Personal Devices (BYOD - Bring Your Own Device):`n a. If personal devices are permitted for business use, they must comply with specific BYOD guidelines (if a BYOD Policy exists) and adhere to this Acceptable Use Policy. b. `nCirque Corporation`n reserves the right to wipe company data from personal devices used for business purposes upon separation from the company or in case of security incidents.`n5.7. Resource Consumption:`n a. Do not consume excessive network bandwidth, storage, or processing power for non-business purposes. b. Avoid unauthorized streaming, large file downloads for personal use, or excessive personal cloud storage synchronization on company networks.`n6. Prohibited Activities`nThe following activities are strictly prohibited when using `nCirque Corporation`n's IT resources:`nIllegal Activities:`n Engaging in any activity that violates local, national, or international laws or regulations.`nUnauthorized Access:`n Attempting to gain unauthorized access to any system, network, or data, whether internal or external to `nCirque Corporation`n.`nMalicious Activities:`n Distributing, installing, or promoting malware (viruses, worms, ransomware, etc.), or engaging in any activity that could harm IT resources or data.`nHarassment/Discrimination:`n Creating, transmitting, or accessing content that is harassing, discriminatory, sexually explicit, defamatory, or otherwise offensive.`nCopyright Infringement:`n Downloading, sharing, or distributing copyrighted material without proper authorization.`nCommercial Use:`n Using `nCirque Corporation`n's IT resources for personal commercial purposes (e.g., operating a personal business) without explicit written permission.`nImpersonation:`n Impersonating another person or entity.`nSecurity Bypasses:`n Attempting to bypass or disable security controls (e.g., firewalls, antivirus, content filters).`nResource Abuse:`n Deliberate or reckless destruction, modification, or disruption of `nCirque Corporation`n's IT resources or data.`nMonitoring/Interception:`n Attempting to monitor or intercept network traffic or communications of others without authorization.`n7. Monitoring and Audit`nCirque Corporation`n reserves the right to monitor, log, and audit the use of its IT resources to ensure compliance with this policy, protect its assets, and for legal and regulatory purposes. Users should have no expectation of privacy when using company IT resources.`n8. Non-Compliance`nViolation of this policy may result in disciplinary action, up to and including termination of employment, and may also lead to legal action in accordance with applicable laws.`n9. Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Responsible for implementing technical controls to enforce this policy and for monitoring compliance.`nHuman Resources:`n Responsible for enforcing disciplinary actions related to policy violations.`nAll Personnel:`n Responsible for understanding, signing (where required), and complying with this policy.`n10. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's IT resources, business operations, or legal/regulatory environment.`nIS-CIRQ-P-020-G: Remote Work Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-020-G`nStandards Name: Remote Work Policy`nCategory: Human Security Related`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee, IT Manager, HR Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish the security requirements and responsibilities for `nCirque Corporation`n personnel working remotely. This policy aims to ensure the secure use of `nCirque Corporation`n's information assets, systems, and data when accessed from outside the traditional office environment, mitigating risks associated with remote access, device security, and physical environment. It supports `nCirque Corporation`n's commitment to flexible work arrangements while maintaining a robust information security posture.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all `nCirque Corporation`n personnel (employees, contractors, temporary staff) authorized to perform work remotely, whether from a home office, co-working space, or other approved off-site locations. It covers all `nCirque Corporation`n-issued and approved personal devices used for business purposes when accessing `nCirque Corporation`n's IT resources. This policy applies globally across all `nCirque Corporation`n locations.`n3. Definitions`nRemote Work:`n Performing job duties for `nCirque Corporation`n from a location other than a designated `nCirque Corporation`n office.`nRemote Worker:`n Any individual authorized by `nCirque Corporation`n to perform work remotely.`nCompany-Issued Device:`n Hardware (e.g., laptops, tablets, smartphones) provided by `nCirque Corporation`n for business use.`nPersonal Device (BYOD):`n A privately-owned device used for `nCirque Corporation`n business purposes (if permitted by separate BYOD policy).`nVPN (Virtual Private Network):`n A secure connection over a public network (like the internet) that allows remote users to access `nCirque Corporation`n's internal network resources as if they were physically present in the office.`nRMM (Remote Monitoring and Management):`n Software used by `nCirque Corporation`n's IT department to monitor, manage, and secure remote devices and systems.`n4. Policy Requirements`n4.1. Remote Work Environment Security:`n a. Remote workers must establish a secure workspace that prevents unauthorized access to `nCirque Corporation`n information and devices. This includes implementing a "Clear Desk and Clear Screen" policy at their remote location. b. Physical security measures, such as locking doors when leaving the workspace, must be maintained. c. `nCirque Corporation`n information should not be left visible to unauthorized individuals (e.g., family members, visitors). d. Confidential discussions should be conducted in private settings to prevent eavesdropping.`n4.2. Device Security:`n a. `nCompany-Issued Devices:`n * Must be used as the primary means for conducting `nCirque Corporation`n business remotely. * Must be kept physically secure at all times to prevent theft or unauthorized access. * Must remain connected to `nCirque Corporation`n's `nRMM tool`n for monitoring, patch management, security updates, and troubleshooting. * Must use full disk encryption where technically feasible. * Must comply with IS-CIRQ-P-019-G: Acceptable Use Policy. b. `nPersonal Devices (if permitted):`n * Must adhere to a separate Bring Your Own Device (BYOD) policy (if applicable). * Must have up-to-date operating systems, antivirus software, and firewall protection. * Must be protected by strong passwords/PINs and biometric authentication where available. * `nCirque Corporation`n's IT reserves the right to enforce security configurations (e.g., mobile device management) or restrict access if security standards are not met. * Users acknowledge that `nCirque Corporation`n may remotely wipe company data from personal devices upon termination or in case of security incidents.`n4.3. Network and Connectivity Security:`n a. Remote workers `nmust use `nCirque Corporation`n's provided VPN`n to connect to all internal network services and resources (e.g., file shares, internal applications, development environments). This ensures encrypted communication and routes traffic through `nCirque Corporation`n's security infrastructure. b. Public Wi-Fi networks should be avoided for accessing sensitive `nCirque Corporation`n information. If unavoidable, the VPN `nmust`n be active at all times. c. Personal home networks used for remote work must be secured with strong, unique Wi-Fi passwords and updated router firmware.`n4.4. Data Handling and Storage:`n a. Sensitive and confidential `nCirque Corporation`n data must `nnot`n be stored directly on local hard drives of remote devices unless explicitly permitted and protected by encryption. All data should primarily reside on company network drives or approved cloud storage accessible via VPN. b. Removable media (e.g., USB drives) containing `nCirque Corporation`n data should be used sparingly and only if approved and encrypted, as per IS-CIRQ-P-010-G: Information Transfer Policy. c. Secure disposal of `nCirque Corporation`n documents and media must be followed, even in remote locations.`n4.5. Software and Applications:`n a. Only `nCirque Corporation`n-approved and licensed software should be installed and used for business purposes on company-issued devices. b. Personal software or unauthorized applications should not be installed on company-issued devices. c. All software must be kept up to date with patches and security fixes.`n4.6. Monitoring and Auditing:`n a. Remote workers acknowledge that their use of `nCirque Corporation`n-issued devices, company network access via VPN, and activities related to `nCirque Corporation`n business are subject to monitoring and auditing by `nCirque Corporation`n. This includes monitoring by the `nRMM tool`n on their computers. b. This monitoring is conducted to ensure compliance with this policy, maintain system health, troubleshoot issues, detect security incidents, and protect `nCirque Corporation`n's assets.`n4.7. Incident Reporting:`n a. Any suspected security incidents (e.g., loss or theft of a device, unauthorized access attempts, malware infection, suspicious activity) encountered while working remotely must be reported immediately as per IS-CIRQ-P-014-G: Information Security Incident Management Policy and IS-CIRQ-PR-020-G: Incident Response Procedure (Global Core).`n5. Responsibilities`nRemote Workers:`n Adhere to all aspects of this policy, report security incidents promptly, and ensure the security of their remote workspace and devices.`nIT Manager/IT Department:`n Provides secure remote access solutions (VPN), deploys and manages the `nRMM tool`n, provides company-issued devices, and ensures technical support for remote workers.`nHuman Resources:`n Manages the administrative aspects of remote work arrangements and communicates policy requirements to personnel.`n6. Non-Compliance`nFailure to comply with this policy may result in disciplinary action, up to and including termination of employment, and may also lead to legal action, as outlined in `nCirque Corporation`n's disciplinary procedures.`n7.
