Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's remote work practices, IT infrastructure, or the security threat landscape.`nIS-CIRQ-P-021-G: Monitoring, Measurement, Analysis, and Evaluation Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-021-G`nStandards Name: Monitoring, Measurement, Analysis, and Evaluation Policy`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to define `nCirque Corporation`n's framework for monitoring, measuring, analyzing, and evaluating the performance and effectiveness of its Information Security Management System (ISMS). This policy ensures that the ISMS is continually assessed against its objectives, controls are performing as intended, and opportunities for improvement are identified to maintain and enhance information security posture. This policy aligns with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause 9.1 (Monitoring, measurement, analysis and evaluation).`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all aspects of `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS, covering all processes, controls, and objectives defined within the ISMS scope, across all global operations (US, Taipei, China). It applies to all personnel involved in the monitoring, measurement, analysis, and evaluation activities.`n3. Definitions`nMonitoring:`n Observing the status of the ISMS, a process, or a control to identify changes or deviations.`nMeasurement:`n Quantifying the performance or effectiveness of a process or control.`nAnalysis:`n Examination of data and information to identify trends, patterns, and root causes.`nEvaluation:`n Determining the significance, effectiveness, and suitability of the ISMS based on the results of monitoring, measurement, and analysis.`nISMS Objectives:`n Specific, measurable, achievable, relevant, and time-bound goals for information security.`nKey Performance Indicators (KPIs):`n Measurable values that demonstrate how effectively an organization is achieving key business objectives.`nKey Risk Indicators (KRIs):`n Metrics used to provide an early signal of increasing risk exposure.`n4. Policy Requirements`n4.1. What to Monitor, Measure, Analyze, and Evaluate:`n a. The performance of the ISMS as a whole. b. The effectiveness of information security controls. c. The achievement of information security objectives. d. Processes and procedures related to information security. e. Risk levels and the effectiveness of risk treatment plans. f. Incidents and their resolution. g. Compliance with legal, regulatory, and contractual requirements.`n4.2. How to Monitor, Measure, Analyze, and Evaluate:`n a. `nMonitoring &amp; Measurement:`n * Establish and track relevant Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) and Key Risk Indicators (KRIs) for information security. Examples include: * Number of security incidents * Mean Time To Detect (MTTD) and Mean Time To Respond (MTTR) for incidents. * Number of successful phishing simulations. * Vulnerability scan results (number of critical/high vulnerabilities). * Compliance rates (e.g., patch management, password complexity adherence). * Backup success rates. * Utilize automated tools (e.g., SIEM, RMM, vulnerability scanners, log management systems) for continuous monitoring and data collection. * Conduct regular reviews of logs, alerts, and system reports. * Perform internal audits and independent reviews of specific controls or processes. b. `nAnalysis &amp; Evaluation:`n * Analyze collected data to identify trends, patterns, root causes of incidents or control failures, and areas of non-compliance. * Compare actual performance against defined ISMS objectives, KPIs, and KRIs. * Evaluate the effectiveness of implemented controls in reducing identified risks. * Assess the overall suitability, adequacy, and effectiveness of the ISMS.`n4.3. When to Monitor, Measure, Analyze, and Evaluate:`n a. Monitoring and measurement activities shall be conducted on an ongoing basis for critical controls and systems. b. Formal analysis and evaluation of collected data shall occur at planned intervals, typically monthly or quarterly by relevant teams, and consolidated for management review. c. Significant changes to the ISMS, IT infrastructure, or the threat landscape may trigger immediate monitoring and evaluation activities.`n4.4. Who Monitors, Measures, Analyzes, and Evaluates:`n a. `nIT Department:`n Primarily responsible for technical monitoring and measurement, including system logs, network traffic, security tool outputs, and incident data. b. `nSecurity Team:`n Responsible for analyzing security-related data, conducting vulnerability assessments, and evaluating the effectiveness of security controls. c. `nInternal Audit Function:`n Conducts independent assessments of ISMS processes and controls. d. `nManagement Review Committee:`n Responsible for the overall evaluation of the ISMS performance and effectiveness during management review meetings.`n4.5. Documentation and Reporting:`n a. The results of monitoring, measurement, analysis, and evaluation activities shall be documented. b. Reports on ISMS performance shall be generated periodically and presented to relevant stakeholders, including the Management Review Committee. c. Findings, conclusions, and recommendations for improvement shall be clearly documented.`n5. Improvement`nThe output of monitoring, measurement, analysis, and evaluation activities shall feed directly into `nCirque Corporation`n's continual improvement process, leading to corrective actions, updates to policies/procedures, and enhancement of information security controls. (Refer to IS-CIRQ-P-023-G: Nonconformity and Corrective Action Policy and IS-CIRQ-P-024-G: Continual Improvement Policy).`n6. Responsibilities`nExecutive Committee:`n Ensures adequate resources are allocated for monitoring, measurement, analysis, and evaluation activities and reviews overall ISMS performance.`nIT Manager:`n Oversees the implementation and operation of technical monitoring and measurement tools and processes.`nInformation Security Officer/Team:`n Directs and performs analysis and evaluation of security metrics, ensures compliance with this policy.`nProcess Owners:`n Responsible for monitoring and measuring the performance of controls within their respective areas.`n7. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-022-G: ISMS Performance Monitoring Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-023-G: Internal Audit Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-022-G: Management Review Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-023-G: Nonconformity and Corrective Action Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-024-G: Continual Improvement Policy`n8. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS, organizational structure, or external requirements.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-022-G: ISMS Performance Monitoring Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-022-G`nStandards Name: ISMS Performance Monitoring Procedure`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to outline the specific steps and responsibilities for monitoring and measuring the performance of `nCirque Corporation`n's Information Security Management System (ISMS). This ensures that information security controls are operating effectively, ISMS objectives are being met, and data is collected for analysis and evaluation as required by IS-CIRQ-P-021-G: Monitoring, Measurement, Analysis, and Evaluation Policy.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all personnel responsible for monitoring, collecting, and reporting data related to the performance of the ISMS, information security controls, and processes across all `nCirque Corporation`n global operations (US, Taipei, China).`n3. Roles and Responsibilities`nIT Manager:`n Overall owner of this procedure; ensures resources are available for monitoring tools and activities.`nInformation Security Officer (ISO)/Security Team:`n Defines specific metrics, reviews monitoring data, performs analysis, and reports on ISMS performance.`nIT Department Personnel:`n Implements and maintains monitoring tools, configures logging, reviews alerts, and collects technical performance data.`nProcess Owners:`n Responsible for monitoring the effectiveness of information security controls within their respective business processes.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Identify and Define Metrics (KPIs/KRIs)`n a. `nFrequency:`n Annually, or upon significant changes to the ISMS or risk landscape. b. `nResponsible:`n Information Security Officer (ISO)/Security Team in conjunction with IT Manager and relevant process owners. c. `nSteps:`n 1. Review `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS objectives, risk treatment plans, and control implementation status. 2. Identify specific Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) and Key Risk Indicators (KRIs) that effectively demonstrate the performance of the ISMS and its controls. Examples include: * Number of detected security incidents per month/quarter. * Average time to resolve critical vulnerabilities. * Percentage of employees completing security awareness training annually. * Backup success rates. * Patching compliance rates for critical systems. * Number of attempted unauthorized access events. * Effectiveness of phishing simulations (e.g., click-through rate). 3. For each KPI/KRI, define: * The specific metric to be measured. * The target performance level (e.g., "99% backup success rate"). * The measurement frequency (e.g., daily, weekly, monthly, quarterly). * The data source (e.g., SIEM, RMM, vulnerability scanner, HR system). * The owner responsible for collection. 4. Document the defined metrics in a central ISMS performance dashboard or dedicated register.`n4.2. Implement Monitoring Tools and Processes`n a. `nFrequency:`n Ongoing, as part of IT operations and security management. b. `nResponsible:`n IT Department Personnel, ISO/Security Team. c. `nSteps:`n 1. Deploy and configure monitoring tools (e.g., SIEM, RMM, network monitoring, vulnerability scanners, log management systems) to collect relevant data points for the defined KPIs/KRIs. 2. Ensure that systems and applications are configured to generate appropriate logs and alerts as per IS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure. 3. Establish automated data collection mechanisms where possible. 4. Provide necessary access and training to personnel involved in operating monitoring tools.`n4.3. Collect and Review Monitoring Data`n a. `nFrequency:`n According to the defined measurement frequency for each metric (e.g., daily, weekly, monthly). b. `nResponsible:`n IT Department Personnel, ISO/Security Team, Process Owners. c. `nSteps:`n 1. Collect raw data from various sources (e.g., logs, system reports, audit findings, incident records, HR records). 2. Perform initial review of the collected data for completeness, accuracy, and `nany immediate anomalies or critical alerts. 3. Escalate critical alerts or indicators of potential security incidents immediately as per IS-CIRQ-PR-020-G: Incident Response Procedure (Global Core).`n4.4. Analyze and Report ISMS Performance`n a. `nFrequency:`n Monthly/Quarterly for consolidated reporting, or as needed for specific metrics. b. `nResponsible:`n Information Security Officer (ISO)/Security Team. c. `nSteps:`n 1. Aggregate and analyze the collected monitoring data against the defined KPIs/KRIs and their target performance levels. 2. Identify trends, deviations from baselines, and areas where ISMS performance is below target. 3. Perform root cause analysis for significant deviations or security incidents. 4. Prepare an ISMS Performance Report that summarizes: * Overall ISMS performance against objectives. * Status of key security controls. * Trends in incidents, vulnerabilities, and risks. * Compliance status. * Areas requiring improvement. 5. Present the ISMS Performance Report to the Management Review Committee as part of the management review process.`n4.5. Evaluate Effectiveness and Recommend Improvements`n a. `nFrequency:`n Quarterly for detailed evaluation, and annually as part of management review. b. `nResponsible:`n Information Security Officer (ISO)/Security Team, Management Review Committee. c. `nSteps:`n 1. Based on the analysis, evaluate the overall effectiveness of the ISMS in achieving its objectives and managing information security risks. 2. Identify opportunities for improvement, including: * Adjustments to existing controls. * Implementation of new controls. * Revisions to policies and procedures. * Updates to risk assessments or treatment plans. * Changes in resource allocation. 3. Document nonconformities and initiate corrective actions as per IS-CIRQ-PR-025-G: Corrective Action Procedure.`n5. Documentation`nISMS Performance Dashboard/Register (or similar document detailing KPIs/KRIs, targets, and owners).`nRaw monitoring data and logs.`nISMS Performance Reports.`nRecords of analysis and evaluation findings.`nCorrective Action Requests (CARs) generated.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-021-G: Monitoring, Measurement, Analysis, and Evaluation Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-015-G: Logging and Monitoring Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-020-G: Incident Response Procedure (Global Core)`nIS-CIRQ-PR-023-G: Internal Audit Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-022-G: Management Review Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-024-G: Management Review Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-023-G: Nonconformity and Corrective Action Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-025-G: Corrective Action Procedure`n7. Review`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS, monitoring tools, or relevant standards.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-023-G: Internal Audit Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-023-G`nStandards Name: Internal Audit Procedure`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to define the process for conducting planned and systematic internal audits of `nCirque Corporation`n's Information Security Management System (ISMS). These audits ensure that the ISMS:`nConforms to the requirements of ISO/IEC 27001:2022.`nConforms to `nCirque Corporation`n's own established requirements for its ISMS.`nIs effectively implemented and maintained.`nIs performing as intended.`nThis procedure ensures that internal audits are conducted objectively and independently to provide valuable input for management review and continual improvement of the ISMS.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all internal audits conducted on `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS processes, controls, and areas within the defined ISMS scope, across all global operations (US, Taipei, China).`n3. Roles and Responsibilities`nExecutive Committee:`n Approves the annual internal audit program and ensures the independence and competence of auditors.`nISMS Owner (e.g., Information Security Officer/IT Manager):`n Oversees the internal audit program, ensures audits are conducted as planned, and facilitates the resolution of nonconformities.`nInternal Auditor(s):`n Conducts audits objectively and impartially, prepares audit reports, and verifies the effectiveness of corrective actions. Auditors must be competent and independent of the area being audited.`nAuditee/Process Owner:`n Provides necessary information and access to auditors, and is responsible for implementing agreed-upon corrective actions for identified nonconformities.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Audit Program Planning`n a. `nFrequency:`n Annually. b. `nResponsible:`n ISMS Owner. c. `nSteps:`n 1. Develop an annual internal audit program based on the status and importance of the processes concerned, changes affecting the organization, and the results of previous audits. 2. The program shall define the scope, frequency, methods, responsibilities, and reporting requirements for each audit. 3. Ensure that the entire scope of the ISMS is covered within a defined audit cycle (e.g., every 1-3 years). 4. Obtain Executive Committee approval for the annual audit program.`n4.2. Individual Audit Planning`n a. `nFrequency:`n Prior to each scheduled audit. b. `nResponsible:`n Internal Auditor(s). c. `nSteps:`n 1. Define the specific objectives and scope of the individual audit (e.g., audit of Access Control Policy implementation). 2. Identify the relevant audit criteria (e.g., ISO 27001 clauses, `nCirque Corporation`n policies and procedures, legal/contractual requirements). 3. Select competent and independent auditors for the specific audit. Independence means the auditor should not audit their own work. 4. Develop an audit plan including: * Audit team members. * Audit schedule (dates, times). * Areas/departments to be audited. * Key documents to review. * Personnel to interview. 5. Communicate the audit plan to the auditee/process owner in advance.`n4.3. Conducting the Audit`n a. `nFrequency:`n As per audit schedule. b. `nResponsible:`n Internal Auditor(s). c. `nSteps:`n 1. `nOpening Meeting:`n Conduct an opening meeting with the auditee to confirm the audit plan, explain the audit process, and address any questions. 2. `nInformation Gathering:`n Collect objective evidence by: * Reviewing documented information (policies, procedures, records, logs, IS-CIRQ-PR-006-G: Document Control Procedure). * Interviewing personnel. * Observing processes and activities. * Examining technical configurations and tools. 3. `nEvidence Evaluation:`n Evaluate collected evidence `nagainst the audit criteria. 4. `nIdentify Findings:`n Document all findings, including: * `nConformities:`n Areas where the ISMS effectively meets requirements. * `nNonconformities:`n Deviations from ISO 27001 requirements or `nCirque Corporation`n's own ISMS requirements. (Identify major/minor as per audit judgment). * `nObservations/Opportunities for Improvement (OFIs):`n Areas that are not nonconformities but could be improved. 5. `nClosing Meeting:`n Conduct a closing meeting with the auditee to present the audit findings, ensure clarity, and agree on the next steps.`n4.4. Audit Reporting`n a. `nFrequency:`n Within a specified timeframe (e.g., 5-10 business days) after the audit. b. `nResponsible:`n Internal Auditor(s). c. `nSteps:`n 1. Prepare a formal Internal Audit Report using the IS-CIRQ-F-006-G: Internal Audit Report Template. 2. The report shall include: * Audit objectives and scope. * Audit criteria. * Dates and locations of the audit. * Audit team members and auditees. * Summary of findings. * Details of identified nonconformities, observations, and opportunities for improvement. * Evidence supporting each finding. 3. Submit the draft report to the auditee for factual accuracy review. 4. Issue the final audit report to the auditee, ISMS Owner, and relevant management.`n4.5. Follow-up and Corrective Actions`n a. `nFrequency:`n Ongoing until nonconformities are closed. b. `nResponsible:`n Auditee/Process Owner (for implementing actions), ISMS Owner (for oversight), Internal Auditor(s) (for verification). c. `nSteps:`n 1. For each identified nonconformity, the auditee/process owner shall initiate a corrective action request (CAR) as per IS-CIRQ-PR-025-G: Corrective Action Procedure. 2. The auditee is responsible for determining the root cause of the nonconformity, developing a corrective action plan (including timelines), and implementing the actions. 3. The Internal Auditor(s) (or a designated follow-up auditor) shall verify the implementation and effectiveness of the corrective actions within the agreed-upon timeframe. 4. Once verified as effective, the nonconformity is formally closed. 5. Maintain records of all corrective actions taken.`n5. Documentation`nAnnual Internal Audit Program.`nIndividual Audit Plans.`nAudit Checklists/Work Papers.`nEvidence collected during the audit.`nIS-CIRQ-F-006-G: Internal Audit Report Template (completed reports).`nCorrective Action Requests (CARs) and their closure records.`nRecords of auditor competence and training.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-021-G: Monitoring, Measurement, Analysis, and Evaluation Policy`nIS-CIRQ-F-006-G: Internal Audit Report Template`nIS-CIRQ-P-023-G: Nonconformity and Corrective Action Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-025-G: Corrective Action Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-008-G: Corrective Action Request (CAR) Form`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-006-G: Document Control Procedure`nISO/IEC 27001:2022 Standard (external reference)`nISO 19011: Guidelines for auditing management systems (external reference)`n7. Review`nThis procedure will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS, audit requirements, or regulatory landscape.`nIS-CIRQ-F-006-G: Internal Audit Report Template`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-F-006-G`nStandards Name: Internal Audit Report Template`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Form`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n ISMS Owner / IT Manager`nINTERNAL AUDIT REPORT`nDocument ID:`nIS-CIRQ-F-006-G`nVersion:`n1.0`nAudit ID:`n[e.g., IA-YYYYMMDD-001]`nAudit Date(s):`n[DD-MM-YYYY to DD-MM-YYYY]`nAudit Type:`n[e.g., Initial / Follow-up / Surveillance]`nReport Date:`n[DD-MM-YYYY]`n1. AUDIT FINDINGS`n4.1. Conformities / Areas of Strength:`n[List specific areas/controls that were found to be fully compliant and effectively implemented, providing brief evidence, e.g., "User account provisioning process (IS-CIRQ-PR-008-G) is well-documented and consistently followed, with documented approvals for new access."]`n4.2. Nonconformities:`nNonconformity ID:`n [e.g., NC-001]`nCategory (Major/Minor):`n [Major (significant deviation, widespread impact, or systemic failure) / Minor (isolated deviation, limited impact)]`nArea/Process:`n [Specific area or process where nonconformity was found]`nAudit Criterion Violated:`n [e.g., ISO 27001:2022 A.9.2.1, IS-CIRQ-P-008-G Section 5.3]`nFinding Description:`n [Clear, factual description of the nonconformity, what was observed, and how it deviates from the criteria. Avoid subjective language. e.g., "During review of terminated employee access logs, 3 out of 15 samples showed network access remained active for more than 48 hours after termination, contrary to IS-CIRQ-P-008-G Section 5.3 which states access must be revoked within 24 hours."]`nEvidence:`n [Specific evidence sighted, e.g., "Termination checklist records for employee X, Y, Z; network access logs for dates D1, D2, D3."]`nReference to Corrective Action Request (CAR) ID:`n [To be filled once CAR is raised, e.g., CAR-005]`nRepeat this format for each Nonconformity.`n4.3. Observations / Opportunities for Improvement (OFIs):`nOFI ID:`n [e.g., OFI-001]`nArea/Process:`n [Specific area or process]`nObservation/Recommendation:`n [Description of an area that is not a nonconformity but could be improved for better security or efficiency. e.g., "Consider implementing automated access review tools to streamline the quarterly user access review process, which is currently done manually and is time-consuming."]`nRepeat this format for each Observation/OFI.`n5. DOCUMENT HISTORY`nVersion`nDate`nChanges Made`n1.0`n[DD-MM-YYYY]`nInitial Release`nIS-CIRQ-P-022-G: Management Review Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-022-G`nStandards Name: Management Review Policy`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish `nCirque Corporation`n's commitment and framework for conducting regular management reviews of its Information Security Management System (ISMS). These reviews are essential to ensure the continuing suitability, adequacy, and effectiveness of the ISMS in addressing information security risks and opportunities. This policy aligns with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause 9.3 (Management review) and demonstrates top management's commitment to information security and continual improvement.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all aspects of `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS, covering all processes, controls, and objectives within the defined ISMS scope across all global operations (US, Taipei, China). It applies to all members of top management and other relevant personnel involved in the management review process.`n3. Definitions`nManagement Review:`n A formal meeting conducted by top management to assess the performance, suitability, adequacy, and effectiveness of the ISMS.`nTop Management:`n The person or group of people who directs and controls `nCirque Corporation`n at the highest level. This typically includes the Executive Committee.`nISMS Suitability:`n The ability of the ISMS to achieve its stated objectives and be appropriate for `nCirque Corporation`n's context.`nISMS Adequacy:`n The sufficiency of resources and controls within the ISMS to meet requirements.`nISMS Effectiveness:`n The extent to which planned activities are realized and planned results are achieved by the ISMS.`n4. Policy Requirements`n4.1. General Requirements:`n a. Top management shall review `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS at planned intervals to ensure its continuing suitability, adequacy, and effectiveness. b. The reviews shall include assessing opportunities for improvement and the need for changes to the ISMS, including the information security policy and information security objectives.`n4.2. Frequency of Management Reviews:`n a. Management reviews shall be conducted at least `nannually`n. b. Ad-hoc or extraordinary reviews may be called by top management when significant changes occur, such as: * Major security incidents or breaches. * Significant changes in `nCirque Corporation`n's business operations, scope, or technology. * Major changes in legal, regulatory, or contractual requirements. * Significant changes in identified risks or opportunities.`n4.3. Management Review Inputs:`n a. The management review shall consider the following inputs: 1. Status of actions from previous management reviews. 2. Changes in external and internal issues that are relevant to the ISMS. 3. Information security performance, including trends in: * Nonconformities and corrective actions. * Monitoring and measurement results. * Audit results (internal and external). * The results of risk assessments and the status of risk treatment plans. 4. Opportunities for continual improvement. 5. Feedback from interested parties (e.g., customers, regulators, suppliers). 6. The results of the evaluation of the effectiveness of the ISMS. 7. Adequacy of resources for the ISMS.`n4.4. Management Review Outputs:`n a. The outputs of the management review shall include decisions and actions related to: 1. The continuing suitability, adequacy, and effectiveness of the ISMS. 2. Opportunities for continual improvement. 3. Any need for changes to the ISMS. 4. Resource needs for the ISMS. 5. Improvements in processes and controls. 6. Updates to information security objectives. b. Decisions made during the management review must be documented and communicated to relevant personnel.`n4.5. Participants:`n a. The management review shall be chaired by a member of top management. b. Attendees shall include: * Members of the Executive Committee. * Information Security Officer / IT Manager. * Relevant department heads or process owners as needed, especially those whose areas are subject to specific review or have findings to address. * Legal counsel, if legal or regulatory compliance issues are on the agenda.`n4.6. Documentation of Management Reviews:`n a. Records of management reviews, including the agenda, presentations, discussions, decisions, and action items, shall be maintained using the IS-CIRQ-F-007-G: Management Review Meeting Minutes Template. b. These records shall demonstrate compliance with this policy and ISO/IEC 27001 requirements.`n5. Responsibilities`nExecutive Committee:`n Approves this policy, participates in and chairs management reviews, and ensures that necessary resources and actions are provided to maintain and improve the ISMS.`nISMS Owner (e.g., Information Security Officer/IT Manager):`n Coordinates the preparation for management reviews, compiles and presents relevant information, documents the meeting, and tracks action items.`nAll Relevant Personnel:`n Provide necessary input for the review and implement agreed-upon actions.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-021-G: Monitoring, Measurement, Analysis, and Evaluation Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-022-G: ISMS Performance Monitoring Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-023-G: Internal Audit Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-006-G: Internal Audit Report Template`nIS-CIRQ-PR-024-G: Management Review Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-007-G: Management Review Meeting Minutes Template`nIS-CIRQ-P-023-G: Nonconformity and Corrective Action Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-024-G: Continual Improvement Policy`n7. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS, organizational structure, or external requirements.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-024-G: Management Review Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-024-G`nStandards Name: Management Review Procedure`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to outline the specific steps and responsibilities for conducting formal management reviews of `nCirque Corporation`n's Information Security Management System (ISMS). This ensures that the reviews are performed systematically, cover all required inputs, and result in documented decisions and actions that lead to the continuing suitability, adequacy, and effectiveness of the ISMS, as required by IS-CIRQ-P-022-G: Management Review Policy.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all management reviews of `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS, involving top management and relevant stakeholders across all global operations (US, Taipei, China).`n3. Roles and Responsibilities`nExecutive Committee:`n Approves the management review schedule, participates in the reviews, makes decisions, and ensures the allocation of necessary resources.`nISMS Owner (e.g., Information Security Officer/IT Manager):`n Coordinates the management review process, prepares the agenda, compiles necessary inputs, facilitates the meeting, records minutes, and tracks action items.`nPresenters (e.g., IT Manager, HR Manager, Legal Counsel, Department Heads):`n Prepare and present relevant information and data as inputs to the management review.`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Planning and Scheduling Management Reviews`n a. `nFrequency:`n At least annually. b. `nResponsible:`n ISMS Owner in coordination with the Executive Committee. c. `nSteps:`n 1. Determine the annual schedule for management reviews, ensuring all ISMS elements are covered over the planned cycle. 2. Identify and schedule participants, ensuring representation from top management and relevant departments. 3. Communicate the schedule and purpose of the review meetings to all required attendees well in advance. 4. Determine if any ad-hoc reviews are required due to significant changes or incidents, as per IS-CIRQ-P-022-G: Management Review Policy.`n4.2. Preparation for Management Reviews`n a. `nFrequency:`n Prior to each scheduled review. b. `nResponsible:`n ISMS Owner and Presenters. c. `nSteps:`n 1. `nISMS Owner:`n * Develop a detailed agenda for the management review, ensuring all required inputs from IS-CIRQ-P-022-G: Management Review Policy are covered. * Collect and consolidate all necessary information and data for the review inputs. This includes: * Status of actions from previous management reviews. * Changes in external and internal issues (e.g., new regulations, organizational changes). * Information security performance reports (nonconformities, monitoring results, audit results, risk assessments, status of risk treatment plans). * Opportunities for continual improvement. * Feedback from interested parties. * Evaluation of ISMS effectiveness. * Adequacy of resources. * Distribute the agenda and pre-reading materials to attendees at least [e.g., 5 business days] prior to the meeting. 2. `nPresenters:`n * Prepare presentations and reports for their respective input areas, highlighting key findings, trends, and recommendations.`n4.3. Conducting the Management Review Meeting`n a. `nFrequency:`n As per schedule. b. `nResponsible:`n Executive Committee (Chair), ISMS Owner (Facilitator/Minute Taker), all Attendees. c. `nSteps:`n 1. `nOpening:`n The chair opens the meeting, confirms the agenda, and states the objectives of the review. 2. `nReview Previous Actions:`n Review the status of actions identified from the previous management review meeting. 3. `nInput Presentation and Discussion:`n Each presenter provides updates on their assigned inputs. Discussions should focus on: * Analysis of performance against objectives and targets. * Identification of trends and root causes of deviations. * Evaluation of control effectiveness. * Discussion of risks and opportunities. * Resource allocation and needs. * Consideration of feedback from interested parties. 4. `nDecision Making:`n Top management shall make decisions regarding: * The continuing suitability, adequacy, and effectiveness of the ISMS. * `nOpportunities for continual improvement. * Any need for changes to the ISMS, including the information security policy and objectives. * Resource needs for the ISMS. 5. `nAction Item Assignment:`n Assign clear ownership, target dates, and required resources for all identified action items. 6. `nClosing:`n The chair summarizes key decisions and action items and formally closes the meeting.`n4.4. Documenting Management Review Minutes`n a. `nFrequency:`n Immediately following the meeting (e.g., within 3-5 business days). b. `nResponsible:`n ISMS Owner. c. `nSteps:`n 1. Prepare comprehensive meeting minutes using the IS-CIRQ-F-007-G: Management Review Meeting Minutes Template. 2. The minutes shall accurately reflect the discussions, decisions made, conclusions reached, and all assigned action items with owners and deadlines. 3. Distribute draft minutes to attendees for review and comments. 4. Finalize the minutes based on feedback and obtain approval from the chair. 5. Store the final, approved minutes as a formal record of the management review.`n4.5. Follow-up on Action Items`n a. `nFrequency:`n Ongoing, until all actions are completed. b. `nResponsible:`n Action Item Owners (for implementation), ISMS Owner (for tracking). c. `nSteps:`n 1. Action item owners are responsible for implementing their assigned actions by the agreed-upon deadlines. 2. The ISMS Owner shall regularly track the progress of all action items, escalating delays or issues to the Executive Committee as necessary. 3. The status of these actions will be a required input for the subsequent management review.`n5. Documentation`nManagement Review Schedule.`nMeeting Agendas.`nPre-reading materials/presentations (e.g., ISMS performance reports, audit summaries).`nIS-CIRQ-F-007-G: Management Review Meeting Minutes Template (completed minutes).`nAction Item Tracker (or similar mechanism for tracking decisions and actions).`n6. Status of risk treatment plans reviewed."]`nDiscussion: [Review of identified risks, effectiveness of risk treatments, any new or emerging risks.]`nDecision(s): [e.g., "Approve proposed risk treatment plan for supply chain software risk; establish new project for implementation."]`n3.5. Results of Internal &amp; External Audits`nPresentation by: [Presenter's Name]`nData Reviewed: [e.g., "Internal Audit Report IA-20250615-001 on Access Control; no external audit performed since last review."]`nDiscussion: [Summary of audit findings, status of open nonconformities, overall audit program effectiveness.]`nDecision(s): [e.g., "Acknowledge findings from IA-20250615-001. Ensure all identified CARs are opened and tracked."]`n3.6. Is it suitable for current needs?]`nDecision(s): [e.g., "ISMS remains largely suitable and adequate, but requires targeted improvements in threat intelligence integration and vendor security assessments."]`n3.8. Are they sufficient?]`nDecision(s): [e.g., "Approve budget request for new vulnerability management software and additional FTE for security operations."]`n3.9. DOCUMENT HISTORY`nVersion`nDate`nChanges Made`n1.0`n[DD-MM-YYYY]`nInitial Release`nIS-CIRQ-P-023-G: Nonconformity and Corrective Action Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-023-G`nStandards Name: Nonconformity and Corrective Action Policy`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish `nCirque Corporation`n's commitment and framework for identifying, addressing, and preventing nonconformities within its Information Security Management System (ISMS). This policy ensures that identified deviations from requirements are promptly corrected, their root causes are eliminated, and measures are taken to prevent recurrence, thereby driving continual improvement of the ISMS. This policy aligns with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause 10.1 (Continual improvement - Nonconformity and corrective action).`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all nonconformities related to `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS, whether identified through internal audits, management reviews, incident management, performance monitoring, external audits, or other means. It covers all processes, controls, and personnel within the defined ISMS scope across all global operations (US, Taipei, China).`n3. Definitions`nNonconformity (NC):`n A non-fulfillment of a requirement. In the context of the ISMS, this could be a deviation from ISO 27001 requirements, `nCirque Corporation`n's own ISMS requirements (e.g., policies, procedures), or legal, regulatory, or contractual obligations.`nCorrective Action (CA):`n Action to eliminate the cause of a detected nonconformity and to prevent recurrence.`nRoot Cause Analysis (RCA):`n A systematic process for identifying the underlying causes of a nonconformity, rather than just addressing its symptoms.`nCorrective Action Request (CAR):`n A formal document or record used to initiate and track the process of addressing a nonconformity and implementing corrective actions.`n4. Policy Requirements`n4.1. Identification and Reporting of Nonconformities:`n a. Any personnel who identify a nonconformity, or potential nonconformity, related to the ISMS shall report it to their manager or the ISMS Owner/Security Team. b. Nonconformities can be identified through various sources, including: * Internal and external audits. * Security incident investigations. * Monitoring and measurement activities. * Management reviews. * Feedback from interested parties (e.g., customers, regulators). * Employee suggestions or observations.`n4.2. Response to Nonconformities:`n a. When a nonconformity occurs, `nCirque Corporation`n shall: 1. `nReact to the nonconformity:`n Take immediate action to control and correct it (i.e., address the symptoms). 2. `nDeal with the consequences:`n Address any immediate impacts or damages. 3. `nEvaluate the need for action to eliminate the cause(s):`n Determine if a corrective action is necessary to prevent recurrence.`n4.3. Root Cause Analysis (RCA):`n a. For all significant nonconformities (e.g., those impacting critical systems, recurring issues, or high-risk areas), a Root Cause Analysis shall be performed. b. The RCA process aims to identify the "why" behind the nonconformity, not just the "what." Techniques may include 5 Whys, Fishbone Diagram, etc.`n4.4. Corrective Action Planning and Implementation:`n a. Based on the RCA, a corrective action plan shall be developed. This plan must include: * Specific actions to address the root cause(s). * Clear responsibilities for implementation. * Realistic target dates for completion. * Resources required. b. Corrective actions shall be proportionate to the significance of the nonconformity and the risks involved. c. Actions shall be implemented in a timely manner.`n4.5. Verification of Effectiveness:`n a. Once corrective actions have been implemented, their effectiveness shall be verified. This involves confirming that the action taken has eliminated the root cause and prevented recurrence. b. Verification methods may include re-auditing, re-testing, reviewing new performance data, or observing updated processes.`n4.6. Review of Corrective Actions:`n a. The results of corrective actions shall be reviewed periodically (e.g., during management reviews) to ensure their ongoing effectiveness and to identify any systemic issues or further opportunities for improvement.`n4.7. Documentation and Record Keeping:`n a. All nonconformities and subsequent corrective actions shall be documented using the IS-CIRQ-F-008-G: Corrective Action Request (CAR) Form. b. Records shall be maintained as documented information of: * The nature of the nonconformities and any subsequent actions taken. * The results of any corrective action taken. c. These records are crucial for demonstrating compliance and for input into continual improvement.`n5. Responsibilities`nExecutive Committee:`n Provides leadership and resources for managing nonconformities and corrective actions; reviews significant nonconformities during management reviews.`nISMS Owner (e.g., Information Security Officer/IT Manager):`n Overall responsibility for the nonconformity and corrective action process; ensures proper logging, tracking, and closure of CARs; reports on the status of nonconformities to top management.`nProcess Owners/Action Item Owners:`n Responsible for investigating nonconformities within their area, conducting RCA, developing and implementing corrective action plans, and confirming effectiveness.`nInternal Auditors:`n Responsible for identifying nonconformities during audits and verifying the effectiveness of corrective actions.`nAll Personnel:`n Responsible for reporting nonconformities and supporting the implementation of corrective actions within their scope.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-021-G: Monitoring, Measurement, Analysis, and Evaluation Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-023-G: Internal Audit Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-006-G: Internal Audit Report Template`nIS-CIRQ-P-022-G: Management Review Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-025-G: Corrective Action Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-008-G: Corrective Action Request (CAR) Form`nIS-CIRQ-P-024-G: Continual Improvement Policy`n7. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS, processes for handling nonconformities, or relevant standards.`nIS-CIRQ-PR-025-G: Corrective Action Procedure`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-PR-025-G`nStandards Name: Corrective Action Procedure`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Procedure`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n ISMS Owner / IT Manager`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this procedure is to establish a systematic process for addressing nonconformities identified within `nCirque Corporation`n's Information Security Management System (ISMS). It provides detailed steps for initiating, investigating, planning, implementing, verifying, and closing corrective actions, ensuring that the root causes of nonconformities are eliminated and recurrence is prevented, as per IS-CIRQ-P-023-G: Nonconformity and Corrective Action Policy.`n2. Scope`nThis procedure applies to all identified nonconformities impacting `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS, regardless of their source (e.g., internal/external audits, security incidents, monitoring results, management reviews, stakeholder feedback). It covers all personnel involved in the corrective action process across all global operations (US, Taipei, China).`n3. Roles and Responsibilities`nISMS Owner (e.g., Information Security Officer/IT Manager):`n Oversees the overall corrective action process, assigns CARs, tracks progress, and reports on the status of nonconformities.`nOriginator:`n The individual who identifies and reports a nonconformity (e.g., Internal Auditor, security analyst, employee).`nAction Item Owner (Responsible Party):`n The individual or team responsible for investigating the nonconformity, performing root cause analysis, developing and implementing the corrective action plan.`nVerifier:`n The individual responsible for verifying the implementation and effectiveness of the corrective action (often the Originator or a designated auditor/ISMS staff member).`n4. Procedure`n4.1. Identification and Initiation of Corrective Action Request (CAR)`n a. `nFrequency:`n As nonconformities are identified. b. `nResponsible:`n Originator, ISMS Owner. c. `nSteps:`n 1. `nIdentify Nonconformity:`n A nonconformity is identified (e.g., during an internal audit, an incident investigation, or routine monitoring). 2. `nImmediate Correction (Containment):`n The immediate response to the nonconformity should be to contain its impact and correct the immediate issue (e.g., block unauthorized access, restore data). This is a temporary fix for the symptom. 3. `nInitiate CAR:`n The Originator completes the initial sections of the IS-CIRQ-F-008-G: Corrective Action Request (CAR) Form, providing a clear and factual description of the nonconformity, the relevant ISMS requirement violated, and any objective evidence. 4. `nSubmit CAR:`n The completed CAR form is submitted to the ISMS Owner. 5. `nAssign CAR ID &amp; Owner:`n The ISMS Owner logs the CAR in the CAR tracking system/register, assigns a unique CAR ID, and assigns an "Action Item Owner" (Responsible Party) based on the nature of the nonconformity and the relevant process owner. An initial target date for root cause analysis and action planning is set.`n4.2. Investigation and Root Cause Analysis (RCA)`n a. `nFrequency:`n Within the initial target date set by the ISMS Owner (e.g., 5-10 business days from CAR assignment). b. `nResponsible:`n Action Item Owner. c. `nSteps:`n 1. `nAcknowledge CAR:`n The Action Item Owner acknowledges receipt of the CAR. 2. `nGather Information:`n Collect additional relevant information, data, and evidence related to the nonconformity. 3. `nPerform RCA:`n Conduct a systematic Root Cause Analysis (e.g., using techniques like "5 Whys," Fishbone/Ishikawa diagram, FMEA) to determine the underlying reasons why the nonconformity occurred. This goes beyond the symptom to identify the systemic issues. 4. `nDocument Root Cause:`n Record the identified root cause(s) on the IS-CIRQ-F-008-G: Corrective Action Request (CAR) Form.`n4.3. Corrective Action Planning`n a. `nFrequency:`n Immediately following RCA completion. b. `nResponsible:`n Action Item Owner. c. `nSteps:`n 1. `nDevelop Action Plan:`n Based on the `nidentified root cause(s), develop a detailed corrective action plan outlining specific actions required to eliminate the cause and prevent recurrence. 2. `nDefine Deliverables &amp; Responsibilities:`n For each action, specify clear deliverables, assign responsibilities to individuals, and set realistic completion dates. 3. `nUpdate CAR Form:`n Document the corrective action plan on the IS-CIRQ-F-008-G: Corrective Action Request (CAR) Form. 4. `nObtain Approval:`n The Action Item Owner submits the proposed corrective action plan to the ISMS Owner for review and approval.`n4.4. Implementation of Corrective Actions`n a. `nFrequency:`n As per the planned schedule. b. `nResponsible:`n Action Item Owner and assigned personnel. c. `nSteps:`n 1. `nExecute Actions:`n Implement the agreed-upon corrective actions according to the plan and deadlines. 2. `nDocument Evidence:`n Maintain records and evidence of the implementation of each action (e.g., screenshots of configuration changes, training records, updated procedure documents, meeting minutes). 3. `nUpdate Status:`n Update the status of the CAR in the tracking system/register.`n4.5. Verification of Effectiveness`n a. `nFrequency:`n Upon completion of actions, within an agreed-upon timeframe (e.g., 30-60 days after implementation). b. `nResponsible:`n Verifier (e.g., Originator, ISMS Owner, Internal Auditor). c. `nSteps:`n 1. `nReview Evidence:`n The Verifier reviews the documented evidence of implementation. 2. `nAssess Effectiveness:`n The Verifier assesses whether the implemented corrective actions have effectively eliminated the root cause and prevented the recurrence of the nonconformity. This may involve: * Reviewing new performance data from monitoring (e.g., IS-CIRQ-PR-022-G). * Conducting follow-up interviews or observations. * Performing re-audits or re-tests of the affected control/process. 3. `nDocument Verification:`n Record the results of the verification on the IS-CIRQ-F-008-G: Corrective Action Request (CAR) Form.`n4.6. Closure of CAR`n a. `nFrequency:`n Upon successful verification of effectiveness. b. `nResponsible:`n ISMS Owner (with Verifier's recommendation). c. `nSteps:`n 1. If the corrective actions are deemed effective, the Verifier recommends closure of the CAR to the ISMS Owner. 2. The ISMS Owner formally closes the CAR in the tracking system/register. 3. If the corrective actions are `nnot`n effective, the CAR is reopened, and the process reverts to Section 4.3 for further action planning and implementation.`n4.7. Review for Changes and Continual Improvement`n a. `nFrequency:`n Regularly, especially during management reviews. b. `nResponsible:`n ISMS Owner, Management Review Committee. c. `nSteps:`n 1. The ISMS Owner shall periodically review the history of nonconformities and corrective actions (e.g., quarterly) to identify recurring issues or systemic weaknesses. 2. Information on nonconformities and corrective actions, their root causes, and effectiveness shall be a key input to management reviews (IS-CIRQ-PR-024-`nG). 3. This information contributes to `nCirque Corporation`n's continual improvement efforts (IS-CIRQ-P-024-G).`n5. Documentation`nIS-CIRQ-F-008-G: Corrective Action Request (CAR) Form (completed).`nCAR Tracking Register/System.`nEvidence of RCA activities.`nEvidence of corrective action implementation.`nRecords of verification of effectiveness.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-023-G: Nonconformity and Corrective Action Policy`nIS-CIRQ-F-008-G: Corrective Action Request (CAR) Form`nIS-CIRQ-PR-023-G: Internal Audit Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-021-G: Monitoring, Measurement, Analysis, and Evaluation Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-022-G: ISMS Performance Monitoring Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-022-G: Management Review Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-024-G: Management Review Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-P-024-G: Continual Improvement Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-020-G: Incident Response Procedure (Global Core)`n7. What was observed?] *Example: "During the monthly review of user access logs, it was observed that User ID 'jsmith' retained access to the Finance Sharepoint for 72 hours after their termination date of 2025-06-01."]`nRelevant ISMS Requirement Violated:`n [Reference the specific standard clause, policy, or procedure violated.] `nExample: "IS-CIRQ-P-008-G: Access Control Policy, Section 5.3 (Access Revocation) - 'Access for terminated employees must be revoked within 24 hours of termination.' Also, ISO 27001:2022 A.9.2.1."`nObjective Evidence:`n [Provide specific references to records, logs, reports, or observations that substantiate the nonconformity.] `nExample: "HR termination record for 'jsmith' (ID: HR-T-005). IMMEDIATE CORRECTION (Containment / Symptom Fix)`nAction Taken (Temporary Fix):`n [What immediate action was taken to control the nonconformity and deal with its consequences? This is typically a band-aid, not the long-term solution.] `nExample: "User ID 'jsmith' access to Finance Sharepoint manually revoked on 2025-06-04 at 10:30 AM."`nDate Completed:`n [DD-MM-YYYY]`n3. INVESTIGATION AND ROOT CAUSE ANALYSIS (RCA)`nAction Item Owner (Responsible Party):`n [Name, Department]`nRoot Cause Analysis Performed (Methodology used, e.g., 5 Whys, Fishbone):`n [Describe the method used for RCA.] `nExample: "5 Whys analysis was conducted."`nIdentified Root Cause(s):`n [What were the underlying systemic reasons for the nonconformity? Why did it happen?] *Example: "The automated HR-IT termination workflow failed to trigger the access revocation script for the Finance Sharepoint due to an incorrect group mapping. This manual review process, intended as a fallback, has a 48-hour SLA, which was exceeded due to personnel being on leave."]`n4. Reviewed updated HR-IT workflow configuration. 2. Monitored daily reconciliation report for 3 weeks (no discrepancies found). 3. Reviewed training records and interviewed Help Desk staff on updated procedure."`nResults of Verification (Effective? DOCUMENT HISTORY`nVersion`nDate`nChanges Made`n1.0`n[DD-MM-YYYY]`nInitial Release`nIS-CIRQ-P-024-G: Continual Improvement Policy`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-024-G`nStandards Name: Continual Improvement Policy`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to establish `nCirque Corporation`n's commitment to the continual improvement of its Information Security Management System (ISMS). This policy ensures that the ISMS remains effective, suitable, and adequate in managing information security risks and opportunities in a dynamic environment, constantly striving for enhanced performance. This policy aligns with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause 10.2 (Continual improvement).`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all processes, activities, and personnel involved in the operation and enhancement of `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS across all global operations (US, Taipei, China). It covers all initiatives aimed at improving the overall information security posture and the effectiveness of the ISMS.`n3. Definitions`nContinual Improvement:`n A recurring activity to enhance performance. In the context of the ISMS, it refers to ongoing efforts to improve the suitability, adequacy, and effectiveness of the information security system.`nISMS:`n Information Security Management System.`nPDCA Cycle (Plan-Do-Check-Act):`n A cyclical process for continuous improvement, used as the underlying methodology for the ISMS.`n4. Policy Requirements`n4.1. Commitment to Continual Improvement:`n a. `nCirque Corporation`n is committed to continually improving the suitability, adequacy, and effectiveness of its ISMS. b. This commitment is demonstrated through the allocation of necessary resources, management review, and the implementation of identified improvements.`n4.2. Improvement Methodology (PDCA Cycle):`n a. `nCirque Corporation`n shall utilize the Plan-Do-Check-Act (PDCA) cycle as the foundation for its continual improvement efforts: * `nPLAN:`n Establish information security objectives and processes necessary to deliver results in accordance with `nCirque Corporation`n's overall information security policy. (e.g., Risk assessment, ISMS planning). * `nDO:`n Implement the information security policy, controls, and processes. (e.g., Control implementation, operations). * `nCHECK:`n Monitor, measure, and review performance against information security policy and objectives, and report results. (e.g., Monitoring, internal audits, management reviews). * `nACT:`n Take corrective actions and implement improvements based on the results of the check phase. (e.g., Corrective actions, ISMS updates).`n4.3. Sources of Improvement:`n a. Inputs for continual improvement shall be systematically gathered from various sources, including but not limited to: * Results of IS-CIRQ-P-021-G: Monitoring, Measurement, Analysis, and Evaluation Policy and IS-CIRQ-PR-022-G: ISMS Performance Monitoring Procedure. * Outcomes of IS-CIRQ-PR-023-G: Internal Audit Procedure and IS-CIRQ-F-006-G: Internal Audit Report Template. * Decisions and actions from IS-CIRQ-P-022-G: Management Review Policy and IS-CIRQ-PR-024-G: Management Review Procedure. * Nonconformities and the results of IS-CIRQ-P-023-G: Nonconformity and Corrective Action Policy and IS-CIRQ-PR-025-G: Corrective Action Procedure (IS-CIRQ-F-008-G: Corrective Action Request (CAR) Form). * Information security incidents and their post-incident reviews. * Changes in internal and external issues (e.g., new threats, technologies, legal requirements). * Feedback from interested parties (e.g., customers, employees, regulators, suppliers). * Suggestions from employees and subject matter experts.`n4.4. Planning and Implementation of Improvements:`n a. Opportunities for improvement shall be assessed, prioritized based on risk and benefit, and documented. b. An action plan, including responsibilities, timelines, and necessary resources, shall be developed for each significant improvement initiative. c. Implemented improvements shall be monitored and evaluated to confirm their effectiveness and contribution to the ISMS.`n4.5. Documentation and Communication:`n a. Records of continual improvement activities, including identified opportunities, action plans, implementation status, and verification of effectiveness, shall be maintained. b. The outcomes of improvement initiatives shall be communicated to relevant stakeholders as appropriate.`n5. Responsibilities`nExecutive Committee:`n Demonstrates leadership and commitment to continual improvement by providing resources, reviewing improvement progress, and making strategic decisions during management reviews.`nISMS Owner (e.g., Information Security Officer/IT Manager):`n Champions continual improvement, oversees the collection and analysis of improvement inputs, facilitates the planning and tracking of improvement initiatives, and reports on progress to management.`nProcess Owners:`n Responsible for identifying and implementing improvements within their respective ISMS processes and controls.`nAll Personnel:`n Are encouraged to identify and report opportunities for improvement related to information security.`n6. Related Documents`nIS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy`nIS-CIRQ-P-021-G: Monitoring, Measurement, Analysis, and Evaluation Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-022-G: ISMS Performance Monitoring Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-PR-023-G: Internal Audit Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-006-G: Internal Audit Report Template`nIS-CIRQ-P-022-G: Management Review Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-024-G: Management Review Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-007-G: Management Review Meeting Minutes Template`nIS-CIRQ-P-023-G: Nonconformity and Corrective Action Policy`nIS-CIRQ-PR-025-G: Corrective Action Procedure`nIS-CIRQ-F-008-G: Corrective Action Request (CAR) Form`n7. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS, business operations, or the information security landscape, to ensure its ongoing relevance and effectiveness.`nIS-CIRQ-P-02`n5`n-G: `nEmployee Termination Process`nDocument: IS-CIRQ-P-024-G`nStandards Name: Continual Improvement Policy`nCategory: ISMS Support Process`nDivision: Policy`nStandard Retention: Exist and No Corrections`nStandard Type: Global`nVersion:`n 1.0 `nEffective Date:`n 2025-07-01 `nReview Date:`n 2026-07-01 `nApproved By:`n Executive Committee`n1. Purpose`nThe purpose of this policy is to define Cirque Corporation’s standardized process for the termination of employees, contractors, and temporary personnel to ensure the orderly separation of individuals from the organization while protecting company assets, information, and systems. This process supports the confidentiality, integrity, and availability of information and aligns with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clauses 6.3, 7.5, and Annex A controls related to human resource security and access management.`n2. Scope`nThis policy applies to all Cirque Corporation personnel, including full-time employees, part-time employees, contractors, consultants, and temporary workers, across all global operations (US, Taipei, China). It covers voluntary and involuntary terminations, role separations, and contract expirations.`n3. Definitions`nTermination`n: The formal end of an individual’s employment or contractual relationship with Cirque Corporation, whether voluntary or involuntary.`n• `nAccess`n `nRights`n: Logical and physical permissions granted to personnel, including system accounts, credentials, and facility access.`n• `nCompany Assets`n: Any physical or digital property owned or licensed by Cirque `nCorporation, including hardware, software, data, documentation, and intellectual property.`n• `nExit Process`n: The coordinated set of activities required to ensure secure and compliant employee separation.`n4. Policy Requirements`n4.1. General Termination Principles`na. All terminations shall be handled in a consistent, professional, and legally compliant manner.`nb. Termination activities shall be coordinated between Human Resources (HR), Information Technology (IT), the employee’s manager, and other relevant stakeholders.`nc. Termination actions shall be executed promptly to minimize operational and information security risks.`n4.2. Notification and Authorization`na. HR shall formally initiate the termination process upon approval from authorized management.`nb. IT and relevant process owners shall be notified in advance of the termination date when feasible, or immediately in cases of involuntary or high-risk terminations.`nc. The termination date and time shall be clearly communicated to all responsible parties.`n4.3. Access Revocation`na. All logical access rights (e.g., network accounts, email, VPN, cloud services, applications) shall be disabled or revoked no later than the effective termination date and time.`nb. Physical access (e.g., badges, keys, facility access) shall be revoked concurrently with logical access.`nc. Privileged or administrative access shall be revoked immediately upon termination notification.`n4.4. Asset Recovery and Information Protection`na. All company assets issued to the individual shall be identified, collected, and accounted for, including but not limited to laptops, mobile devices, ID badges, documents, and storage media.`nb. Confidential information, intellectual property, and proprietary materials shall be returned or securely retained by Cirque Corporation.`nc. Any data stored on personal or third-party devices (if permitted) shall be returned or securely removed in accordance with company policy.`n4.5. Knowledge Transfer and Exit Activities`na. When applicable, managers shall ensure that critical knowledge, documentation, and responsibilities are transitioned prior to the termination date.`nb. HR may conduct an exit interview to gather feedback and reinforce ongoing confidentiality obligations.`nc. Continuing obligations related to confidentiality, intellectual property, and non-disclosure agreements shall be reaffirmed to the departing individual.`n4.6. Documentation and Recordkeeping`na. All termination activities shall be documented and retained in accordance with Cirque Corporation’s record retention requirements.`nb. Evidence of access revocation and asset recovery shall be maintained for audit and compliance purposes.`n5. Responsibilities`nExecutive Committee: Provides oversight and ensures adequate resources and authority for effective termination controls.`n• Human Resources: Initiates and coordinates the termination process, ensures legal and policy compliance, and maintains personnel records.`n• IT / ISMS Owner: Ensures timely revocation of system access, protection of information assets, and verification of completion.`n• Managers: Coordinate operational handover, identify assigned assets, and support HR and IT during the termination process.`n• Departing Personnel: Return all company assets and comply with ongoing confidentiality and contractual obligations.`n6. Policy Review`nThis policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are significant changes to `nCirque Corporation`n's ISMS, business operations, or the information security landscape, to ensure its ongoing relevance and effectiveness.
